## §-0. THE QUEUE — read this first (added v1.24, 2026-08-10)

**Why this section exists.** The roadmap had grown to 22 sections and ~203 KB, lettered `§-1b`
through `§-1m` in the order the owner's briefs arrived rather than by meaning — and `§-1c`, the
defect register, physically sits *after* `§-1m` as a scar from the D-21 incident. **Finding "what is
next" meant reading the archive.** Everything below §-0 is now explicitly the ARCHIVE — how each
item was measured, and why. **§-0 is the only ordered list, and it is the one to keep current.**

### A. BLOCKED ON THE OWNER — no work can start, and each is one decision

| # | Decision | What it gates | Deferrable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**Q1**~~ | **ANSWERED 2026-08-10.** Owner: *"Club is not a term we are using. Player cards are universal. They can remove certain identification details but not shut off. They can set up individual blocks of people."* So: **the word is ORGANIZATION**, never club; **player cards are visible across all organizations** with member-chosen field-level redaction but no full hide; **blocks are individual person-to-person** and must therefore work across organizations — which makes **D-18 (one identity rule) the first build** of the cross-org track, since person-level blocks need a person-level identity and `contacts` is per-org | ~~sub finder + messages universal~~ → now gated only on **D-18** | — |
| **Q2** | Marketplace: **owner says scope it in a DEDICATED PLANNING SESSION** — *"we need to scope this out in more detail… No commission or fee for now since it is a passthrough and used for backlinks."* The planning questions to bring to that session are written at the end of §-1m. **PM-1 (external registration link) stays independently buildable** | PM-2…PM-3 — **PM-4 re-gated (iteration 90): §-1q makes it a host account + grants + approvals, so it unblocks after SG-3c, not after the session** | Yes — until he calls the session |
| ~~**Q3**~~ | **ANSWERED 2026-08-10:** grouping is fine. **And it grew a build item, K-13:** a multi-sort control on the pool board — registration date, alphabetical, rank (and reverse), group, division, gender, where each applies. The T2-8 sorter is the substrate; "rank" needs a decision about what rank means pre-standings (seed? level? K-1's number?) — **take the sensible default (the K-1 team number) and say so, per RALPH §B** | ~~K-13, queued below~~ K-13 shipped v0.144.0 | — |
| **Q4** | Member menu order — variations to choose from ("1 Dashboard, 2 Inbox, 3 …") | the member rail | Yes |
| **Q5** | Does a member's "favourite team" let them **see** anything extra, or is it decoration? | **K-9** team logos | Yes |
| **Q6** | "Round robin tournament" — a bracket-less event, or a round-robin **final** after pools? | **K-5** formats | Yes |
| **Q7** | Should the rescue-link route require **admin** rather than staff? And `PERMISSIONS` declares two exports admin-only while both are enforced at staff — correct the declaration or tighten the gates? | **S-2a / S-2b / S-2c** | Yes |
| **Q8** | Carried: is the next milestone **1.0.0**? · a tag vocabulary, or a decision there isn't one · a region mapping for the area signal | reporting polish | Yes |

**None of these blocks the build queue in section B.** They are listed first because they are the
only things that need him, not because they are urgent.

Deploy changed nothing: 0 of 7 events carry either column | — | — |

| ~~**11**~~ | ~~**T2-1a** schedule undo/redo (§-1j)~~ **SHIPPED v0.151.0 — the whole settled sentence in one unit: hold changes until Save, revert back and forward, confirm to save.** The editor's moves are now LOCAL (the pool board's "nothing saves until you say so", plus a history: undo/redo buttons + Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Shift+Z, disabled by stack depth); fairness is still scored by the SERVER after every held move through a new **no-write `preview` endpoint** (same loader, same `poolReport` — the editor's charter forbids a second definition of "fair", and holding would otherwise have frozen the panel at the last save); Save posts the changed positions to a new **`apply` endpoint — the one writer** — which validates the RESULT (one stranger match poisons the whole save; two matches on one slot refused wholesale on the final arrangement, not the request), parks on distinct negative courts mid-write (the old route's own idiom), and audits once. **The v0.65.0 save-every-move route is REMOVED** — an editor that holds would have left it caller-less, and two writers of one arrangement is how they disagree; its tests were REWRITTEN to their purposes (a swap never loses a match; a reposition lands where sent). Dirty state is visible and guarded: "Save N changes", ● Unsaved changes (the pool board's own token), confirm on event-switch/reload, beforeunload — hold-until-Save makes silent loss POSSIBLE where save-every-move could not lose anything, so every exit asks. **The archive row HELD; the enumeration was wrong about one consumer** (`sandbox_seed.test.mjs` also drove the removed route — rewritten as an exchange, because a full 12×5 grid has no empty slot and the save honestly refuses collisions). Mid-build, three of my own guards were caught by their own controls: a foreign-match NC green off route-absence 404 (strengthened to require the refusal's own sentence), and two needles at the wrong grain (comment vs code; escaped-regex vs plain bytes) | — | — |

Migration 0048 adds `webauthn_credentials.uv_required` (DEFAULT 0 — the deploy changed no login). **NEW enrolments must verify outright**: register-options demand it AND the flag is enforced server-side (an option is a hint; the flags check is the enforcement), credentials born with `uv_required = 1`. **EXISTING credentials ratchet**: the first login that carries the UV flag flips the bit — the live credential is Windows Hello (`device_label "Windows PC"`, counter 0), which always verifies, so the owner self-ratchets on his first sign-in after deploy — and from then on a UV-less assertion from that credential is refused with its own sentence (a verifying authenticator that suddenly stops is the shape of a cloned key). **Login options deliberately stay "preferred"** — a client-side "required" is the only thing that could lock a legacy credential out, and it adds no security the per-credential server check doesn't. §-1i's pinned "CURRENT CONTRACT" test rewritten to its surviving purpose (no lockout: a never-verified credential still signs in, and does NOT silently ratchet on a presence-only login); the register round-trip fixture now enrols the way real authenticators must (UV flag set) — both rewrites named, never deleted. The archive block HELD in every particular | — | — |

| ~~**17**~~ | ~~**K-13** multi-sort on the pool board (owner 2026-08-10)~~ **SHIPPED v0.144.0.** Four of the six keys were settled by MEASURING, not choosing: **rank = the K-1 team number** per Q3's own default, which is also **registration date** (board_no is rank by `t.id`, ids are AUTOINCREMENT — two of his words, one control, because offering both would produce byte-identical output); **group = the Level sort already shipped** in v0.125.0, whose own comment has called it grouping since the day it landed; **division sorts by the division's `rank`**, the director's ordering, never its name; **gender = `teams.gender_division`**, in the schema since migration 1 and never selected. **"Where each applies" is COMPUTED from the board, and live D1 is why:** every team in production is ("Coed","BB/A") or (NULL,NULL), so Gender would reorder nobody on any board that exists — **and neither would Level, which had been an always-visible option since v0.125.0.** An option is now offered only when sorting by it could separate two teams, which repaired the shipped Level and Seed options while adding the new ones. **Reverse inverts the COMPARISON, not the array**, so the blanks v0.125.0 deliberately put last stay last in both directions. The option list and the sort share `sortPick` — one place a key becomes a value, so what a director is offered and what they get cannot drift apart | — | — |
| ~~**18**~~ | ~~**SG-1** the drop-in sheet (§-1o)~~ **SHIPPED v0.132.0.** `sheet.html?event=N` + two public routes in registrations.js; a sheet sign-up IS a registration (team_id/waiver_id NULL), so SG-2's threshold/cancel and the staff list compose for free; free AND priced per the Q5 rider; §8 pinned by a positive-controlled payload walker. **~~SG-4~~ (SHIPPED v0.153.0 — the age band + the coverage honesty) → ~~SG-2~~ (SHIPPED v0.154.0 — `events.min_signups` + the count line) → ~~SG-6~~ (RESOLVED v0.155.0 — the calendar ALREADY EXISTED; the `?mode=month` landing shipped) → ~~SG-5~~ (SHIPPED v0.156.0 — the hub grew Overview + Announce for every type, and B16's promised second caller: `POST /api/events/:id/notify`, news to the participants; see §-1o) → SG-3 (**§-1q ANSWERED by the owner 2026-08-15 — both questions settled verbatim in §-1q's tail; SG-3a is authorised EXCEPT its one non-additive step: the 3-row role-CHECK rebuild runs only after he says go on the SQL shown in §-1q.** Then SG-3b screens, SG-3c approvals with the pending-partner shape he specified. **Creating Cathy's account stays gated on SG-3a** — under today's code a staff account gets all ~180 gates including S-2a's rescue-link)** | — | — |
| ~~**19**~~ | ~~**P-E** the day sheet (§-1n)~~ **SHIPPED v0.163.0 — a print MODE of Tournament Ops (H-3's precedent: no new page, no rail entry, no new route).** Print/Email day sheet compose schedule + pools + bracket into ONE print job, a page break per section, from THREE reads that already exist and are already called: the page's own `sheetRows()` (print/CSV/email can never disagree about a game), the pool board's `GET …/board`, admin-brackets' `GET …/brackets`. `body.print-day` around `print()`, removed on `afterprint` — the pool sheet's own print byte-untouched; empty/failed reads render honest sentences. Every innerHTML interpolation routes through a local escaper (the page's older grid predates the discipline; the new code doesn't inherit it — `day_sheet.test.mjs` enforces at the html-statement grain). **Named collateral rewritten to purpose:** bracket_rewire's one-call-site pin moved to the POST grain (its uniqueness was always the GENERATE). **The prompt's own premise measured false the right way: print_parity's rule is set-DERIVED, so the fourth surface stays green by construction — no rewrite was ever needed** | — |
| ~~**20**~~ | ~~**D-18** one identity rule (§-1c)~~ **SHIPPED v0.166.0 (iteration 97) — and this row understated the unit TWICE.** There were **five** resolvers, not two (member_portal, messages, profiles, waivers each kept a private copy, and calendar.js had a private function sharing the shared helper's NAME), and the "case-sensitive" half was **false** — `contacts.email` and `users.email` are both `COLLATE NOCASE` in D1. The rule was unified **upward**: a contact linked by `user_id` IS that user's record outright, an email match is the fallback — because those private copies were MORE correct than the shared helper on exactly this axis. Four readers adopted it; **profiles.js is the ONE declared exemption (it is the LINKER)**, aligned to the same order. `contact_identity.test.mjs` (7, real router). Live D1 at build: 49 contacts / 1 linked / 0 divergent — behaviour-preserving today | — |
| ~~**21**~~ | ~~**K-14** sort/filter tabs on the member events list (owner 2026-08-11, Q2 rider)~~ **SHIPPED v0.145.0 — and the recorded premise was wrong in the way that mattered.** §-1m said `schedule.js:21-23` "already has a working `.tab` mechanism … K-14 is extending an existing tab row, not building one." The listener is at **:26-30**, and that row is **`List | Month` — a VIEW switcher.** Extending it would have put "in what order" and "in what layout" in one control. **It also hid a live trap:** the listener bound `document.querySelectorAll(".tab")` GLOBALLY, so any second `.tab` on the page joined the view switcher and clicking it set `mode = undefined`, dropping the page into the calendar branch — a filter that silently changes the view. Both rows now carry ids and both listeners are scoped; a guard fails if an unscoped selector returns. **What shipped:** type tabs built from the loaded events (**All + each type present**, row hidden below two types), a sort (**Date** always, **Name**/**Price** when they vary) and a direction toggle that names itself per key. **Live D1 decided it:** the schema allows five event types and only `tournament` and `league` have ever been published, so a static row would have shipped three permanently empty tabs. The **org filter**, which already computed `seen.size > 1` and drew itself anyway, now hides too | — | — |
| ~~**22**~~ | ~~**K-15** Square catalog items for priced events (owner 2026-08-11, Q5 rider)~~ **SHIPPED v0.148.0.** Migration 0047 adds `events.square_item_id` + `square_variation_id` (the plans-table shape). **The credential question, answered at build time as instructed: ONE platform token** — `orgs.square_location_id || env.SQUARE_LOCATION_ID` is the org axis, exactly the fallback registrations.js's payment links always used; per-org tokens have no infrastructure anywhere in the code. `ensureEventSquareItem` (memberships.js, beside its SUBSCRIPTION_PLAN sibling, injected into events_admin through `wireEventsAdmin` — a direct import would cycle) re-reads the ROW and judges the result: external → never (PM-1's rule at the destination), unpriced → never, already-itemed → idempotent, keyless → silent (an event without an item loses no function, unlike a plan), locationless → warning. Item name is `<event> — <date>` so a recurring series' sessions stay distinguishable. Hooks at the two unbypassable ends: `insertEvent` (return shape → `{id, square}`, every caller visited) and `bulkEdit`'s price path. The citation HELD on re-measure (first time in three sessions); what was false instead: **"memberships.js's existing Square tests" do not exist — no test had ever set a Square key or stubbed fetch**, so `event_square_item.test.mjs` brings the suite's first outbound-fetch capture and pins the SANDBOX host. **D-35** (editSeries skips `externalPriceConflict` — a third write path PM-1's enumeration missed) and **D-36** (`orgs.square_location_id` has no writer) recorded | — | — |
| ~~**23**~~ | ~~**WF-1** the Events & Programs page~~ **SHIPPED v0.133.0.** Calendar caps at CAL_DAY_CAP(3) + "+N more" day modal; `grid-template-rows: auto repeat(6, 1fr)` makes week rows even; the Views tab's `orgs` ReferenceError fixed (page declares + fetches its own list) | — | — |
| ~~**24**~~ | ~~**WF-2** bracket board shows only ACTIVE brackets~~ **SHIPPED v0.134.0.** `loadBrackets` requires live matches (live-verified post-deploy: the predicate renders exactly the 4 real brackets and suppresses all 11 strands); generation self-heals matchless rows on its write path; refused 409s stay write-free; way-in pinned | — | — |
| ~~**25**~~ | ~~**WF-3** pool board: divisions pivot horizontal/vertical~~ **SHIPPED v0.135.0.** Second `pb-seg` control in the owner's words (Horizontal bands / Vertical columns); `PB_DIV_VIEWS` one-list discipline; CSS-only pivot (render() never reads it — no orientation can drop a division); persisted + validated; D-24 classes pinned | — | — |
| ~~**26**~~ | ~~**WF-4** registrations × waivers~~ **SHIPPED v0.136.0.** Waivers column (signed/total chips through the door gate's pair — one judgement) + `POST /api/events/:id/waiver-reminders` as the second caller of the now-shared `waiverGaps`/`sendWaiverReminders`; 2-day dedupe binds both callers; address-less counted not skipped; keyless-honest. The F-27 guard rewritten to follow the pair to its new home | — | — |
| ~~**27**~~ | ~~**WF-5** the per-event manager hub~~ **COMPLETE — all four units SHIPPED.** H-1 v0.139.0 (shell + first two tabs) · H-2 v0.140.0 (tab row complete, visibility by event type, scoring pane forks tournament/league) · H-3 v0.141.0 (the all-events financial overview as a MODE of the Registrations page — no route and no query added; the property was pinned instead) · **H-4 v0.142.0** (the live tile: score as headline, leader emphasis, an en-dash placeholder instead of an empty gap, conditional seed chips from `teams.seed`; the polling and diff were NOT rebuilt). **B4's live tiles are absorbed and closed with it.** | — | — |
| ~~**28**~~ | ~~**WF-6** print ⇒ email + CSV parity~~ **SHIPPED v0.138.0.** Two CSVs added (league weeks, scoring links) + `BT_ADMIN.emailDocument` on all three print surfaces. **The email half turned out to be a REUSE, not a build:** `marketing.js` already ships event-scoped segments (W-F), campaigns and a keyless-honest `sendCampaign`, and `admin-marketing.js` already accepts `?event=` — so the button hands the printed document to that path and the honesty lives in ONE place. Recipient default said out loud: the event's own registrants. **B10 absorbed and closed with it.** `print_parity.test.mjs` states the ask as a set rule, so a fourth print surface fails on the day it is added | — | — |
| ~~**29**~~ | ~~**D-28** the org email in member copy (§-1c)~~ **SHIPPED v0.143.0.** The recorded five sites were re-measured and all five held at their exact lines — the second recorded claim in seven iterations to survive contact. `publicOrgBrand` now returns `admin_email`; `site-nav.js` v2.18 fills any `[data-org-contact]` anchor and exposes `window.btOrgContact` for pages that render after the rail paints. **Live-verified per org: 1 → `admin@boomtownvb.com`, 2 → `admin@matchptsocial.com`, 4 → `admin@coloradoboom.com`** — so the owner's constraint (org 1's address is RIGHT, and must never resolve to his login email) holds by construction, since the value comes from `orgs.admin_email` and `publicOrgBrand` takes no session. **Fail closed BY MARKUP:** anchors ship `href="help.html"` and are only rewritten to a `mailto:` once a non-empty address resolves, so the degenerate state is a live page rather than a dead link. The "exactly three fields" rule on that public SELECT was rewritten, not relaxed — the invariant was always PUBLICATION fields, never the count | — | — |

### D. Housekeeping done in v1.24

- **A label collision is FIXED: `M-1…M-4` meant two different things** — §-1h's mobile/QOL items and
  §-1m's marketplace units. **The collision was introduced in v1.23, by me, one session ago.** The
  marketplace units are renamed **PM-1…PM-4**; §-1h keeps M-1…M-4. Recorded as **D-25** because the
  lesson generalises: *this project has paid three times for ambiguous anchors in code, and the
  roadmap's own identifiers were never checked for the same thing.*
- **Measured, not estimated:** 22 sections, **210 item-definition lines (158 open / 52 closed)**, and
  **39 labels that appear under more than one section** — most of those are legitimate register
  cross-references; **M-1…M-4 and S-3 were the two that denote genuinely different work.** `S-3` is
  left alone deliberately: both meanings are closed, so renaming would be churn with no reader.

**v1.21 (2026-08-10, iteration 50):** **§-1j T2-5 FULLY CLOSED — the cross-pool residual shipped in
v0.124.0.** `crossPoolOrder` arranges each bracket group so round one does not repeat pool play,
applied AFTER the A/BB split (the split is by finish) and skipped for a hand-picked seed list.
Byes stay with the top finishers, within-pool order is preserved, the mapping is total, and a
single-pool or unpooled event is returned untouched. **The guard measures against a DERIVED floor:
byes are taken by the best finishers, so equal pool SIZES do not mean equal representation in
round one — some rematches are pigeonhole arithmetic, and asserting zero would have sent the next
reader hunting a bug that does not exist.** Next per the sequence: **T2-8 pool board.**
**v1.20 (2026-08-10, iteration 49):** **§-1j T2-11's card grid CLOSED and §-1c D-22 CLOSED, in one
commit (v0.123.0).** The member front door offers no admin destination; the single sanctioned exit
is one staff-gated Control Center card, and the guard now reaches `app.js`. **T2-11's REMAINING
HALF IS AN OWNER DECISION, not a build:** `home.html` v2.0.0 (announcements + CTA row) exists and
nothing routes anyone to it — the owner asked to "open to the designed home page, not the features
selection screen", but which of the two landing pages survives is **D-19's open question** and
changes what every signed-in user sees first. Ask before building. Next per the sequence:
**T2-5's cross-pool seeding residual.**
**v1.19 (2026-08-10, iteration 48):** **§-1j T2-3, T2-4b and T2-1d struck — CLOSED in v0.122.0.**
Captains ride along, with the **gating distinction made explicit**: the staff feed names them in
full, the two UNGATED feeds honour the captain's own visibility. The 15-point score cap is gone
(chips + a numeric box bounded by `points_to`). **T2-1d was NOT a dangling class** — `.ed-side`
was one name doing two jobs (tile spans AND the fairness aside), so panel padding/border/sticky
leaked into every 62px tile and the mobile `order:-1` reordered the tile's grid. **New defect
D-23: dangling classes measured (197 across 54 pages); `.sr-only` is undefined on `help.html`
and `live.html`, so screen-reader-only text renders VISIBLY there** — recorded, not chased.
Next per the sequence: **T2-11's cards + D-22's widened guard in the same commit.**
**v1.18 (2026-08-10, iteration 47):** **§-1j T2-5's REWIRE HALF struck — CLOSED in v0.121.0.** The
button posts the modern engine (body key `a_size`, the legacy `aSize` was silently ignored),
renders the engine's summary, links `admin-brackets.html?event=` (deep link added — the
admin-waitlists idiom, chipping at T2-10). The legacy route + `createBracket` are REMOVED, not
orphaned; `bracket_rewire.test.mjs` pins the client/server pair as a relationship and asserts the

named: CROSS-POOL round-one seeding (#1A v #4B; 3 pools → #1A–#4C, #1B–#4A) is NOT in this unit**
— it is a seeding-order change inside `generateBracketFor` and is the next T2-5 work, with the
property guard (no round-one pair shares a pool, 2–4 pools; every breaking team lands exactly one
slot). Next per the sequence: **T2-3 + T2-4b + T2-1d as one small-fixes unit.**
**v1.17 (2026-08-10, iteration 46):** **§-1j T2-13 + T2-14 struck — CLOSED in v0.120.0.**
`leagues.js` attaches the bearer token (and its false comment is rewritten); the 401 card now
distinguishes no-session from signed-in-but-unlinked. `token_convention.test.mjs` holds the rule

## §-1. TESTER-ROUND FIXES — build these first (added v1.3, 2026-08-05)

Full evidence, per-screen results and the reproductions are in
`docs/2026-08-05_audit_tester-round_v1_0.md`. Summary of why this section exists: measured in a live
signed-in browser on org 1, **the screens the tester round reported dead all work** — the Pool Board
renders 30 draggable tiles, Tryouts renders 6 events and its cards, and every endpoint they call
returns 200 with real data. The apparent total failure came from four defects, not from the modules.

| Block | Item | Why it is here | Effort | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** | **Reorder + widen `WIPE_SQL`** in `worker/src/sandbox.js`; derive delete order from the schema graph | **Generate AND wipe are both permanently dead after the first successful run.** `brackets` is deleted at index 3, the `matches` that reference it at index 8; D1 enforces FKs, `D1.batch()` is one transaction, so the whole reseed rolls back → the 500. The blocking rows are written by generate's own last step (drawing Winter Jam's bracket), so press #1 works and every press after it fails. Wipe shares the list, so there was no recovery path | S | — |
| **A2** | **Second-press negative control** for the seeder | A suite of 1258 stayed green through a totally dead button because the fixture test seeds an **empty** database. Must be generate → draw bracket → generate again, and must fail before A1 | S | — |
| ~~**A3**~~ | ~~KOTC session in the seed; price the paid test registrations~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — every square-paid test registration gets a COMPLETED `payments` mirror at the event price ($895 all-time), `payments` joined WIPE_SQL before `registrations`, and a draft KOTC session with a 12-player entry list sits on the Thursday league (round 1 left for the real engine) | S | ✅ |
| ~~**B1**~~ | ~~Org switcher lists only orgs from `/api/me`~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — admin-nav.js v2.22 populates from the intersection of `/api/orgs` and the caller's admin/staff roles, **plus a self-heal**: a stored `bt_org` outside the role list resets to the first role org and reloads once | S | ✅ |
| ~~**B2**~~ | ~~Active org visible on every admin screen~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — the header wordmark names the ACTIVE org (post-paint text swap beside the org's own logo; static markup untouched so header byte-identity holds) | S | ✅ |
| ~~**B3**~~ | ~~Org-scoped empty state~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — `BT_ADMIN.orgEmptyState()` names the org, offers one-tap switches to the caller's other orgs, and offers Generate test data on org 1 (the seeder writes org 1 only — see §-1c) — wired into all six event-driven modules | S | ✅ |
| ~~**B4**~~ | ~~403 renders as "no access to \<org\>"~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — `BT_ADMIN.loadFail()` renders a 403 as "You don't have access to \<org\>" with switch actions; the blame string is retired and `org_honesty.test.mjs` scans the widest set for its return | S | ✅ |
| **B5** | Decide `bt_org`: `localStorage` vs session scope | Still an owner call — the v0.89.0 self-heal makes either answer safe (a poisoned value can no longer stick), which lowers the stakes | XS | Owner call |
| ~~**C1**~~ | ~~Version-derived SW cache name; drop `ignoreSearch`~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — sw.js v2.0 derives `CACHE` from the swept buster and the fallback matches the exact URL; `sw_cache.test.mjs` reconstructs the 67-release defect as its negative control | S | ✅ |
| ~~**C2**~~ | ~~One-time unregister / cache purge~~ **DONE v0.89.0** — the first activation of sw.js v2.0 evicts every cache `!== CACHE`, including the poisoned `bt-shell-v1`; the SW update check bypasses HTTP cache, so a normal navigation delivers it with no flag needed | S | ✅ |
| **C3** | Confirm C on the owner's machine (DevTools → unregister → clear → hard reload) | **[INFERENCE]** best explanation for the unlabeled gold buttons: measured clean, that control is `#0B0B0D` on `#D4AF37`, ~13:1, label present. Decides whether C is urgent or merely correct | XS | Owner |
| ~~**D1**~~ | ~~Create-session + entry-list UI for KOTC~~ **DONE v0.90.0** — admin-event.js v0.5.0 carries the King of the Court card (sessions list, "+ New session", member-search entry-list picker; creating a session opens the picker immediately). The court board's empty state now links to a control that exists | M | ✅ |
| ~~**D2**~~ | ~~Guard: every admin route has a caller in `web/`~~ **DONE v0.90.0** — `route_reachability.test.mjs`: routes derived from the two routing idioms, callers from web/ with template holes masked, matched token-wise. **Baseline of 25 uncalled routes recorded (§-1c D-4) as a shrink-only ratchet**: a new uncalled route fails at birth, a healed one demands its strike | S | ✅ |
| ~~**E1**~~ | ~~Brackets chooser: empty state + backdrop close~~ **DONE v0.91.0** — a filter miss says "No teams match…" with the recovery in the sentence; backdrop click closes | XS | ✅ |
| ~~**E2**~~ | ~~Divisions: "more to do" vs "broken"~~ **DONE v0.91.0** — the strip is a checklist (`role="status"`, lead-in, a fix in each sentence), the court badge explains a missing court count instead of contradicting Suggest, the state line says why Save is off, and the duplicated status strings collapsed into one renderer | S | ✅ |
| ~~**E3**~~ | ~~First paint: auto-select the first event everywhere~~ **DONE v0.91.0** — tournament.html opens on the first real event; Scoring Links keeps its deliberate no-write-on-paint but the empty state is now the instruction AND the primary action | S | ✅ |
| ~~**E4**~~ | ~~Rename the two undated migration files~~ **DONE v0.91.0** — `2026-07-22_0003_admin_schedule.sql`, `2026-07-26_0025_guardian_invite.sql` (their real apply dates); contents byte-identical because they are the record of what ran on live D1; a lexical sort now replays in apply order | XS | ✅ |

**Sequence: A → B → C → D → E.** A unblocks all testing (nothing else can be exercised without a
sandbox). B removes the cause of most of the report. C prevents a recurring "works on my machine"
class. D closes a feature that is fully built and entirely unreachable. E is real but cosmetic beside
the rest.

**Three process gaps this round exposed, all cheap to close:**
1. **No test runs twice.** The state that breaks a reseed is the state the previous reseed left behind.
2. **No test runs as a second org.** Every guard runs on org 1 with data; the whole failure surface in
   this report lives one dropdown away and no test has ever been there. Needs a user in an **empty**
   org and a user with **no role**.
3. **Reachability is unguarded in the client direction.** A route with no caller passes every gate.

## §-1b. THE REGISTRATION-FIRST WORKFLOW PROGRAM (added v1.4, 2026-08-05 — owner spec)

**The owner's operating thesis, verbatim in substance:** registration is how the business collects
its data. A registration (league or tournament — today a Google Form) creates the database of
fields; captains + team name + chosen level are the primary keys of that database (contact info is
for communication; team names carry strength history). Every operational artifact — pool sheets,
brackets, league rosters, weekly schedules, tryout evaluation tables, member records, comms/marketing
segments — is **populated FROM registrations**. The platform must make that flow visible and direct:
each module starts from the registration database, not from its own empty state.

**Symptom → root-cause map for the owner's 2026-08-05 report** (before building anything from it):
"most screens fail / couldn't load your events / members broken / circular navigation on Edge and
Chrome" → **R1 org poisoning (Block B) + R3 stale service worker (Block C)** — the audit measured
these screens working on org 1. "Regenerate test data fails after the 2nd load, even after wiping" →
**R2, FIXED v0.88.0 and verified live twice**; if it persists on the owner's machine it is R3 serving
pre-fix JS — re-verify after Block C, do not reopen `sandbox.js` without a live repro. "Court board
empty / no test data" → **R4 (Block D) + A3**. What is genuinely NEW feature work is below.

| Unit | What it is | Existing substrate (verify by grep, not trust) | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**W-A**~~ | ~~Registration → league roster wiring~~ **DONE v0.92.0** — the claim verified true server-side (submitRegistration wrote teams + members since day one); what was missing was every read/edit surface. New staff roster routes in registrations.js (GET/PATCH team, POST/PATCH/DELETE members, all org-scoped soft-delete), a shared roster modal (`team-roster.js`) opened from the event page's registrations table AND League Manager's levels board, the registration named inside the roster and the roster one tap from the registration, plus seeded league teams linked to their registrations. `team_roster.test.mjs` drives the REAL public registration first, then edits the roster it created | M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-B**~~ | ~~League weekly schedule → edit → export → score by link~~ **DONE v0.93.0** — verified first: generate-week, 2-tap differential scoring ("Who won? → by how many") and the captains' score links ("We won / They won → by N") already satisfied the owner's differential rule. Built the real gaps: **edit a matchup by entry** (modal on every unscored game → the formats.js `/schedule/teams` route, uncalled since the format engine shipped — first strike from the D-4 baseline) · **drag-drop discoverable** (toolbar link to Schedule Editor with the league preselected via new `?event=` support) · **export** (print stylesheet — the sheet on the gym door — plus per-week Copy-as-text for group texts and email) | M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-C**~~ | ~~Tournament flow: registrations → divisions → pool sheets~~ **DONE v0.94.0** — verified first: `poolSizes()` has implemented the owner's split table since v0.70.0 (measured: 15→8+7, 16→8+8, 17→9+8, 19→10+9, 5→one small pool with the honest note; the 8-game floor doubles small pools). The gap was the SCREEN: Tournament Ops gains **"Plan the day"** — equal-game options → plain-sentence plan preview **with the pool-split defaults riding along** (`/formats/plan` now returns `pool_split`) → commit via `generate-schedule` (409-then-replace on an explicit second press). **Three more D-4 strikes (24 → 21)**: `formats/options`, `formats/plan`, `events/*/generate-schedule`. AA/Open-smaller and A/BB-mix-then-split ride as panel guidance pointing at Divisions. Crossover-bracket aiming 4/8 = brackets v0.66.0 (existing) | M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-D**~~ | ~~Seeding suggestions from history~~ **DONE v0.95.0** — built from the spec, not re-derived. `worker/src/board_suggest.js` (read-only) + a fifth board-payload key + one panel + `board_suggestions.test.mjs` (+22). **No route, no migration.** Four signals, each independently guarded: `spread_winners` · `spread_strength` · `split_repeat` · `spread_area`. The 6–11 preference suppresses a *suggestion*, never a save; an unsatisfiable suggestion is dropped in silence; no sentence names a person. **The owner settled spec §7 and it widened the design:** "won before" is no longer a boolean but a **placement within a division plus a tier-weighted score** — tiers 100 apart (Open/AA 400 · A 300 · BB 200 · B 100 · Rec 0) with placing 0–100 inside a tier, so the bottom of A and the top of BB come out equal *by construction*, which is the "minor overlap … similar to ELO" the owner described. Subs excluded (owner: "non issue" for tournaments). `standings.rank` is never read — placement is recomputed per division | M | ✅ |
| **W-H** | **Persisted team ratings (the real ELO).** The owner's "then convert into ElO number for raning **potentially**" — W-D derives its score read-only at board time, which is correct and needs no storage. A durable rating that survives across events needs a ratings table, a write path on score entry, and a K-factor decision. **Owner called it "potentially": do not build it until they ask.** | W-D's `tierOf` + placement recomputation is the scoring half already written and measured | M |
| **W-I** | **League sub record** (owner 2026-08-05, answering the W-D subs question): *"For leagues yes, and we can count the number of wins they have as subs on their record (and divisions played). The team record doesnt matter if they have subs, but they should be noted if they get subs regularly to admin."* So: count a sub's **wins** and divisions played, **never their losses**, and flag to staff a team that takes subs regularly. Nothing to do with seeding — W-D excludes subs entirely | `team_members.is_sub` exists and live D1 has **zero** sub rows, so this is unexercised against real data. `subs/requests` and `subs/signups` are two of the D-4 uncalled routes | M |
| ~~**W-E.1**~~ | ~~Tryouts: the director's sortable roll-up~~ **DONE v0.96.0** — verified first, and the engine was already there again: `GET /api/admin/tryouts/:id/summary` had been built, tested and org-scoped since **v0.60.0 with no caller**, while the page's "Director summary" button pointed at `admin-buildstatus.html`. Built the screen: the roll-up as a **view on the same page** (same event picker, one tap either way), every evaluated player with offers / noes / coach count / **rating range** / "2/3 offer", each column sortable from its header, sort state in `aria-sort` and shown as an arrow character rather than colour. **The rating is a range and nothing averages it** — `rollUp`'s own comment says a mean of 3.5 erases the 2-and-5 case, and NC-1 mutates the real client to compute one and proves the guard catches it. `tryouts_rollup.test.mjs` (+11). **D-4 baseline 21 → 20** | M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-E.2**~~ | ~~Tryouts: the squad board~~ **DONE v0.97.0** — the four squad routes (`squads` GET/POST, `squads/:id` PATCH/DELETE, `squads/:id/assign`, `squads/:id/remove`) were all built, tested and org-scoped with **no caller anywhere**; verified before designing, for the fifth consecutive W-unit, and again the engine was whole and only the screen was missing. Built `admin-squads.html` + `admin-squads.js` as a **second screen** — this row’s own words, and the pool board is the named precedent. **Two taps, no dragging:** pick a player, pick the team; assign MOVES an already-placed player, so a move is the same two taps. **Every listener is delegated and attached once at boot** — §-1c D-6 deliberately NOT inherited, enforced by a positional guard. The server owns "short" and "full"; the client renders `squadNeeds()`’s verdict and does no arithmetic of its own. `tryouts_squads.test.mjs` (+14) with three negative controls that mutate the real client. **D-4 baseline 20 → 16** | M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-E.2b**~~ | ~~Tryouts: staff card correction~~ **DONE v0.98.0 — and tryouts is FINISHED.** `PUT /api/admin/tryouts/:eventId/card/:contactId` had been built, tested and org-scoped since **v0.60.0 with no caller**; verified before designing for the sixth consecutive W-unit. Built only the form, on the evaluate card in `admin-tryouts.html` as this row said — a quiet "Fix details" disclosure per player, **two taps and typing** to correct a card (req #19), covering exactly `CARD_COLS`. **A list crosses the wire as an ARRAY, never a comma string**: `parseList` JSON.parses a string first, so a lone `"16"` parses as the *number* 16 and comes back empty — a raw-string post would silently delete the age group just typed, while `"14U, 16U"` works, which is why that bug would pass review. **The client does no unit arithmetic** — height is stored metric and rendered imperial by the server, and a feet/inches box would round-trip lossily and rewrite a stored height on every unrelated save. Both `#tList` listeners delegated once at boot (§-1c D-6 not inherited, asserted positionally); a re-render keeps the draft and restores focus. `tryouts_card.test.mjs` (+12) with three negative controls that mutate the real client. **D-4 baseline 16 → 15, and the tryouts cluster is empty** | S | ✅ |
| ~~**W-E.3**~~ | ~~"Send offers from the table"~~ **DROPPED 2026-08-06 — owner: "that is done in a separate system."** Asked once, answered once. There is no offer route, there will not be one, and nothing in this platform sends, tracks or records an offer to a tryout player. Same shape as SafeSport/M25: the correct build is **no field and no route**. The tryouts workflow ENDS at the squad board — evaluate → roll up → place on a team. Do not re-open | Nothing to build. Deliberately | — | ⛔ |
| ~~**W-F**~~ | ~~Registration → members → comms~~ **DONE v0.99.0.** This row's own note was right and the verification cost one grep: `contacts` IS populated by checkout (`registrations.js:302`), the members screen (`admin-users.js`) DOES render name / email / phone / from / opt-in with bulk unsubscribe, and — unlike the six W-units before it — the segments engine was **already fully called** (15 call sites, no D-4 entry). So the gap was none of the three parts, it was the **join between them**: the filter vocabulary was `tags` · `played` · `since`, and `played` knows only the event **TYPE**. Live D1 says **0 of 49** org-1 contacts carry a tag, so the tag filter reaches nobody, and "email the people who registered for the Valentines tournament" was **not expressible at all**. Shipped: an `event` filter on segments, whose clause pins `e.org_id` to **?1** — the org bind `BASE_WHERE` already carries — so it costs no extra bind and a stored `filter_json` holding another org's event id still matches nothing. **A dropped event filter WIDENS the segment rather than emptying it** (no clause → `BASE_WHERE` alone → all 49 instead of 14), and a `<select>` posts the STRING `"7"`, so `asEventId` coerces before validating and A2/A3 prove that against the real helper. The flow is visible: **"Email these registrants" on the registrations screen → the segment form opens with the event chosen and the segment named** — one tap there, one confirm here. `marketing_segments_event.test.mjs` (+14) with four negative controls that mutate the real source. No migration, no new route, D-4 unchanged at 15 | S–M | ✅ |
| ~~**W-G**~~ | ~~Seed data modeled on the real 2026 events~~ **DONE v0.100.0.** **The blocker recorded here for three sessions was false: Google Drive IS reachable headless.** One `search_files` call settled it, and the "needs an interactive session" note had been carried forward on trust since 2026-08-02. Read the real **2026 Spring REVCO** export (21 team rows, same form as Valentines) and reshaped the fixture to carry four shapes real sign-ups have and the seed could not produce: (1) **a contact with NO email** — the "Emails of Teammates" cell is free text holding three addresses for a team of four, one address for everybody, or the literal `N/A`/`No`/`Don't have.`; with an address on every seeded row `reachable` and `total` on the marketing overview could never differ, so W-F's segments counted every contact reachable forever. Its id is EVEN on purpose: all contactOffsets are even so captains are always odd ids, and a registering captain always supplies an address. (2) **`email-sent`** — the commonest real unpaid state (3 of 21 Spring, 4 of 29 Valentines); the registrations screen's Unpaid filter is pending/email-sent/cash-pending and the fixture produced only two of the three. (3) **`A/AA` alongside `BB/A`** — the only two labels the form offers; `tierOf` scores them 350 and 250, and no seeded team occupied the 350 rung, so no seeded comparison could span two tiers. (4) **a team name containing a comma** (`Jarvis, Jork It A Lil`, real) — the naive-CSV-split shape, same class as the apostrophe in `Spike Lee's` that `q()` exists for. Also fixed a comment reading "24 test people" over a 48-name list. `sandbox_real_shapes.test.mjs` (+10) — every assertion proves the shape **changes a downstream answer** rather than that a row exists, four NCs mutate the real seeded rows. `tierOf` exported so the level check runs against the real matcher, not a copy of its regex. No migration, no route | S–M | ✅ |

**Sequencing:** W-A → W-B (league spine) → W-C → W-D (tournament spine) → W-E (tryouts) → W-F →
W-G feeds all of them and may be pulled earlier if an interactive session has Drive access.
**Every W-unit starts at a registration and ends at an operational artifact** — that is the owner's
acceptance test.

## §-1d. THE NAVIGATION & PROGRAM-SCOPING PROGRAM (added v1.5, 2026-08-06 evening — owner spec)

The owner's second report of 2026-08-06. It is a **product/IA program**, not a defect list: the
modules work, but everything is offered everywhere, and the menu has grown by accretion. Queues
behind §-1 and §-1b. **One unit per iteration**; N-1 and N-2 below are ordinary bugs and jump the
queue.

**Owner's words, kept verbatim so a paraphrase cannot drift** (the C1 lesson):

> "when registrations are created, we will have different types of programs and those should not be
> displayed in other modules. Meaning create league --> only league module, create tournament -->
> tournament, etc. Try out - > try outs. They should not be visible in every module. The tournament
> module should potentially be available for league for their tournament, but it might be a sub
> category of the league with the league teams. We need to simplify the menu a bit and keep
> tournaments into a collapsable section or its own menu along the top bar rather than down the
> total menu. We woud not do bracket without pool, and etc. Tournaments Manager on the side menu,
> then the top tabs in the screen can be each function. League should be similar rather than have
> broken out into additional menus. Menus need to optimzied and reviewed for brevity."

### ✅ N-4a. **THE TOURNAMENT-INSIDE-A-LEAGUE QUESTION IS ANSWERED (2026-08-08) — and the answer is BOTH, keyed on where the roster comes from.**

**Owner, verbatim:**
> "Yes, there is a tournament inside a league, so we can either allow the tournament module to pull
> league data (teams) in and then design the tournament with the league players. Some leagues have
> all their players play since it is during a league night (can be within the league module) vs
> another league may do a separate tournament which invites everyone to register then put the
> tournament on based on whos attends."

**This is not a fudge — it names two genuinely different products, and the discriminator is the
ROSTER SOURCE:**

- **Shape A — the league-night tournament.** It happens *during* a league night, the players are
  already there, and the roster is **the league's existing teams/players**. There is no separate
  registration. **It lives inside the League module.**
- **Shape B — the linked open tournament.** It is announced separately, **anyone registers**, and
  the bracket is built from **whoever actually attends**. It is an ordinary tournament that merely
  links back to the league.

**WHAT THIS SETTLES, and it is the thing that was blocking the whole program:**
1. **The §-1d navigation shape.** Shape A is a **tab INSIDE the League frame** — the owner says so
   directly ("can be within the league module"). Shape B is a **peer frame**, an ordinary
   tournament. So the answer to "is Tournaments a tab inside League or a frame beside it" is
   **both, and the event's roster source decides which**. A frame design that assumed one shape
   would have been wrong for half the real cases.
2. **The data question underneath it.** Shape A needs the tournament module to **read the league's
   teams** rather than its own registration list. Shape B uses the existing registration path
   unchanged. **Neither requires a new roster table** — it requires knowing which source to read.
3. **N-6 ("we would not do bracket without pool") is still open** and is a different question.

**NOT YET SETTLED, and it should be asked before this is built:** what marks an event as Shape A?
The likeliest shapes are a `parent_league_event_id` on the tournament, or a flag plus a league
reference. **Do not invent the column** — §-1c D-5 already records that `standings.rank` is an
event-wide pool finish with no division filter, and `createBracket` seeds from `ORDER BY rank`, so
"draw the bracket from the league's teams" collides with an existing ranking defect. **Read D-5
before designing Shape A's seeding.**

### ~~N-1. The member/admin shell ping-pong~~ — **FIXED v0.101.0**

**TWO mechanisms, and the second is the one that actually traps you.** Both fixed; guards in
`header_actions.test.mjs`.

**(a) The member sidebar advertised the admin shell.** `site-nav.js:235-242` pushed a `Manage`
group of four admin destinations (Tournament Ops, Events & Programs, Registrations, Member
Management) into the MEMBER nav for any staff viewer, while the admin shell header links back to
the member site — each shell offered the other. Deleted; the single header `#btHdrAdmin` link is
the way back. Also serves N-5 (brevity).

**(b) THE BACK-TRAP — the real cause.** `grep -rn "location.replace" web/` returned **nothing**:
every bounce in the codebase used `location.href`, which **pushes** a history entry. So a bounce
off an admin page left `[..., admin.html, home.html]` on the stack — press Back, the browser
re-enters `admin.html`, **repaints the entire admin shell** (`admin.html` ships its rail and all
module names as static markup, so it is on screen before `/api/me` can answer), and `guard()`
bounces forward again. **Back never escapes, and the admin shell is re-exposed on every attempt.**
That is "switch back and forth and expose the admin page", precisely. `guard()`'s four rejection
paths now use `location.replace`. Deliberate user navigations (View as member, the two
`history.back()` helpers) still push — a user's own click belongs in their history.

**The likeliest way the owner hit it:** *View as member* sets `bt_demo_member`, and that flag is
the FIRST condition in `guard()` — so while it is set, **every** admin page bounces. Pressing Back
to get to the Control Center loops until the flag is cleared.

**Also fixed:** `site-nav.js:154`'s `|| (me.roles || [])[0]` fallback handed the caller their first
role in ANY org when they had none in the org on screen, so a member here who is staff elsewhere
saw the Admin link for an org they hold no role in.

**Not a breach.** `role` comes from `/api/me`, and every `/api/admin` route re-checks server-side
against `userId + orgId`. A member never reads admin data — see §-1e S-1 for the two genuinely
ungated routes, which are a different matter.

**Still open, found while fixing this** — recorded, not chased:
- **N-1c. The admin rail links out to member pages** (`admin.html:120,125-128` → `settings.html`,
  `index.html`, `schedule.html`, `live.html`, `leagues.html`). Clicking one swaps the whole shell,
  and the way back only appears once `/api/me` resolves. Shell-swap in the other direction. **This
  belongs to the N-4 menu work.**
- **N-1d. Soft lockout if `/api/me` is slow or 5xx.** `site-nav.js:171` wraps BOTH the Exit pill and
  the Admin reveal in `if (signedIn)`, which is only true on a 200. A staff user in demo mode with
  a failing `/api/me` gets neither control, and `guard()` still bounces them off every admin page —
  no UI route back. The `#logoutBtn` precedent already solved this shape: reveal from the local
  token, not the response.
- **N-1e. The admin shell paints before it is authorised.** One `/api/me` round trip of full rail +
  module names for anyone who reaches an admin URL. No data (every module awaits `guard()`), but it
  is the expose half of the report and `replace()` only stops the *loop*, not the *flash*.

### N-2. A modal that hangs, and one whose input is unexplained

**Owner:** *"we have a few areas where things time out like the screen with the pop up - that pop up
freezes and does nothing, and i do not know what to enter."* Two complaints in one sentence —
(a) a modal that appears to do nothing when used, (b) a modal whose field does not say what it
wants. Candidates are inventoried by the 2026-08-06 audit; **the owner should be asked which screen**
if the inventory does not produce an obvious single answer, because "the screen with the pop up" is
not yet resolvable to one page with confidence.

### N-3. Program-type scoping — a program appears only in its own module

Registrations create programs of a type; today the modules do not consistently narrow by it, so a
league shows up in tournament surfaces and vice versa. **The rule:** create league → League module
only · create tournament → Tournament only · tryout → Tryouts only.

### N-4. "Tournaments Manager" — one rail item, functions as in-screen tabs

Collapse the tournament cluster from several rail entries to **one** (`Tournaments Manager`), with
each former entry becoming a **tab inside that screen**. The owner offers two shapes — a collapsible
rail section, or a top bar — and prefers the functions live as top tabs in the screen.
**Reuse an existing tab idiom if one exists; inventing a second one is the defect** (the v0.96.1
trap). **League gets the same treatment** once the tournament shape is proven.

### N-5. Menu brevity pass

*"Menus need to optimzied and reviewed for brevity."* After N-4, re-count the rail and state the
number. Click minimization is already standing owner requirement #19 — **count the taps and say the
number** in the review.

### N-6. "We would not do bracket without pool"

A workflow ordering rule: brackets are not a first move. Whether this becomes a hard refusal or a
default-with-override is **an owner decision** — the standing §-1b rule is that the owner's own
process rules ship as **defaults an operator can override, never as refusals**, so the default
reading is: do not *offer* brackets before pools exist, but do not *forbid* them.

### Open owner questions raised by this spec — ask, do not guess

1. **Tournament inside a league.** The owner wrote *"should potentially be available"* and *"it
   might be a sub category of the league with the league teams."* That is thinking aloud, not a
   decision. **Do not build it until the owner confirms** whether a league-owned tournament is (a)
   a tournament tagged to a league, drawing that league's teams, or (b) a separate tournament that
   merely links back.
2. **N-6:** hard refusal or soft default (above).
3. **N-2:** which screen, if the audit cannot single one out.
