## §-1e. SECURITY REVIEW & PENETRATION TEST (added v1.5, 2026-08-06 — owner request)

**Owner:** *"Add to roadmap total security check and penetration test all the segments of the
website."* Recorded as a standing engineering track, not a one-off — a one-off audit is stale the
day after it is written, which is the same reason `route_reachability` is a ratchet.

**Done in this session, so the track starts from a measured baseline rather than zero:**

**Finding S-1 — NOT clean. TWO ungated routes, and my own scan reported clean before finding them.**

### ~~S-1a~~ — **FIXED v0.102.0**, and the ratchet that enforces it now exists

`GET /api/admin/permissions` is gated with `requireStaff` (inline-block style, `admin.js`).
**`requireStaff`, not `requireAdmin`**: it is the least that closes the disclosure and it cannot
break a working caller. The single caller is `admin-users.js:286`, on a page whose own data is
separately admin-gated (`listUsers` → `requireAdmin`), so tightening to admin is available and is a
**reviewable decision, not something to slip in under cover of a security fix.**
`worker/test/admin_route_gating.test.mjs` asserts every `/api/admin/*` dispatch is gated by one of
the three styles, with NCs that re-open S-1a from the fixed source, strip `pos.js`'s single
module-level gate (exposing all 11 of its routes, not one), prove a gate DEFINITION is not a call
site, and prove comment-blanking preserves offsets and line numbers.

**IT WAS RUN BEFORE THE FIX AND WATCHED TO FAIL — and that one run did two jobs.** It named the
real defect (so it is not vacuous) **and named nothing else across the other 101 dispatch sites**
(so it has no false positives — where two earlier hand-rolled scans produced 25 between them). A
guard whose first run reproduces a known defect *and only that* has earned its assertion.

**IT ALSO CAUGHT ITS OWN BUG ON THAT SAME RUN, which is the reusable part.** It reported *two*
ungated routes: S-1a, and `consent.js:503 /api/admin/media-consent/*`. Reading the code rather than
believing the output settled it — `historyMediaConsent` and `withdrawOptOut` both gate on their
first line (`consent.js:400,442`). **The scan was wrong, not the code.** The cause: that module
splits the dispatch across two statements — `mt = p.match(/…/);` then `if (mt) { … }` — and a
region that stops at the `;` never reaches the handlers. Fixed by following the assignment to the
`if` that tests it, *precisely* rather than by widening the region, because an over-wide region
lets a neighbouring gated route vouch for an ungated one — **and being too permissive is the worse
failure, because it reports clean.** The standing lesson, now paid for twice in this track: **a
scan is a guard, and its output is not evidence until it has been checked against code you read.**

**The original finding, kept for the record:**

**S-1a. `GET /api/admin/permissions` had no session check of any kind** (`worker/src/admin.js:48`):
`if (p === "/api/admin/permissions" && m === "GET") return json({ roles: ROLES, permissions: PERMISSIONS });`
It is the FIRST dispatch line in `adminRoutes`, above every gate. It returns two module-level
constants — the role list and the capability matrix — so there is **no org data and no PII**, and
the severity is low. But it is an ungated `/api/admin/*` route and it discloses the authorisation
model to anyone who asks. **Fix: gate it like its neighbours.** Cheap, and it makes the invariant
in item 1 below true instead of nearly-true.

**S-1b. `GET /api/orgs` is unauthenticated and lists every active org** (`worker/src/index.js:363`
→ `listOrgs(env)`, which takes no `ctx`). It is routed *above* the `startsWith("/api/")` branch, so
it never reaches `buildCtx`. Any anonymous caller gets `id, name, slug, logo_url, brand_json` for
all active orgs. This may well be deliberate — the sign-in surface needs org branding — but it is
not written down as deliberate anywhere, and `header_shell.test.mjs:85-88` pins the admin
switcher as a caller, so **check who actually needs it unauthenticated before gating it.**

**HOW I NEARLY SHIPPED "CLEAN" — this is the reusable part, and it is this project rule verbatim.**
I wrote a scan, it reported *no ungated admin handlers across ~25 modules*, and I recorded that
here as a verified finding. It was wrong. The scan treated the line
`async function requireAdmin(env, ctx)` — a **definition** — as evidence that `admin.js` was gated,
so it skipped the module and never examined line 48. **"Assert call sites, never definitions" is a
standing rule in INDEX §4 and I broke it inside the very check meant to enforce security.** Two
earlier versions were worse: one stripped comments without preserving line numbers so every
reported line number was wrong, and one matched the guard clause
`if (!p.startsWith("/api/admin/pos/")) return null;` as though it were a route. All three reported
confidently. **A scan is a guard. It gets a negative control before its output is believed —
including, and especially, when it reports clean.**

**What IS confirmed, and it is most of the surface:** every other `/api/admin/*` route is gated, in
one of three legitimate styles, and a check that knows only one style produces nonsense:

- **Three legitimate gating styles exist**, and a check that knows only one reports garbage.
  (i) *module-level* — one gate before the admin dispatch block (`pos.js:21`, `marketing.js:188`);
  (ii) *per-handler* — the handler's first statement gates (`events_admin.js:89`);
  (iii) *inline block* — `if (p === "/api/admin/…") { const deny = await H.requireStaff(...)` as the
  block's first line (`documents.js:281`, `tiers.js:236`, `passes.js:204`, `calendar.js:450`).
- Two earlier versions of the scan produced **25 false positives**: the first stripped comments
  without preserving line numbers so every reported line was wrong; the second matched
  `if (!p.startsWith("/api/admin/pos/")) return null;` — a guard clause — as if it were a route.
  **Both reported confidently.** The lesson is the standing one: a scan is a guard, and a guard
  gets a negative control before its output is believed.
- `requireStaff` itself is correct: it reads `user_org_roles` for **`ctx.userId` + `ctx.orgId`**,
  so it cannot be satisfied by a role in a different org (`index.js:339-343`).

**Consequence for N-1:** the member/admin bleed is presentation-only — no member can read admin
data. Say so plainly to the owner rather than letting a UI complaint read as a breach. **S-1a and
S-1b are separate, real, and neither is what the owner saw.**

### S-2 — the authorization matrix, v0.103.0. **THE MEASUREMENT IS THE FINDING.**

**`requireAdmin` has EXACTLY FOUR call sites in the entire worker, all four in `admin.js`**
(`listUsers`, `addUser`, `setRole`, `revokeRole`). Every other admin route across all 38 modules —
roughly **190 gate calls** — admits **staff**. The admin-only surface is **3 dispatch sites out of
102**. That is not a defect on its face; it is the shape of the system, and it was never written
down.

**THE LIVE NUMBER THAT RANKS EVERYTHING BELOW, read from production D1 on 2026-08-08:**
`user_org_roles` holds **3 admin rows and ZERO staff rows, across ONE user account**. So today
every one of those ~190 `requireStaff` gates admits exactly `{the single admin}`. **The
staff-vs-admin distinction is currently theoretical, and nothing below is exploitable by anyone.**
**It stops being theoretical the moment the first staff member is added** — which is precisely what
`addUser` exists to do. Everything in S-2 is a loaded gun, not a fire.

**What v0.103.0 SHIPPED, and what it deliberately did not.** The guard pins **what the codebase
already decided** — the admin-only set may GROW freely, and may never SHRINK — plus behavioural
proof through the real router that staff gets 403 on all four admin-only handlers, member gets 403
on a staff route, anonymous gets 401 on both, and admin gets through. **It encodes no opinion about
which routes deserve admin.** That is an owner judgement and it is asked once, below.

**S-2a. `POST /api/admin/security/rescue-link` is STAFF-gated** (`security.js:50`, module-level).
`rescueLink` (`security.js:111-125`) takes any email, looks it up in `users` with **no org
predicate and no role restriction**, and issues a magic sign-in link. **A staff member can trigger
a sign-in link for ANY account on the platform** — including an admin, including admins of orgs
they hold no role in. **Severity depends entirely on delivery, and the two cases are far apart:**
with `BREVO_API_KEY` set the link is *emailed to the victim* and the caller never sees it (a
phishing-assist and an audit-log entry, not a takeover); **without it the worker is in sandbox mode
and `sendLoginLink` returns `dev_link` in the response body — to the caller — which is
account takeover in one request.** Sandbox is `!env.BREVO_API_KEY` (`index.js:561-567`).
**Whether live has that secret is UNVERIFIED [INFERENCE]:** `wrangler.toml:29` calls it "optional;
auth runs in sandbox mode until added" and the setup guide still offers to walk through Brevo
signup, which suggests it is not set — but a Worker secret cannot be read from here, and probing
`/api/auth/request-link` would INSERT a junk `magic_links` row into production, so it was not
probed. **Settle this before the first staff hire, not after.**

**S-2b. `finance_export` is declared admin-only and enforced at staff.** `PERMISSIONS`
(`admin.js:37-41`) declares `finance_export: { admin: true, staff: false }`. `reports.js` gates
`revenue.csv` (:96), `revenue-all.csv` (:135), `sales` (:179) and `pos-sales` (:395) with
`requireStaff`. **The declared matrix and the enforced matrix disagree.** Note `revenue-all.csv` is
cross-org but *correctly* scoped — it joins `user_org_roles` on the bound `user_id` for
`role IN ('admin','staff')` (`reports.js:143-153`), so it never reaches an org the caller has no
role in. The finding is the role tier, not a leak.

**S-2c. `crm_export` is declared admin-only and enforced at staff.** Same shape: `PERMISSIONS`
declares `crm_export: staff=false`; `marketing.js:188` gates the whole module with `requireStaff` —
segments, campaign creation and send, and `segments/:id/preview`, which returns a **sample of up to
ten real contacts**.

**S-2d. `addUser` and `setRole` validate the request body BEFORE they gate** (`admin.js:113-118`,
`:134-137`). An anonymous POST with a malformed email or an invalid role gets **400, not 401** — so
the endpoint confirms its own existence and its input vocabulary to a caller who has not signed in.
No org data and no PII; same low severity and same class as S-1a. **v0.102.0's ratchet is right to
permit it** — `addUser` genuinely must parse the body to learn which org to gate against — but the
session check (`401`) could run first, before the parse, without touching that constraint. **The
authorization matrix's own probes had to send valid bodies to reach the gate at all**, which is how
this surfaced.

**THE STRUCTURAL FINDING, and the one worth fixing regardless of the policy answers: `PERMISSIONS`
IS A DECLARATION NOTHING ENFORCED.** `admin.js:36` introduces it as the matrix that makes
permissions "explicit, not folklore", and the UI renders it as authoritative — while **nothing
connected any entry in it to any gate.** It could drift arbitrarily far from what the code does and
no test would notice. **That is the same failure class as S-1a: something that looks authoritative
and is wired to nothing.** v0.103.0 closes it for the one capability whose route mapping is
unambiguous — `manage_users` — by asserting both the declaration AND that staff is behaviourally
refused on all four user-management routes. The other capability names map onto routes only through
a judgement the owner has not made, which is exactly S-2b and S-2c.

**The track — remaining, in priority order:**

1. ~~**Close S-1a, then build the `admin_route_gating.test.mjs` ratchet.**~~ **DONE v0.102.0** — see
   the S-1a block above. 102 dispatch sites, all gated, enforced on every run.
2. ~~**Authorization matrix by role.**~~ **DONE v0.103.0** — `worker/test/authorization_matrix.test.mjs`,
   14 tests. See the S-2 block below for what it measured and the four findings it produced.
3. ~~**Cross-org isolation sweep.**~~ **DONE v0.104.0** — `worker/test/cross_org_isolation.test.mjs`,
   8 tests; D-8 and D-11 both closed. **See the S-3 block below — the premise was wrong in a way
   that matters, and the owner's "universals" turned out to be an unbuilt feature with a safety
   cost, not an exemption to honour.** The original wording is kept below for the record.

   ~~**NEXT UNIT, AND ITS PREMISE CHANGED ON 2026-08-08.** It was~~
   "`org_id` on every read AND write, no exceptions". **The owner has now said that is wrong**
   (answer to O-F3, handoff §4): *"the subfinder can be universal amongst all the orgs, player
   library, inbox, etc — leagues and tournaments can be nestled per org."* So the invariant is no
   longer universal; it is **"org-scoped by default, with a NAMED and JUSTIFIED cross-org set."**
   **This makes the unit harder, not easier, and it must not be started by grepping for missing
   `org_id`** — that would flag the deliberate exceptions as defects and, far worse, would let a
   genuinely leaky read hide inside "oh, that one is meant to be universal". **The first task is to
   turn the owner's sentence into an explicit per-module list**, because "etc" is not a
   specification: sub-finder (`subs.js`), player library (`library.js`?), inbox (`messages.js` /
   `member-inbox`) are named; leagues and tournaments are explicitly per-org; **everything else is
   unstated and must be asked or defaulted to org-scoped.** `reports.js revenueAllCsv` is already a
   deliberate, correctly-implemented cross-org read and is the worked example of what an approved
   exception looks like — scoped to the caller's own `user_org_roles`, no client-supplied org id.
   §-1c **D-8** (`refreshStandings` reads without an org filter) is a known live instance and is
   NOT one of the owner's exceptions.
4. **Injection surface.** D1 is parameter-bound throughout, which is the right default; the check is
   for any string-built SQL. Also the HTML sink review — `innerHTML` with interpolated server data
   (`site-nav.js`'s v2.14 comment records one latent case already fixed by moving to DOM APIs).
5. **Session/auth review.** Cookie flags (`HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=None`), token hashing (SHA-256
   at `index.js:671-685`), expiry/revocation, and the magic-link flow.
6. **Client-side trust review.** Every place the UI decides something the server should decide —
   N-1's `role` fallback is one; find the rest.
7. **Public/unauthenticated surface.** The routes deliberately outside `buildCtx` —
   `/api/public/org-brand`, `/api/calendar/*.ics`, `/api/signup`, `/api/unsubscribe`,
   `/api/webhooks/square` — each is a deliberate hole and each needs its own justification re-read.
8. **Dependency and secret hygiene.** No committed credentials; token scopes minimal.

### S-3 — cross-org isolation, v0.104.0. **THE PREMISE WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.**

**S-3a. THE OWNER'S "UNIVERSALS" DO NOT EXIST YET — they are an unbuilt feature, not an exemption,
and building them literally would cost member privacy and defeat a safety feature.** The unit was
scoped as "org-scoped by default, with a named cross-org set". Reading the code first — before
writing any check — showed **all three named modules are rigorously org-scoped TODAY, by deliberate
design:**
- **Player library** = `librarySearch` (`messages.js:155-186`): `WHERE p.org_id = ?` bound to
  `ctx.orgId`, **plus a privacy gate** (`tierClause(signedIn, staff)` over
  `member_profiles.visibility`), **plus block lists** (`member_blocks WHERE org_id=?`).
- **Inbox** = the same module (`/api/messages/*`).
- **Sub finder** = `subs.js`, whose own header documents `GET /api/subs/requests → open requests,
  org-scoped`; `sub_requests WHERE r.org_id=?1`, `sub_signups WHERE s.org_id=?1`.

**Two consequences the owner cannot be assumed to have intended, and neither is a scoping detail:**
1. **Profile visibility was chosen inside one org.** A member set `visibility` knowing who could
   see it. Making the library universal republishes those profiles to every other org's members —
   people they never opted into being visible to.
2. **`member_blocks` IS ORG-SCOPED, so blocks would silently stop working.** Block someone in org 1
   and the row is written `WHERE org_id=1`. Go universal without re-scoping blocks and **the person
   you blocked reappears in the library and can message you from org 2.** That is a safety
   regression wearing the costume of a scoping change. `subs.js notifyMatches` compounds it — it
   **emails** matching signups, so universal means cross-org email fan-out.

**Recorded as a QUESTION (§5), not built.** The owner said this in an aside inside an answer about
login branding; it is a substantial feature with a privacy blast radius and it deserves its own
decision. **No universal behaviour was implemented in v0.104.0.**

**S-3b. THE SQL SCAN WAS WRITTEN FIRST AND THROWN AWAY — the numbers are the lesson.** The obvious
unit is "every statement touching an org-scoped table carries an `org_id` predicate". Built and run,
it accused **139 of 542** statements. Reading them showed the overwhelming majority are correct:
`UPDATE contacts SET … WHERE id=?` is safe **because the id was already validated against
`ctx.orgId` one statement earlier, in another function** (`admin.js:197-198` is the canonical
pattern — fetch, compare `row.org_id !== ctx.orgId`, 404). A 139-entry ratchet is a wall, not a
guard, and the one real defect would be invisible inside it. **Ask what unit the check counts, then
ask what a single real defect does to that number.** Here the answer was "nothing distinguishable",
so the check was discarded rather than pinned.

**What shipped instead is REACHABILITY, asserted through the real router:** can a caller holding a
role only in org 2 read or mutate org 1's rows by id? **Five routes across five modules and five
tables were probed and all five are correctly isolated.** Every probe carries a positive control
against the attacker's own org, because a 404 from isolation and a 404 from a mistyped URL are
byte-identical. Mutating probes re-read the victim row from D1 and assert it is byte-unchanged — a
handler can answer 200 while writing to another org, and the response alone cannot show it.

**NC-X3 IS THE TEST THAT EARNED THE GREEN, and it failed on its first run.** Every other assertion
in the file expects an EMPTY list, and a detector that can never produce a non-empty list satisfies
all of them forever while testing nothing. NC-X3 grants a legitimate role and demands the detector
report **both** failure kinds. It reported mutations on all four mutating probes and **no data leak
at all** — because the one READ probe pointed at an event with no divisions seeded, so
`{divisions: []}` came back and **that probe would have passed against a handler that ignored
`org_id` entirely.** Fixed by seeding a division named so a leak is visible. **An empty response
cannot distinguish "isolated" from "nothing there".**

**SCOPE, STATED BECAUSE THIS REPORTS CLEAN: there are 80 id-taking `/api/admin/*` routes and this
probes FIVE.** The other 75 are not cleared. Widening is one row per route in the `PROBES` table.

**Closed in this release:** **D-8** (`refreshStandings` read `matches`/`teams` by `event_id` alone
while the write beneath pinned `org_id`; `orgId` was already a parameter, so the fix was free) and
**D-11** (the played segment filter's `events` JOIN now pins `e.org_id = ?1`, reusing the numbered
parameter so it consumes no bind). **The D-8 test was watched failing against the unfixed source
before the fix was restored.**

**Rules for this track.** Testing is against **the code and a local shim**, never by attacking live
production — the owner's data is real. Anything needing live confirmation is written down and the
owner is asked. **No finding is reported without the file:line that proves it**, and a scan that
reports clean states what it covered and what it did not.

## §-1f. SIGN-IN & MEMBER-MENU PROGRAM (added v1.6, 2026-08-08 — owner brief)

**Owner, verbatim:** *"fix login page to be single login - admins will login wiht memberes with
admin/member tab so they can operate as either with 1 button after login. please increase security
of login with appropriate system increase such as password strength but do not enforce if it is
weak. Add branding around the login page that fits. Ensure the membership pages menus seemto shift
and reload every interaction, this needs to be fixed. REview the order of the menu buttons against
best practices and common sorting/ordering. Suggest grouping for the menu page for members."*

**READ THE PREMISES BEFORE BUILDING ANY OF IT.** Recon was run against the code before this section
was written, and **three of the six asks are already built, and one cannot be built as described.**
Recording the brief verbatim above and the corrections below, because the gap between the two is the
useful part.

### F-1. Single login with a role switch — **HALF ALREADY BUILT, and the built half does nothing**

**The login is already single.** There is no second login page. `web/index.html` → `app.js`'s
`renderLogin()` is the only sign-in surface; `admin.html` and the member pages have none.

**It already has the tabs the owner asked for** — `app.js:94-97` renders a `role="tablist"` with
`Member` and `Manager` buttons. **They are cosmetic.** `pickRole()` (`app.js:110-122`) writes
`localStorage.bt_login_role`, toggles two classes and `aria-selected`, and swaps one hint sentence.
`submit()` (`app.js:128-147`) posts `{ email }` to `/api/auth/request-link` **and never reads the
role.** The chosen tab has zero effect on authentication, on the session, or on where you land.
**This is almost certainly what the owner is reporting** — a control that looks like it does
something and does not.

**The thing actually asked for does not exist:** *"operate as either with 1 button AFTER login."*
There is no post-login role toggle. `renderDashboard`/`paint()` (`app.js:169-194`) derives
`role = roleByOrg[orgId]` from `/api/me` and computes `staff = role === "admin" || role === "staff"`,
which decides the card set. An admin cannot choose to view as a member.

**SEQUENCED AFTER §-1e PRIORITY 2 — NOT NEGOTIABLE, AND NOT A SCHEDULING PREFERENCE.** "Operate as a
member" is a §-1e **item 6** client-side-trust question, and the authorization matrix is its input.
The design fork it turns on: is the switch a **UI filter** (server still sees admin, and an "acting
as member" admin retains every admin privilege — cosmetic, cheap, and a lie the UI tells) or a **real
privilege drop** (the session carries an acting-role the server enforces — honest, and a
session/authorization change that the matrix must define first)? **Building either before the matrix
exists means guessing which one the gates already assume.** Owner question O-F1 below.

### F-2. "Password strength" — **CANNOT BE BUILT AS DESCRIBED. OWNER QUESTION, NOT A DEFECT.**

**There are no passwords in this system.** Authentication is (i) a one-time emailed magic link
(`/api/auth/request-link` → `/api/auth/verify`) and (ii) WebAuthn passkeys (`worker/src/webauthn.js`,
`web/assets/passkey.js`). `web/assets/settings.js:79` says it to the user verbatim: *"There are no
passwords here. A passkey (Face ID / fingerprint) is both your password and your second factor."*

**A password-strength meter requires first ADDING passwords, and that is a security regression, not
an increase.** Passkeys and magic links are phishing-resistant, have no shared secret to reuse, and
leak nothing in a breach. Adding a password would introduce all three weaknesses in order to display
a meter measuring the weakness just introduced. **Do not build this. Ask O-F2.**

**What a genuine "increase security of login" looks like on THIS stack** — all verifiable, none
requiring a password, and none to be built unasked: magic-link rate limiting and per-token
single-use/expiry review (`index.js:671-685` hashes tokens SHA-256 — confirm expiry and revocation);
passkey enrolment prompted rather than buried in Settings; step-up (require passkey, not link) for
admin-role actions; session cookie flags and idle expiry; sign-in notification email; the
`admin-security.html` rescue-link path already logs every rescue — confirm it is rate-limited.
**This list is a menu for the owner, not a queue. §-1e item 5 is the track that owns it.**

### ~~F-3. Login-page branding~~ — **SHIPPED v0.106.0. THIS ROW SAT OPEN FOR THIRTY-TWO RELEASES AFTER IT WAS BUILT** (struck 2026-08-18, iteration 109, found by resolving RF-8's citations by content). `handoff:74` records it: *"v1.35/v0.106.0: per-org login branding built by copying `applyOrgBrand`… S-1b CLOSED"* — and `app.js:157-159` builds the lockup today. **The residue is real and belongs to RF-8:** the lockup is CONDITIONAL on `loginOrgHint()` returning a value, so an arrival with no `?org` hint still gets the bare card this row described. The text below is the original measurement, kept for that residue

The login card (`app.js:90-106`) is an unbranded `<h1>Sign in</h1>` on a bare card. The member nav by
contrast already carries the logo and wordmark (`site-nav.js:273-274`,
`assets/logo-boom-icon-512.png`). **Branding the sign-in surface per-org is what S-1b is about** —
`GET /api/orgs` is unauthenticated precisely because a sign-in surface may need org branding before
anyone has signed in. **F-3 and S-1b must be settled together or the answer to S-1b will be invented
by whoever builds F-3.** If login branding is Boomtown-only (one brand, not per-org), S-1b's
justification weakens and the route may be gateable. **Ask O-F3 and S-1b as one question.**

### F-4. Menu shift/reload on every interaction — **filed as a defect: §-1c D-15.** See that row.

### F-5. Menu order review — **REAL, UNBUILT, and cheap.** `site-nav.js:175` defines `NAV` as an
ordered array of groups. A review against convention (frequency-first, destructive/account last,
stable position across roles so muscle memory survives a role change) is a read plus a reordered
array. **It is deliberately NOT bundled with F-1** — reordering the rail while the role switch
changes what is IN the rail means neither change can be evaluated.

### F-6. Member menu grouping — **ALREADY PARTLY BUILT.** `site-nav.js` already pushes labelled
groups (`{ label: "You", items: [...] }` at :218, `{ label: "Account", items: [...] }` at :260).
**The ask is a review and a proposal, not a build.** Deliver it as a written proposal against the
existing groups; do not silently regroup a live nav.

**Owner questions — ALL THREE ANSWERED 2026-08-08. These are SETTLED; re-asking one is a defect.**

- **O-F1 — ANSWERED: a REAL PRIVILEGE DROP.** *"yes agreed drop privileges."* So "operating as a
  member" must be something the SERVER enforces, not a UI filter. **What that costs, established by
  v0.103.0's matrix rather than guessed:** both gates read the database at request time —
  `requireStaff`/`isStaff` (`index.js:331-343`) and `requireAdmin`/`isAdmin` (`admin.js:21-32`)
  each `SELECT … FROM user_org_roles WHERE user_id=?1 AND org_id=?2`. **Nothing anywhere reads a
  role carried on the session.** So an acting-role cannot be added at the UI and cannot be added to
  `ctx` alone — it has to live where those two queries can see it. The minimum honest shape is: a
  column on the session row (migration), `buildCtx` reading it, and **both** gate functions
  consulting it — because a drop that only `requireStaff` honours leaves all four `requireAdmin`
  routes open to a "member". **v0.103.0's ratchet is what will catch that half-implementation**, and
  the acting-role work must extend the matrix in the same commit.
- **O-F2 — ANSWERED: no passwords.** *"ok."* The platform stays passwordless (magic link +
  WebAuthn). **F-2 is STRUCK as a build item.** The five passwordless hardening options remain
  available but none were selected, so none are queued; §-1e item 5 owns them.
- **O-F3 — ANSWERED: PER-ORG branding.** *"Per org."*
  > **⚠ CORRECTION, 2026-08-08 (iteration 25). THE OWNER'S ANSWER STANDS; THE INFERENCE I DREW FROM
  > IT WAS WRONG, AND S-1b IS RE-OPENED.** I wrote that per-org branding "settles S-1b — `/api/orgs`
  > stays open", reasoning that a login screen must enumerate orgs before anyone signs in.
  > **`GET /api/public/org-brand?org=<id|slug>` has existed since v0.50.0**
  > (`announcements.js:94-106`): ONE org, by id or slug, returning exactly
  > `{org_id, display_name, logo_url}`, active-and-undeleted only, `Cache-Control: max-age=300`,
  > deliberately outside `buildCtx`. **That is the per-org login-branding endpoint, already built
  > and already narrow.** A login screen never needed `/api/orgs`.
  > So the remaining unauthenticated purpose of `/api/orgs` — which returns `id, name, slug,
  > logo_url, brand_json` for EVERY active org — is **enumeration for the admin org switcher, a
  > signed-in surface** (`header_shell.test.mjs:85-88`). **`/api/orgs` may well be gateable after
  > all.** Do not gate it on this paragraph: confirm the caller set first, in BOTH directions.
  > **The lesson is the one this track keeps paying for: a SETTLED owner answer does not make my
  > inference from it settled. Re-examining my own reasoning is not re-asking the owner.**

  The login surface must show org branding before anyone has signed in. Write it down as deliberate rather than
  leaving it as an unexplained hole — that was the whole point of asking. **It does NOT follow that
  the current payload is right:** `listOrgs` returns `id, name, slug, logo_url, brand_json` for
  every active org, and a login screen needs branding for the org being signed into, not an
  enumeration of all of them. **A narrower public projection is available and is a real
  improvement** — but it is a change to a route with a pinned caller
  (`header_shell.test.mjs:85-88`), so it belongs to F-3's build, not to a security sweep.
  **The owner also scoped the wider product in the same answer** — sub-finder, player library and
  inbox universal across orgs; leagues and tournaments nested per org — which **redefines §-1e
  priority 3** and is recorded there.

### ✅ F-5 / F-6 — the menu review, DELIVERED v0.106.0 as a PROPOSAL. **Nothing was regrouped.**

**What exists today** (`site-nav.js`), measured rather than remembered — **13 items in 2 groups** for
a signed-in member:
- **Explore** (7): Home · Schedule · Live scores · Leagues · Community Play · Player Library · Help & FAQ
- **You** (6): My Dashboard · Notifications · Inbox · My Profile · Membership · Settings
- Signed-out: Explore + **Account** (Sign in). Signing in APPENDS "You" below Explore, so nothing
  above it moves — **position is already stable across the signed-out/signed-in change**, which is
  the property F-5 most needed and it is already true.

**THE ONE FINDING THAT IS NOT A JUDGEMENT CALL — recorded as §-1c D-19.** *"Home"* (`index.html`)
and *"My Dashboard"* (`home.html`) are **both landing pages, both in the rail, and the names do not
distinguish them.** `index.html` signed-in renders a card grid (`app.js renderDashboard`);
`home.html`'s own `<title>` is literally "My Dashboard" and it opens "Welcome back". A member cannot
predict which one they are about to get. **This is an IA defect, not a preference** — but the fix is
a naming-or-merge decision that belongs with the frame work, so it is recorded, not chased.

**The proposed regrouping, against what exists — FOUR groups, frequency-first:**

| Group | Items | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **You** *(top when signed in)* | My Dashboard · Notifications · Inbox | Highest-frequency, and the only two badge-bearing items in the rail. They currently sit BELOW seven Explore items. |
| **Play** | Schedule · Leagues · Live scores · Community Play | The actual product. Leagues above Live scores: a league night is a weekly destination, live scores are an event-day one. |
| **Directory** | Player Library | Reference, not a task. Its own group so it stops reading as a play surface. |
| **Account** *(last)* | My Profile · Membership · Settings · Help & FAQ | Convention: account and destructive last. Help sits here rather than in Explore, where it currently competes with product surfaces. |

**AND THE RECOMMENDATION IS TO HOLD IT.** §-1d/§-1g C-2 restructures this rail into module frames
with in-screen tabs — the owner's own spec — and **a regroup applied now is work the frame decision
would throw away.** The two cheap, direction-independent items are worth taking whenever the rail is
next touched: **fix the Home/My Dashboard collision (D-19)**, and **move Help & FAQ out of Explore**.
Everything else waits for the frame.

**Priority order within §-1f**, now that nothing is blocked on an unasked question: **D-15 (defect,
standalone)** → **F-3 (per-org login branding; S-1b settled as deliberate)** → **F-5/F-6 (menu order
review and grouping proposal, no dependency)** → **F-1 (the real privilege drop — needs a migration
and extends the v0.103.0 matrix)**. **F-2 is struck.**

## §-1g. MEMBER-CHOSEN MULTI-ORG MEMBERSHIP + THE MODULE TAB FRAME (added v1.7, 2026-08-08 — owner concept)

**STATUS: DISCUSSION, NOT APPROVED, NOTHING BUILT.** The owner asked to *"plan and discuss"*. The
verified findings below are durable; the proposal is not a decision.

**Owner's words, verbatim:**
> "I like the idea of members choosing to add organizations to their active list. This would manage
> different organization from a platform perspective, but the members system is layered where
> members can add or remove (or have both) companies / org on that appear as options, and thus 1
> person would only need 1 member profile and have access to utilize data from any company. Some
> companies may only utilize certain portions of the features."
> "when doing the horizontal buttons that becomes a frame itself where it does not reload and acts
> as a tab with the vertical menu on the side, reducing the options on the left menu and keep items
> together that are only applicable to certain modules."

### THE RANKING FACT, read from live D1 on 2026-08-08 — **this is greenfield**

**4 active orgs (6 rows), 49 contacts ALL IN ORG 1, 49 distinct emails, ZERO people in two orgs,
1 user account, 1 member profile.** The multi-org membership problem **does not exist yet**. Nobody
has a duplicate identity to reconcile and no member has ever joined a second org. **That makes this
decision far cheaper NOW than at any later point**, and it means a migration would move almost no
rows. It also means nothing here is urgent in the "something is broken" sense.

### C-1 — Multi-org membership. **HALF OF IT IS ALREADY BUILT, and the half that is missing is the interesting half.**

**Already true today, verified in code:**
- **`users` is GLOBAL** — no `org_id` column. One person, one login, already.
- **`user_org_roles` is already the many-to-many join** (`user_id + org_id + role`), and the org
  switcher already filters `/api/orgs` through `/api/me` roles (`admin-nav.js`).
- So **"one person, many orgs" is the existing architecture for IDENTITY and ACCESS.**

**NOT true today, and this is the gap:**
- **`contacts` is PER-ORG** (`org_id NOT NULL`). The *member record* — name, phone, city, tags,
  opt-in — is one row **per org**. A person in two orgs is two contact rows.
- **`member_profiles` is PER-ORG too** (`librarySearch` pins `p.org_id`), so the *player card*
  (positions, skill level, height/reach, bio, visibility, avatar) is per-org as well.
- **There is NO member-initiated join or leave anywhere.** A contact is created by checkout
  (`registrations.js`), the signup widget, or an admin (`addUser`). **A member cannot add an org to
  their own list — the verb the owner is asking for does not exist.**
- **Two different resolvers disagree on how a user maps to a contact.**
  `index.js:243-252 contactForSession` joins `lower(u.email) = lower(c.email)` and uses
  `contacts.user_id` only for ordering; `messages.js:601-609 ownContact` matches
  `(user_id=?2 OR email=?3)`. **`contacts.user_id` exists but is not authoritative in either.**
  Any multi-org identity work must settle this FIRST — recorded as **§-1c D-18**.

**So the owner's ask decomposes into three things of very different size:**
1. **A member-initiated join/leave** (the missing verb). Small-to-medium, self-contained, and
   valuable on its own.
2. **Promoting the member PROFILE from per-org to global** (one player card, many orgs). This is
   the real architectural change, and it is **the same decision as the cross-org "universals"
   question already owed in §5** — a global player card IS a cross-org library. It carries the same
   two consequences: profile visibility chosen inside one org becomes visible everywhere, and
   **`member_blocks` is org-scoped, so blocking silently stops working across orgs.**
3. **Per-org feature subsets** (*"some companies may only utilize certain portions"*). This is an
   org-level feature-flag/entitlement model. **Nothing like it exists** — no `org_features` table,
   no capability column. It is also the natural substrate for C-2's menu reduction: a rail that
   hides what an org does not use.

**RECOMMENDED SEQUENCE, and the reason is dependency, not preference:** (1) settle the identity
resolver (D-18) → (2) the member-initiated join/leave against the EXISTING per-org contact model →
(3) per-org feature subsets → (4) the global profile, LAST, because it is the only step that is
hard to reverse and it needs the blocking model rebuilt in the same change. **Steps 1–3 deliver most
of what the owner described without touching member privacy.**

### C-2 — The module tab frame. **THIS IS §-1d, RESTATED — do not treat it as new.**

The owner specified it on 2026-08-06 in §-1d, verbatim: *"keep tournaments into a collapsable
section or its own menu along the top bar rather than down the total menu… Tournaments Manager on
the side menu, then the top tabs in the screen can be each function. League should be similar…
Menus need to optimzied and reviewed for brevity."*

**What is NEW in the 2026-08-08 phrasing is one clause: *"it does not reload"*.** That is the same
complaint as **§-1c D-15** (member menus shift and reload on every interaction). **The owner is
describing the defect and proposing the architecture that removes it in one sentence** — in-screen
tabs that swap a panel instead of navigating the page.

**C-2 IS STILL BLOCKED BY THE THREE §-1d QUESTIONS, and one of them decides the tab structure
itself:** whether a tournament inside a league is *tagged to the league and drawing its teams* or
*separate and merely linked* determines whether "Tournaments" is a tab INSIDE the League frame or a
peer frame beside it. **Designing the frame before that answer is designing the wrong frame.**

**Also note N-1c (already recorded):** the admin rail links out to member pages, swapping the whole
shell. A tab frame that does not reload makes that inconsistency more visible, not less.


## §-1h. INTERFACE, MOBILE & QOL REVIEW (added v1.9, 2026-08-09 — owner request)

Owner, 2026-08-08: *"Suggest additional features and QOL changes and upgrades. Do a review on
interface and modernize. Ensure usable on mobile."*

**MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — AND MY FIRST SCAN WAS WRONG.** An initial grep reported "9 pages with a
fixed-pixel grid and no mobile breakpoint," which would have been a serious finding. It was a false
positive from my own filter: those declarations are `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(140px, 1fr))`, which
collapses to a single column by construction and needs no breakpoint at all. Re-measured by
stripping `minmax(…)` before matching, only **three** pages carry a genuinely rigid sidebar
(`admin-brackets` 240px, `admin-documents` 240/260px, `admin-schedule-editor` 280px) **and all three
already collapse at a breakpoint.** The recorded lesson stands: *do not believe your own ad-hoc scan
when the real tooling disagrees.*

### M-0. The platform is in better mobile shape than expected — baseline

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Pages with a viewport meta | **59 of 59** |
| Pages with 44px minimum touch targets | 40 files |
| Pages sizing inputs at 16px (blocks iOS zoom-on-focus) | 22 |
| Rigid sidebars without a breakpoint | **0** |
| Deliberate horizontal scrollers (`admin-pos` heatmap, `admin-facility` court grid) | correct as built |

### M-1. THE ONE REAL MOBILE DEFECT: wide admin tables squash rather than scroll

**12 of the 14 pages containing a `<table>` have no horizontal-scroll wrapper.** `.tbl` is
`width: 100%` (`admin.css:59`) and `app.css:251` sets `body { overflow-x: hidden }` inside the phone
media query — with the honest comment *"Never let a phone-width layout force a sideways scroll."*
The intent is right; the interaction is not. A `width: 100%` table cannot overflow, so it **squashes**:
`admin-reports`' seven-column revenue table (Event · Type · Date · Regs · Card · Cash · Total) gets
roughly **55px per column on a 390px phone**, and the money columns are the ones that shrink.

Affected: `admin-announcements`, `admin-faq`, `admin-league`, `admin-member-fields`, `admin-passes`,
`admin-plans`, `admin-reports`, `admin-sms`, `admin-staff-pay`, `admin-waitlists`, `admin-waivers`,
`tournament`.

**THE FIX IS ALREADY IN THE REPO AND APPLIED TO EXACTLY ONE TABLE.** `app.css` has a phone
treatment for `.score-table` — `tr { display: block }`, each row becoming a stacked card. That is the
right pattern for narrow screens and it is written, tested on one surface, and not reused. This is
the fourth time a unit's answer was already in the codebase. **Preferred fix: extend the
`.score-table` card pattern to `.tbl` behind the existing phone breakpoint** — one shared rule, not
twelve page edits. Reach for an `overflow-x: auto` wrapper only where a table is genuinely a grid
(the POS heatmap already does this correctly).

### M-2. QOL and feature suggestions, ranked by value over effort

1. **A "what changed" line on the admin dashboard.** Every admin screen loads cold. Registrations
   since yesterday, unpaid balances, waivers expiring inside 30 days and the next event are all
   already queryable — `reports.js` computes most of them. One card, no new tables.
2. **Bulk actions on the registrations table.** Select-many then comp / cancel / remind. The
   single-row versions all exist; this is a checkbox column and a loop.
3. **Undo on destructive admin actions.** Everything soft-deletes (`deleted_at` is universal), so
   an undo is a five-second toast and an UPDATE. The capability is already paid for and unused.
4. **Save the draw settings per event.** The bracket screen's field size, points and slot minutes
   reset on every visit; `events.config_json` already exists to hold them.
5. **A scorer-facing phone view for the bracket.** The bench-and-tree layout is a desk design; on a
   phone at the scorer's table the useful screen is "this court, this game, two score boxes."
6. **Empty states that name the next action.** `orgEmptyState` exists and is used on a few screens;
   most still render an empty container.
7. **Finish the "Can't reach the server" reword (v0.116.0 started it).** `fetch` only throws at the
   network/CORS layer, which can be OUR fault as easily as the member's wifi — the v0.115.0 CORS
   outage read as "check your connection" for a day because the product said so. `admin-nav.js`'s
   copy now names both sides; **14 sibling copies** (app.js, home.js, register.js, kotc.js, score.js,
   lfg.js, settings.js, member-inbox.js, library.js, leagues.js, tournament.js, guardian-complete.js,
   admin-registrations.js, admin-uploads.js) still blame the user unconditionally. One mechanical
   sweep, same sentence family, per-surface tone kept (score/kotc say "signal", courtside).

### M-3. Modernisation — what NOT to do

The design system is coherent already: tokens, `.btn`, `.mf-*`, a real dark/light theme, pre-paint
theme and rail state, `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured, focus-visible rings. **The interface does
not need a restyle; it needs the existing system applied to the surfaces that predate it.** A
rewrite would discard the token work and the accessibility work and reintroduce the twenty-four
broken buttons C11 already paid for once.

### M-4. Member-landing motion pass (owner request 2026-08-09) — PLANNED, values decided

`home.html` ("My Dashboard") is the screen a member sees every visit: greeting `<h1>` + a card grid
(What's happening · Your results · Messages · My events/Your teams · sub-play CTA row) filled
async from `/api/home/feed`. **A daily screen earns restraint, not theatre** — every choice below
is frequency-scoped, and each animates only `transform`/`opacity` (compositor-only), rides the
existing `--ease-out` / `--dur-*` tokens, and collapses to opacity-only under
`prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Build, in this order (one release, ~1 file + guard):**
1. **Card entrance stagger, once per session.** Cards rise in with `opacity 0→1` +
   `translateY(8px)→0`, 220ms `--ease-out`, 40ms stagger between cards. Gate on a
   `sessionStorage` flag so returning via Back or a refresh inside the session paints instantly —
   the delight is for arrival, not for the tenth visit of the evening.
2. **Skeleton → content crossfade.** The async fill currently pops. 120ms opacity crossfade,
   nothing moves — content must land where the skeleton stood (skeletons already mirror layout).
   *(Measured FALSE at build, v0.162.0: the placeholders are `help-text` "Loading…" lines, not
   layout-mirroring skeletons. The fade shipped as first-fill-only opacity; no skeleton system
   was built — restraint per this block's own charter.)*
3. **Unread badge pop.** When the mail badge count CHANGES, scale `1→1.15→1`, ~160ms. State
   indication, not decoration; no animation when the count is unchanged on load.
4. **Announcement dismiss/mute collapse.** The list closes around a removed item —
   height+opacity, 200ms `--ease-out` — instead of the jump-cut reflow. (Height animation is the
   one deliberate exception to compositor-only; it is a rare, user-initiated event.)

**Explicitly NOT animated, and why:** page-to-page navigation (MPA; a nav transition tax on every
click), the theme toggle (fired often; already correctly instant), anything keyboard-initiated,
and no looping/ambient motion anywhere — a dashboard is glanced at, and looping motion competes
with reading. `.btn:active { scale(0.97) }` press feedback already exists and stays.

**Guard shape when built:** a region test asserting the stagger CSS exists AND is fenced by both
gates (`prefers-reduced-motion` and the session flag), with a negative control that strips the
reduced-motion fence and watches the guard fire.


## §-1i. SECURITY REVIEW & SUGGESTED FEATURES (added v1.10, 2026-08-09 — owner request)

Owner, 2026-08-09: *"suggest any security features."* Measured against the code, not recited.

### S-3.0 What is already strong — say it, so the gaps below are read in proportion

| Property | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **No passwords, ever** | Settled owner rule. There is no password store to breach, no reuse risk, no reset flow to attack. |
| Sign-in token entropy | `crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32))` — 256 bits (`index.js:792`). |
| Tokens hashed at rest | `magic_links.token_hash` and `sessions.token_hash`; the plaintext token is never stored. |
| Single-use links | `used_at` checked and set on verify (`index.js:611,614`). |
| Expiry on both | Magic links 15 min; sessions carry `expires_at` and are filtered on every read (`index.js:729`). |
| Sessions revocable | `revoked_at` honoured in the session lookup — the mechanism for "sign out everywhere" already exists. |
| No user enumeration | `requestLink` behaves identically for a known and an unknown address. |
| CORS | Allowlist echo from `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, no wildcard (`index.js:761-768`). |
| Org isolation | Measured behaviourally in v0.104.0; the three "universal" modules are rigorously org-scoped. |
| Real privilege drop | v0.107.0 — `sessions.acting_role`, honoured by BOTH gate predicates. |
| Audit trail | **182 `audit()` call sites** across the worker. |

### S-3a. **[CRITICAL, BUT CONDITIONAL] Sandbox login is FAIL-OPEN**

`sendLoginLink` (`index.js:580-598`):

```
if (env.BREVO_API_KEY) { …send the email…; return { mode: "email" } }
// SANDBOX mode — no email provider configured; return link for on-screen testing.
return json({ ok: true, mode: "sandbox", dev_link: link, … });
```

**If `BREVO_API_KEY` is not set on the live worker, `POST /api/auth/request-link` returns a valid
sign-in token for ANY email address to ANY anonymous caller.** Ask for a link to the owner's
address, read `dev_link` out of the JSON, sign in as the owner. That is total account takeover, and
it needs no vulnerability beyond asking politely.

**The shape is the defect even if the key is set.** A security property must not depend on an
unrelated configuration value being PRESENT — that is fail-open. Deleting or rotating one secret
silently converts the login endpoint into a credential dispenser, and nothing would alert.

**THE FIX:** require an explicit opt-in for sandbox mode and fail closed otherwise —
`if (env.SANDBOX_LOGIN === "1") return dev_link;` then `503 "Sign-in email is not configured."`
**Measured cost: 26 test files depend on `dev_link` and each builds its `env` inline (there is no
shared factory), so this is a real sweep, not a two-line edit.** It was deliberately NOT rushed into
v0.113.0: a login change is the last thing to ship in a hurry.

**THE OWNER CAN SETTLE THE URGENCY IN TEN SECONDS: do you receive sign-in emails?** If yes,
`BREVO_API_KEY` is set, nothing is exploitable today, and this is a scheduled hardening job. If no,
it is live and should be fixed before anything else in this document. *(This is §5's S-2a-bis, open
for several sessions — it is now the highest-value unanswered question in the project.)*

### S-3b. ~~[HIGH] No rate limit on `/api/auth/request-link`~~ **CLOSED — v0.117.0 (iteration 41)**

Was: `requestLink` validated the email format and INSERTed immediately — unauthenticated,
unbounded row growth, and (once the email key is set) unlimited mail to any third-party address.

Closed with `messages.js`'s own flood shape, per TARGET EMAIL: **5 links per 15-minute window**
(derived from `MAGIC_LINK_TTL_MIN`) + **20/day**, enforced **inside `sendLoginLink`** so
`rescueLink` and family invites are bounded through the same door. The 429 is byte-identical for
known and unknown addresses (no enumeration oracle) and the refusal writes nothing.
`auth_rate_limit.test.mjs` (5 tests) is the guard; each was seen firing against the unguarded
route first. **Bonus fix: `rescueLink` no longer reports `ok:true` around a failed
`sendLoginLink`** (flood 429 / Brevo 502) — success is only reported when a link exists.
**Residual, recorded not fixed:** an attacker rotating ADDRESSES still grows `magic_links` at
their pace (per-IP limiting needs a schema addition and a design call — CF-Connecting-IP is
available; file under S-3 follow-ups if wanted before go-live).

### S-3c. ~~[MEDIUM] No security headers on app or API responses~~ **CLOSED — v0.118.0 (iteration 42)**

Was: only `uploads.js` and `calendar.js` set any protective header. Absent everywhere else:
**Content-Security-Policy**, **X-Frame-Options**, **Referrer-Policy** (a magic-link URL leaking via
`Referer` is a token leak), **X-Content-Type-Options**, **Strict-Transport-Security**.

Closed at the fetch EGRESS — the choke point that already merges CORS — **not inside `json()`,
whose reach this entry overstated**: json() never sees the avatar bytes, the three CSV exports, the
ICS feeds, the SMS TwiML, or marketing's unsubscribe page — the only HTML the worker serves, and
the surface that needs CSP most. Applied SET-IF-ABSENT, so `uploads.js` keeps its sandboxed CSP and
marketing's page now owns a CSP that keeps its inline styles alive (`style-src 'unsafe-inline'`).
The dimension not touched is pinned in the same commit (the v0.115.0 rule): an out-of-allowlist
origin still receives zero allow-origin headers. `security_headers.test.mjs` (5 tests) is the
guard; each seen firing against the bare worker first, and the calendar-404 test fails a
json()-level implementation by construction. All five headers verified LIVE by read-only fetches —
including marketing's own CSP and the evil-origin probe (headers are readable without writes, so
this unit got the full live verification S-3b's 429 could not).
**Residual, recorded not fixed:** the static app's headers are set by GitHub Pages, not by us. A
page-side `<meta http-equiv>` CSP for `web/**` is the only page-side lever and is a SEPARATE
decision — meta CSP cannot carry `frame-ancestors`, so it would be a partial control — deliberately
not bolted on here.

### S-3d. **[LOW-MEDIUM] Already recorded, still open** — §-1e **S-2b/S-2c**: `PERMISSIONS` declares
`finance_export` and `crm_export` admin-only while `reports.js` revenue CSVs and all of
`marketing.js` enforce them at **staff**. Nothing is exploitable at 0 staff roles; it activates at
the first hire.

### S-3e. **[LOW] Audit gaps** — `kotc.js` and `member_portal.js` contain no `audit()` call. The
other modules without one (`courts`, `crypto`, `names`, `scheduler`, `resilience`, `board_suggest`)
are pure or leaf and correctly have none.

### S-3f. Security FEATURES worth building, ranked by value over effort

1. **"Sign out everywhere" + a session list.** `sessions.revoked_at` is already honoured on every
   request — the mechanism is built and unexposed. One admin screen and one UPDATE.
2. **A new-sign-in notification email.** The single best detector of a stolen link, and the email
   transport already exists.
3. **An audit log viewer.** 182 events are recorded and there is no way to read them in the product.
   A trail nobody can read is a trail that catches nothing.
4. **Re-authentication for destructive admin actions** (delete an org, export the CRM, wipe an
   event). Compatible with NO PASSWORDS EVER: re-send a magic link and require it inside 5 minutes.
5. **A member-facing "your data" page** — what is stored, export it, request deletion. Every table
   already soft-deletes, so honouring a deletion request is achievable rather than aspirational.
6. **Alerting on failed verifies and rate-limit trips**, once S-3b exists to trip.


### S-4. GO-LIVE AUTH — the owner's three questions, answered from the code (added 2026-08-09)

Owner: *"During build - use the current email system. For go live, propose security feature that
would be ideal. Currently, there is a 2fa. Can we set up a passkey or can we set it up where it
simply does OAuth?"* Audited by eight agents with adversarial verification; three of four headline
findings survived refutation, and the fourth was refuted because it had already been fixed mid-session.

**Q: "Can we set up a passkey?" — IT IS ALREADY BUILT, LIVE, AND UNUSED.**
`worker/src/webauthn.js` is 359 lines of real WebAuthn: six routes, mounted at `index.js:445`,
`crypto.subtle.verify` over `authenticatorData || SHA-256(clientDataJSON)` (`:148-158`), COSE→
WebCrypto key import for ES256 and RS256 (`:241-260`), RP-ID hash (`:145`), origin (`:134`),
single-use purpose-bound challenges (`:200-209`), and signature-counter clone detection (`:160`).
A successful assertion calls the SAME `issueSession` the magic link uses (`index.js:646`).
`web/assets/passkey.js` calls `navigator.credentials.create` and `.get`, and is loaded on the login
page, profile and settings. The live worker answers `/api/passkey/login-options` with 200 while a
fabricated sibling 404s. **Live D1: `webauthn_credentials` holds ZERO rows — nobody has enrolled one.**

**Q: "Currently, there is a 2fa" — NO, AND THAT BELIEF HAD A SPECIFIC CAUSE.** The admin Users
screen rendered a "2FA" column from `users.totp_enabled`, which is read in two places and **written
nowhere**. TOTP was planned and replaced by passkeys; the plan was dropped and the column outlived
it. **Fixed in v0.114.0** — the column now shows a real passkey count. Live D1 confirms: 0 users
with `totp_enabled`, 0 with a `totp_secret`.

**Q: "Or simply do OAuth?" — TECHNICALLY YES, AND IT IS THE WRONG BUILD FOR GO-LIVE.**
1. The frontend is served from a **PATH on `10xequity.github.io`**, not its own host, so a Google
   client would be registered against a shared origin. Workable, not clean.
2. **The deploy workflow carries NO secrets** — `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` invokes
   `cloudflare/wrangler-action@v4` with `apiToken:` and **no `secrets:` input**; `BREVO_API_KEY`
   appears in no workflow file. So `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` would have to be set by hand, and the
   pipeline the roadmap assumed exists does not.
3. **Account-linking is the real risk.** Matching an OAuth identity to an existing user by email is
   only safe if `email_verified === true` is required from the provider; without it, anyone can
   register a Google account claiming an address they do not control and inherit that account.
4. It adds a **third** auth path to maintain beside magic link and passkey, for no security gain
   over a passkey.

**RECOMMENDATION FOR GO-LIVE: passkey-first, magic link as the recovery path. Build no new auth.**
Both halves already exist. What is owed is hardening, in this order:

- **S-4a — ENFORCE USER VERIFICATION.** `webauthn.js:69` and `:119` request
  `userVerification: "preferred"`, and login checks only `parsed.userPresent` (`:146`).
  `parsed.userVerified` is computed at `:217` and **read by nothing** — repo-wide grep returns one
  hit, the assignment. So a bare touch with no Face ID or PIN is accepted, while `settings.js`'s own
  copy tells the member a passkey is "both your password and your second factor". **That promise is
  not enforced server-side.** Fix: `"required"` at both sites and reject `!userVerified` alongside
  the presence checks. **TRADE-OFF, AND IT IS AN OWNER CALL:** this locks out authenticators that
  cannot do UV, which is the likely reason "preferred" was chosen.
- **S-4b — CLOSE THE FAIL-OPEN, AND IT IS WIDER THAN S-3a SAID.** `security.js:111-125` `rescueLink`
  spreads `sendLoginLink`'s body into its own response, so in sandbox mode **any STAFF user can mint
  and read a sign-in link for any account, including an admin** — staff→admin escalation, no
  anonymity needed. **Patching the `/api/auth/request-link` route alone would leave this open: the
  fix must land inside `sendLoginLink` itself.** `security.js:118` also returns
  `404 "No account with that email"`, a user-enumeration oracle (staff-gated).
  **And the odds this is live went UP:** since no workflow ever set `BREVO_API_KEY`, it can only be
  present if someone ran `wrangler secret put` or used the dashboard by hand.
- ~~**S-4c — THE PASSKEY CRYPTO HAS NO TESTS.**~~ **CLOSED — iteration 43, test-only (no release).**
  Was: the hand-rolled CBOR decoder (`:283-323`), DER→raw ECDSA (`:263-279`) and byte-offset
  `authData` walk (`:213-229`) had zero coverage; "fails CLOSED" was an inference from reading.
  Now measured: `webauthn_crypto.test.mjs` — 9 tests, real WebCrypto P-256/RSA keys, an INDEPENDENT
  CBOR encoder and authenticator-shaped DER signatures, all through the real routes via
  worker.fetch. **NO DEFECT FOUND.** Pinned: byte-exact credential-id/COSE storage (an offset
  off-by-N lands there) · truncated CBOR refuses without enrolling · flipped signature, tampered
  clientDataJSON (only the signature can catch it) and flipped rpIdHash under a RE-SIGNED valid
  signature all refuse · counter clone detection both directions · the RS256 branch · and **login
  WITHOUT UV succeeds — the current contract pinned deliberately; S-4a's implementation must
  REWRITE that test, which makes the decision visible.** Vacuity proven on a scratchpad COPY with
  the verify and rpIdHash gates disabled: exactly the four guarding tests reddened, five stayed
  green; the copy never touched the tree (an in-tree sabotage was refused by the permission
  classifier — correctly).
- **S-4d — minor, verified:** counter clone-detection never fires on Apple passkeys (they report 0),
  honestly documented at `:22`; `consumeChallenge` (`:200-208`) is a non-atomic SELECT-then-UPDATE,
  a replay window that still requires a valid signature.

## §-1j. TESTER ROUND 2 (added v1.15, 2026-08-09 — the owner's 18-item report)

**The round the queue waited fourteen releases for.** Items keep the owner's numbering; he
numbered two items 9, split here as **T2-9a / T2-9b**.

**EVERY ITEM BELOW IS MEASURED** (iteration 45, 2026-08-09). Nineteen agents read a throwaway COPY
of the tree — never the tree itself, per D-21 — and every load-bearing verdict was re-checked by a
second agent told to refute the first. **20 findings: 11 CONFIRMED, 8 PARTLY-TRUE, 1
ALREADY-EXISTS.** Verdicts and `file:line` below are what the code shows, not what the report said.

**Measuring changed the plan, which is the whole reason for measuring:**
- **T2-18 is ALREADY-EXISTS** — the member rail has no Registrations item; it was deliberately
  removed in v2.15/v0.101.0. The screenshot's "Registrations" is the **admin-only, staff-gated**
  card on `index.html`.
- **T2-5 is a REWIRE, not a build** — a complete modern bracket engine already exists at
  `/api/admin/events/:id/brackets`; the owner's button posts to a **legacy** route that only ever
  writes round one.
- **T2-13 has a found axis and is roughly a one-file fix** — `leagues.js` is the ONLY member
  surface that never attaches the auth token, and its own comment claims otherwise.
- **T2-14 is mostly EMPTY mistaken for BROKEN** — the empty states are honest and correct; the
  seeder simply writes zero rows to those five tables.
- **T2-9b's KOTC session creation already exists end to end**; only a league-first entry is missing.
- **T2-1's "scroll instead of shrink" is already built** — the real bug is a **dangling class**,
  the same family as v0.116.0's `bt-back`.
- **The most consequential finding is a GUARD BLIND SPOT** (T2-11): `app.js` — the file that paints
  the screenshot — predates the v2.15 rail cleanup and is **the surviving instance of admin
  destinations on a member surface**, and the guard that forbids exactly that reads only
  `site-nav.js`. A passing check whose subject moved is not a check.

### FIXED this session — v0.119.0 (iteration 44)

- **T2-6 — "brackets sheet still broken, cannot get past who plays here pop up, cannot be
  closed."** **FIXED, and it was a whole defect CLASS.** The dialog is hidden with the `hidden`
  attribute while `.br-pick` carried `display:grid; position:fixed; inset:0` — author CSS beats
  the UA's `[hidden]{display:none}`, so the overlay painted from PAGE LOAD and every close path
  (Close button, backdrop, Escape — all correctly built back in E1) was defeated on screen while
  "succeeding" in JS. `hidden_overlay.test.mjs` found the same defect on **ELEVEN elements across
  NINE pages**, all fixed: brackets `.br-pick`, calendar `.reveal`, documents `.dc-gate`,
  `.faq-form`, `.mf-form` ×3 (member-fields, passes, staff-pay), member-home `.feed-prefs` +
  `.cta-tile` + `.sub-strip`, and Community Play `.lfg-form`. Second half: the chooser's list
  buttons also carried the overlay class, each becoming its own full-screen layer inside the
  dialog — removed, uniqueness pinned.
  **This ANSWERS N-2** ("the screen with the pop up") — N-2 is closed.
  **It also changes the triage of T2-11, T2-13 and T2-14**: three home.html elements and the
  Community Play form were painting while hidden, so part of what the owner saw on those screens
  may already be gone. **Retest those three on v0.119.0 before building anything for them.**

### MEASURED — ~~the next unit~~ **CLOSED — v0.120.0 (iteration 46)**

- ~~**T2-13**~~ **FIXED v0.120.0** — token attached, false comment rewritten, honest two-truth 401
  card, `token_convention.test.mjs` corpus rule. ~~**T2-14**~~ **FIXED v0.120.0** — five tables

  **Residual, named:** the server's "Sign in first." for a signed-in-but-contactless session is

- **T2-13 — CONFIRMED, axis found: `leagues.js` never sends the token.** `api()` at

  `app.js:35/46`, `profile.js:36/42`, `site-nav.js:82`, `settings.js:23/37`,

  Bearer). **Partial fix warning:** subs/lfg routes still 401 whenever `contactForSession`

- **T2-14 — PARTLY-TRUE: mostly EMPTY mistaken for BROKEN, plus T2-13 on top.** Every empty state

  → *"Nothing posted here yet."* (`lfg.js:84-86`); `/api/library/search` needs **no auth**

### MEASURED — small units, one session each

- **T2-3 — CONFIRMED, and it is a display change with a ready helper.** The scheduler feed selects

  queries `teams` without the `t` alias those constants expect, so add the alias.** Staff surfaces

  `names.test.mjs:66-78` pins that omitting `full:true` abbreviates — which is correct for
  no-login surfaces and wrong here.
- **T2-4b — CONFIRMED: the cap is ONE client line.** `web/assets/tournament.js:268`
  `Math.min(m.points_to, 15)` renders the `.diff-chip` buttons (`:278`) posting winner+diff to
  `/api/matches/:id/score` (`:287`). **The server has no 15 cap** — winner+diff needs only
  `diff>=1` (`tournaments.js:274-277`), and the exact-score form already accepts integers 0-200
  and refuses ties (`:262-272`). The league dialog is the target shape and already exists: chips
  1-5 **plus a numeric box `max=points_to`** (`admin-league.js:201-202`, submit `:219-222`).
  **Build:** raising the client cap reddens nothing (no test exercises client JS), but the server
  contract is heavily pinned — `brackets.test.mjs:544` (exact-form correction), `:558` (tie
  rejection), `:571` (0-200/integer), `:577` (winner+diff), `:590-59x`.
- **T2-2 — "email schedule to players registered in the league."** Transport exists and is
  Brevo-gated. **The sandbox honesty rule is load-bearing:** with no mail key it must SAY nothing
  was sent, never report success — the exact v0.117.0 `rescueLink` trap in a new costume.
- ~~**T2-8**~~ — **CLOSED v0.125.0 (iteration 51).** All four sub-claims plus the owner's new
  "team numbers … to double check" rider. The bench is bounded (`max-height` + `overflow-y` on the
  LIST, which bounds the section that holds it) and collapsible; `#pbSplit` carries a `bottom`/`side`
  orientation with the bench as a grid TRACK in side view, falling back to the strip under 900px;
  `teams.level` now reaches the board; sorting groups by name/level/captain/seed as a VIEW change
  that never marks the board unsaved. **The team number was the interesting part, because every
  obvious source for it was wrong:** `seed` is NULL for every registered team (only the sandbox
  seeder writes one — a `#seed` badge would be blank on every real board), `board_order` is
  rewritten to a within-pool index on every save, and `id` is global. So `board_no` is rank by `id`
  within the event, with soft-deleted teams COUNTED so a withdrawal leaves a gap rather than
  renumbering everyone below it. Guard: `pool_board_bench.test.mjs` (19 tests, 14-of-16 seen failing
  pre-fix). **Residual, deliberately not built:** sorting by level GROUPS by the registered label and
  does not RANK it — the labels are free text (`BB/A`, `A/AA`, `Open`) with no stored ordering, and
  inventing one would be a skill ranking nobody agreed to. A real ordering needs a level vocabulary
  from the owner, which is the same missing decision as the tag vocabulary in the handoff §5.
  The original measurement, kept because its line numbers are the record of what was found:
  **T2-8 — CONFIRMED on every sub-claim.** `.pb-workspace` is `position:sticky; bottom:0` with
  **no max-height, no overflow, no collapse** (`admin-pool-board.html:76-78`) and its list wraps
  unbounded (`:79`); the code's own comment admits the screen-eating hazard and only disables
  sticky under 700px (`:110-115`) — **so it is a desktop/tablet defect and phones are already
  fine.** No shared stylesheet rescues it: zero `pb-*` rules in tokens/app/admin.css, the page's

- **T2-15 — CONFIRMED, and the values already exist — do not invent them.** `tokens.css` defines

### Bigger units — each needs its own session and a spec moment

- **T2-1 — MEASURED, and it splits into five sub-claims with very different prices.**
  - **T2-1a undo/redo — CONFIRMED, but cheaper than it looks.** No undo exists anywhere (state is
    three variables, `admin-schedule-editor.js:24-27`; every move POSTs and overwrites `data`,
    `:103-107`; Escape only cancels a pick-up, `:140-144`; Reload refetches, it does not revert,
    `admin-schedule-editor.html:156`). Routes are GET schedule / POST move / POST teams only
    (`formats.js:778, 790, 836`). **But the move endpoint is SELF-INVERSE** (moving back undoes a
    move; repeating a swap undoes a swap) **and the prior position is already persisted per move**
    in the audit row (`formats.js:827-828`, from/to/swapped) — **so a LIFO undo/redo stack is
    buildable client-side against the existing endpoint with ZERO new routes.** **The open
    decision:** this roadmap's own words were "undo AND redo across a change history, **then
    save**", which implies DEFERRED persistence — a much bigger change than reversal on a
    save-every-move design. **Settle that with the owner before building.**
  - **T2-1b move teams between schedules — CONFIRMED, and it is the expensive one.** No route
    moves a team across events; both write paths validate same-event and refuse otherwise
    (`formats.js:802-805` 404, `:853-857` 400, and a third at `brackets.js:945-948`). **Hazard:**
    nothing today touches `teams.event_id`, and that team's matches, standings, registrations and
    `score_token` all assume the original event — a bare `event_id` UPDATE orphans all of them.
    `schedule_editor.test.mjs:153-164` and `:166-189` pin both refusals.
  - **T2-1c colour coding — CONFIRMED, with prior art.** `.ed-match` is theme tokens only
    (`admin-schedule-editor.html:35-37`); the sole data-driven styling is the played border
    (`:41`). **Reuse the facility-calendar pattern** — hex from D1 passed as an inline custom
    property, `admin-facility.js:120` `style="--op:${opColor[b.org_id]}"` with legend dots at
    `:26` — the product's only data-driven colouring, and it evades the tokens-only tests.
  - **T2-1d "≥4 nets shrink until unreadable" — PARTLY-TRUE, and the real bug is a DANGLING
    CLASS.** Horizontal scrolling **already works**: a real table with `min-width:150px` slots
    (`admin-schedule-editor.html:33`, 128px under 430px `:59`) inside
    `.ed-grid-scroll { overflow-x:auto }` (`:28`, mounted `:163`); no `table-layout:fixed`
    anywhere in `web/`. **The high-leverage fix is one rename and the CSS is already waiting for
    it:** the aside carries `ed-side` (`:167`) while the selector at `:47` already names
    `.ed-side-panel`. **This is v0.116.0's `bt-back` family — a class in markup with no matching
    rule — which `hidden_overlay.test.mjs`'s sibling guard should generalise to catch.**
  - **T2-1e click-to-swap — PARTLY-TRUE, and the owner is right for a reason worth recording.**
    A keyboard path already exists (Enter picks up, one arrow moves-and-drops, Escape cancels,
    `:124-156`), but **there is no click-to-swap** — a mouse click only focuses. The file's own
    header concedes HTML5 dnd "is unusable with a keyboard and awkward on touch" (`:12-13`), and
    **on a touchscreen HTML5 dnd does not fire at all** — so "drag and drop fails" is true **by
    construction on the tablet at the gym.** **Build:** click-to-swap is a small extension of the
    existing `carrying` state machine reusing the shared `move()` (`:93`). Keep
    `schedule_editor.test.mjs:222-237` (Enter/Escape/all four arrows pinned as code) green.
- **T2-4 — CONFIRMED, with the option count MEASURED by running the code.**
  `GET /api/admin/formats/options` (`formats.js:629-643`) returns `equalGameOptions()`
  (`formats.js:48-58`) **with no floor or ceiling**, and the Plan-the-day screen renders every one

- ~~**T2-5**~~ **FULLY CLOSED.** Rewire in v0.121.0 (button → modern engine, `a_size`, `?event=`

- **T2-5 — CONFIRMED, and it is a REWIRE, not a build.** The button
  (`tournament.html:161` → `tournament.js:305-310`) POSTs `/api/events/:id/bracket`.
  `bracketRoutes` runs first (`index.js:470, 484`) **but matches only `/api/admin/events/...`**
  (`brackets.js:831-1086`), so the button falls through to the **LEGACY** `createBracket`
  (`tournaments.js:40-41 → 338-371`), which writes **only first-round games** (`scheduler.js:222`,
  loop `i < size/2`) and skips byes. **Meanwhile the full modern engine already exists** —
  `POST /api/admin/events/:id/brackets` plus GET / preview / advance / slot / forfeit, all built
  (`brackets.js:825-1126`). **Build:** repoint `tournament.js:305-310` at the modern route (or link
  to `admin-brackets.html`); **no test reddens on the rewire** (nothing tests the legacy HTTP
  route), while `scheduler.test.mjs:63-77` pins `buildBracket`/`seedSingleElim`. Cross-pool seeding
  (#1A v #4B; 3 pools → #1A–#4C, #1B–#4A) is then a seeding-order change inside the modern engine.
  Guard: property over 2–4 pools — no round-one pair shares a pool, and every breaking team lands
  in exactly one slot (a total mapping, so nobody is silently dropped).
- **T2-17 — USAV points library.** Participation-scaled season points for Open/AA: participants
  ×10 to the winner, stepping down 10 per place, tallied across the season.
  **NEEDS-INPUT — BLOCKED:** the USAV sign-up form and the points document are in the owner's
  Google Drive, which this loop cannot read. They must land in `docs/` (or be pasted) first.
  Building from the paraphrase above would encode a guess as a scoring rule.

### Programs — spec with the owner before any code; these reshape navigation

- **T2-9a — THE TOURNAMENT LIFECYCLE.** The owner's spine: registration builds the team list →
  teams separate by the DIVISION they registered in → if a division exceeds ~8/10/12 (**explicitly
  "not a fixed rule"** — operator judgement) sort into pools with assigned courts → the scheduler
  generates the schedule and score-entry links → email/print to that event's participants →
  scores go in → **the bracket populates automatically** unless something strange needs an edit →
  the bracket plays out. **Stages render HORIZONTALLY, not as standalone vertical pages.** The
  same spine serves leagues, including "create a league tournament". **Overlaps §-1b's W-units and
  §-1d's frame questions — reconcile there, do not write a third description of the same flow.**
  **MEASURED — the program is far more WIRING than building, and its parts are healthier than the
  report implies:**
  - **T2-7 divisions — PARTLY-TRUE, the page is NOT dead.** It is reachable from the rail on every
    admin page and calls five live, mounted routes (`admin-divisions.js:169, 214, 227, 240, 253` →
    `divisions.js:304-450`, mounted `index.js:471`), and its output has real consumers — bracket
    generation honours division court ranges (`brackets.js:464-469, 785-792`). **The actual gap is
    upstream:** `formats.js` generate-schedule does not group by division/pool, so the fix lands
    there (group teams by `pool_id`/`division_id`, run `planBestPool` per group), **not on the
    divisions page**. `divisions_page.test.mjs:33` pins that the page calls every division route.
  - **T2-10 score links — CONFIRMED.** `admin-score-links.js` has **zero** URL-param handling and
    defaults to the first event (`:79-89`, `eventId = list[0].id` at `:86`); **no file anywhere
    links to it with a query string** (repo-wide grep: zero hits) — every entry is the context-free
    rail item. **Build hazard:** `divisions_page.test.mjs` pins exact strings inside that file
    (`window.BTQR.download`, `"Save image"`, the copy-link error sentence, and the
    `` `scoring-${b.dataset.team}` `` literal) — keep them verbatim.
  - **T2-16 — PARTLY-TRUE, and three of its five asks ALREADY EXIST.** Check-in and waitlist are
    standalone pages not linked from the flow (`admin-checkin.html:133-134`,
    `admin-waitlists.html:124-135`; `tournament.html:102` links only Registrations) — **but
    `admin-waitlists.js:20-21` already honours a `?event=` deep link that nothing emits.** **Team
    edit + roster updates already exist staff-side** (`registrations.js:129-133`, all
    `requireStaff`-gated at `:545, 566, 590, 608, 632`) with a shared modal `BT_ROSTER.open(teamId)`
    (`team-roster.js:43-99`) **wired only into `admin-event.html` and `admin-league.html`** — its
    own header says it is meant to be called "from wherever a team appears". **Division moves
    already exist** with an audit trail (`divisions.js:394-424, 429-447, 450-501`). **What genuinely
    does not exist: bulk team-field edit, and MEMBER self-service** — every team/roster write is
    `requireStaff`; the member surface is read-only (`registrations.js:1067-1094`). Note
    `patchTeam` already accepts `level` (`:579`) but no UI sends it (`team-roster.js:77` sends name
    only). **Build rule:** member self-service must be NEW `/api/profile/*` or captain-token routes,
    **never a loosened admin gate** — `pool_board.test.mjs:253-267` pins members at 403, and
    `team_roster.test.mjs:72-129` pins soft-delete, cross-org 404 and nameless-rename refusal.
    Captain identity already exists as `teams.captain_contact_id`, gated exactly this way in
    `inviteTeammate` (`registrations.js:1108-1112`) — reuse that.
  - **T2-18 — ALREADY-EXISTS.** The member rail contains no Register/Registration item; the v2.15
    comment (`site-nav.js:230-238`) records the deliberate removal of the "Manage" group.
    `register.html` is reached only from event surfaces (`schedule.js:69, :87`; `leagues.js:81`).
    The screenshot's "Registrations" is the **staff-gated admin card** (`app.js:244`).
    **Do NOT "restore" a Registrations link to the member rail** — `header_actions.test.mjs:263-278`
    pins that the member sidebar offers no admin destination, with mutation NCs.
- **T2-9b — PARTLY-TRUE: KOTC/QOTC session creation ALREADY EXISTS end to end.**
  `POST /api/admin/events/:id/kotc` writes `kotc_sessions` (`kotcplay.js:343-361`) and a "New King
  of the Court session" modal ships on the event screen (`admin-event.js:213-236`, v0.90.0),
  opening the player picker immediately (`:238-295`). The court board has a session `<select>`
  (`admin-kotc.html:259`) and Next round (`:261`) but **no create control**. **What is missing is
  only a league-first entry** — plus Rotating Pairs, which does not exist. **Build:** reuse
  `kotcplay.js` (create `:343`, players `:363`, round `:405`, move `:539`, withdraw `:692`, live
  `:818`) and the three screens; **QOTC must NOT get a gendered field or a second code path** —
  `kotc_sessions.name` already carries the night's identity. Reference data outranks any spec
  sentence (`docs/2026-08-04_reference_kotc-live-run_v1_0.md`).
- **T2-11 — PARTLY-TRUE, and it exposes a GUARD BLIND SPOT — the most consequential finding of the
  round.** The screenshot is **`index.html` signed in**, painted by `app.js renderDashboard`
  (`:208-249`); the eight cards match `:237-246` exactly. **(a) The member RAIL is CLEAN —
  REFUTED:** every href measured is a member page (`site-nav.js:177-183, 199-204, 241`); the old
  "Manage" group of four admin destinations was deleted in v2.15/v0.101.0. **(b) The CARD GRID is
  the real complaint — CONFIRMED:** it leads to `tournament.html` (`app.js:239` — which the suite's
