  own `isAdminSurface` counts as an admin surface, `header_actions.test.mjs:260`),
  `admin-users.html` (`:243`), `admin-registrations.html` (`:244`) and staff-only Foundation
  (`:246`). A plain member sees none of these — **so the owner saw it precisely because he is
  admin.** **(c) The designed member home EXISTS and nothing routes anyone to it — CONFIRMED:**
  `home.html` v2.0.0 has the announcements box (`:125-132`) and CTA row (`:153-158`), but
  `app.js route()` (`:66-70`) and `verifyToken` (`:72-83`) both land on the card grid.
  **THE BLIND SPOT:** `app.js` is v0.6.0 (2026-07-23) — it **predates the v2.15 rail cleanup** and
  is the surviving instance of admin destinations on a member surface, while the guard that forbids
  exactly that (`memberNavVerdict`) reads **only `site-nav.js`** (`header_actions.test.mjs:264`).
  **So a builder can fix `app.js` without reddening anything, and the existing green proves
  nothing about the file the owner is actually looking at.** **Hazards if reworking `index.html`:**
  `header_shell.test.mjs:20, 304` and `header_actions.test.mjs:38` pin `index.html` as the
  documented reduced-header exception that `app.js` owns — giving it the canonical member header
  makes `site-nav.js` bind `themeToggle` a second time (`site-nav.js:128-137` vs `app.js:21`) and
  **the double-bind kills the button** (the v0.52.0 failure class). `login_brand.test.mjs:109-181`
  pins `renderLogin`'s brand markers.
- **T2-12 — PARTLY-TRUE, and half of it is a decision, not a build.** The splash and post-login
  screen do differ (`app.js:142-159` vs `:233-247`; rail `site-nav.js:240-242` vs `:198-205`) —
  **but Explore is identical and first in both, and the signed-in group is APPENDED below it, so
  no shared item ever moves position across the transition.** The menu-order half is **already a
  written, held proposal in this roadmap — do not rebuild it, decide it.** If the rail is
  reordered: `header_actions.test.mjs:263-268` reddens on any admin href entering NAV (including
  `tournament.html`), and `member_nav_paint.test.mjs:95-137` pins `async function init()` and
  `layout.appendChild(aside)`.

### Extrapolated guards (write these WITH each unit, not after)

**Token attachment: every member surface's `api()` attaches `bt_token` — two lists, one source.
This is T2-13's defect class, and the generalisation is worth more than the fix.** ·
**Member-surface destinations: `app.js`'s card grid ∪ `site-nav.js`'s NAV, intersected with
`admin-*`, must be empty — the guard must read BOTH files, because today it reads one and the
defect lives in the other.** · **Dangling classes: every class in markup has a rule in some
stylesheet (T2-1d's `ed-side`; v0.116.0's `bt-back` was the same family and the generalisation
was never written).** · Undo/redo: N ops → undo×N == original, redo×N == final. · Email-schedule:
recipient set both directions + sandbox-says-so. · Captain display: "First L." on no-login
surfaces, `full:true` on staff surfaces. · Curated generator: 8 ≤ games ≤ 10 across team counts
6–24, pool sizes differing by ≤1. · Cross-pool seeding: no round-one pair shares a pool (2–4
pools); every breaking team lands exactly one slot. · Pool board: the bench is its own scroll
container, asserted in CSS — a markup check cannot see the cascade. · Palettes: 4 templates ×
named pairs × AA, NC mutating one `--text`. · Seed data: every seeded table appears in `WIPE_SQL`
and generate-twice stays green. · USAV: formula property tests once the doc lands. · Check-in
recast: the regenerated schedule uses exactly the checked-in set; removals never orphan scores.

### RECOMMENDED SEQUENCE — reordered by what the measurements showed

1. **T2-13 + T2-14 as ONE unit** — the missing `Authorization` header in `leagues.js` (a real
   defect with a found axis) **plus** the LFG/sub/library seed rows (those boards are provably
   empty, not broken). Ship the token two-lists guard as the generalisation.
2. **T2-5, the bracket REWIRE** — repointing one button at an engine that already exists is the
   largest visible win per line changed in the whole round.
3. **T2-3 + T2-4b + T2-1d as one "small measured fixes" unit** — a captain join with a ready
   helper, a one-line client cap, and a one-word class rename.
4. **T2-11's card grid AND the guard blind spot** — fix `app.js`'s admin destinations and widen
   the guard to read `app.js`, because the current green says nothing about the file the owner
   is actually looking at.
5. ~~**T2-8 pool board**~~ **CLOSED v0.125.0**, then **T2-4 curated generator**.
6. **THEN spec T2-9a with the owner** — now knowing it is mostly wiring: check-in/waitlist deep
   links (waitlist already accepts `?event=`), `BT_ROSTER` mounted wherever teams appear, and
   division grouping moved into `formats.js` generate-schedule.

**Why this order changed:** the previous sequence was written from the owner's report and put the
bracket work fifth. Measurement showed it is a rewire of an engine that already exists, and that
two apparent "new features" (T2-18, most of T2-16) are already built. **The strongest alternative
— keep the report's own order — loses because it would have spent a session building a bracket
transfer that was already there.**

### Suggested additions beyond the 18 (recommendation attached)

Print/PDF day-sheet per event (schedule + pools + bracket — the artifact a tournament desk
actually holds) · a QR code per court linking to that court's score entry · a day-of live
dashboard driven by the score flow · SMS day-of blast (the module exists and is dormant) · one
public event page (schedule, standings, bracket, no login) replacing the separate live board.
**Recommended first: the print/PDF day-sheet** — it is the smallest one that changes how a real
tournament day runs, and it needs no new data.

## §-1k. OWNER BRIEF 2026-08-10 — the bracket, the live board, identity and hosting (added v1.22)

The owner's answers to the seven owed questions arrived with **eleven new asks attached**. This
section is the queue for the new asks. **Every disposition below carries how it was checked**,
because the last two rounds both contained a premise that was wrong in the owner's favour and one
that was wrong against it — and the standing rule is that a report is MEASURED before it is queued,
with REFUTED and ALREADY-EXISTS as permitted verdicts.

### Answers that closed open questions (do not re-ask)

- **Passkey enrolled — VERIFIED IN LIVE D1**, not taken on trust: `webauthn_credentials` now holds
  **1 credential across 1 user** (read 2026-08-10, iteration 51). This was `0` for the whole
  project. **S-4b's lockout risk is therefore gone for that user** — closing sandbox login at
  go-live no longer strands the only admin. `BREVO_API_KEY` is still unset, so the magic-link path
  remains sandbox-only and the passkey is now the real key to the building.
- **S-4a — require user verification (Face ID / PIN) on passkeys.** Owner: *"Not necessary but ok."*
  Read as a **yes with no urgency**. The work is `webauthn.js:227`'s `parsed.userVerified` wired to
  a refusal, and **`webauthn_crypto.test.mjs`'s deliberately-pinned "login succeeds WITHOUT the UV
  flag" test must be REWRITTEN, not deleted** — it is the record of the contract that is changing.
  **One caveat to put to him before it ships:** with exactly one credential enrolled, requiring UV
  means a device that cannot do biometrics or a PIN stops being able to sign in at all. Do it while
  sandbox login is still open, never as part of closing it.
- **T2-1a undo/redo — ANSWERED, and it is the expensive shape.** Owner: *"Hold until save. Revert
  back and forward options. Confirm to save."* This settles the open decision recorded at T2-1a: it
  is **DEFERRED persistence**, not reversal-on-a-save-every-move design. So the schedule editor
  stops POSTing each move and holds a change list, exactly as the pool board already does — **the
  pool board is the working precedent for the whole shape** (local model, dirty flag, one Save that
  writes everything, `beforeunload` guard, confirm-on-navigate), and it should be read before this
  is designed rather than after. The move endpoint stays self-inverse, so undo/redo over the held
  list is arithmetic; the cost is the editor's state model, not the routes.
- **The landing page (D-19 / T2-11 half two) — ANSWERED BOTH WAYS.** Owner: *"Card page is ok for
  admin, members should have the one other one designed with inbox, announcements, etc."* So
  `index.html`'s card grid survives **for staff**, `home.html` becomes the **member** landing, and
  **D-19's two-lookalike-pages problem is settled by role rather than by renaming**. The rail order
  he asked for is *"1 Dashboard, 2 Inbox, 3 etc down"* and he asked for **variations to choose
  from** — so this ships as a proposal first, the way F-5/F-6 did in v0.106.0, not as a fait
  accompli.
- **Cross-org — the question was BADLY FRAMED and that is our fault, not his.** He replied *"I do
  not understand."* The question asked him to confirm a recommendation about three modules he has
  never seen behave any other way. **Re-ask it as an observable choice, not a confirmation:** "Today
  a member of Boomtown cannot see, message, or find a sub from another organization. Should that
  stay true?" — and only if he says no does the privacy consequence (profile visibility chosen
  inside one org, blocks stored per-org) need explaining. Recorded because the framing failure is
  the finding: a question the owner cannot answer is a question we asked wrong.
- **USAV (T2-17)** — deferred by the owner to *"towards the completion of the modules."* Not
  blocked, not owed, not queued.

### The eleven new asks, measured

**K-1. Team numbers beside captain names — PARTLY SHIPPED (v0.125.0), and the owner has now given
the real rule, which is a THREE-TIER FALLBACK, not a single source.** Owner 2026-08-10: *"Team number
is assigned based on historical performance data if returning team (or player combinations) then
guess based on admins arrangement of tiles (1 being top down for each division). Then finally
registration order."* So:

| Tier | Source | State |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Historical performance** of a returning team, or of a returning *player combination* | **UNBUILT.** Needs a team-identity rule across events — see the hazard below |
| 2 | **The admin's own tile arrangement** on the pool board, numbered 1..N top-down **within each division** | ✅ **SHIPPED v0.146.0** as `teams.team_no` (migration 0045), written by the board save and by nothing else. It was NOT as cheap as this row predicted: `board_order` + `division_id` do carry the arrangement, but `division_id` has writers outside the board, so the number had to be stored rather than read |
| 3 | **Registration order** | ✅ **SHIPPED v0.125.0**, and since v0.146.0 it is the FALLBACK half of `board_no` — `COALESCE(team_no, registration rank)` — which is what the three-tier design always meant |

**What shipped is the LAST resort, correctly — and that is the right thing to have built first,**
because it is the only tier that always has an answer. Tiers 1 and 2 are *better guesses when they
exist*, and a numbering scheme whose top tier is often absent must still produce a number.

**THE HAZARD IN TIER 1, and it must be settled before anyone builds it.** "Returning team" has no
definition in this schema. `teams` rows are per-event (`teams.event_id`), team NAMES are free text
and reused casually, and the only stable identity across events is the **captain's contact id** plus
the `team_members` roster. So "the same team returning" is really "a player combination seen
before", which is a fuzzy match with a threshold nobody has chosen — 2 of 4 shared players? 3 of 6?
**And it collides with §-1c D-5:** `standings.rank` is an event-wide pool finish with NO division
filter, so "historical performance" read naively off `rank` compares a team that finished 3rd in a
10-team Open field with one that finished 3rd in a 30-team mixed field. **Do not build tier 1 from
`rank` without deciding what a comparable finish is.**

**TIER 2 SHIPPED v0.146.0. This paragraph called it a `ROW_NUMBER()`-shaped READ and it had to be a WRITE** — the prediction held for `board_order` and `pools.sort_order`, which only the board save touches, but not for `division_id`, which `divisions.assign` and the promote/relegate moves rewrite without going near the board. A derived number would have renumbered a saved sheet silently, which is the very failure the rest of this paragraph names. The design it describes below is otherwise exactly what shipped, including the UI statement.

**TIER 2 IS THE NEXT UNIT OF THIS ITEM AND IT IS SMALL.** Once a director arranges the board,
`board_order` is the within-pool index and `division_id` is on the team, so "1 being top down for
each division" is a `ROW_NUMBER()`-shaped read over `(division_id, pool sort_order, board_order)`.
**The one thing to preserve is the property v0.125.0 was built around:** a team number must not
change under a director mid-check. Tier 2 numbers legitimately DO change when the board is
rearranged — so the honest shape is that the number is **frozen at a moment the director chooses**
(the same Save that writes the board), not recomputed on every read. Otherwise the sheet in their
hand disagrees with the screen. **That is a real design decision and it should be stated in the UI**,
e.g. the board reads "Numbers set when you saved" rather than silently renumbering.

**K-2. Seed numbers on the bracket tiles — CONFIRMED UNBUILT, and the class name nearly said
otherwise.** `admin-brackets.js` mentions `seed` in exactly two places, both prose: the
`seeded by …` caption (`:90`) and `bSeedWarn` (`:274`). **No tile carries a number.** The owner
offers two forms — *"1 v 8"* or *"1 A v 4 B"* — and **the second is already computable**, because
v0.124.0's `crossPoolOrder` knows each team's pool when it arranges round one; the pool letter is
in the seeding, not the tile. **Recommendation: build the pool form (`#1 A v #4 B`) and let the
plain form fall out of it when an event has one pool or none**, rather than shipping two modes.

**K-3. Draw the bracket with connecting lines — CONFIRMED UNBUILT. `br-line` IS NOT WHAT ITS NAME
SUGGESTS.** `admin-brackets.html:76` defines `.br-line { display: flex; justify-content:
space-between; gap: 8px; font-size: 13px; }` — it is the **team-and-score row inside a match tile**,
not a connector. The tree is a horizontal flex of `.br-round` columns (`:42`) with **no connectors
of any kind**. **This was checked by reading the rule rather than trusting the selector**, which is
the `ed-side` lesson (v0.122.0) applied before building instead of after. Connectors between rounds
are the work, and SVG overlaid on the existing column layout is the shape that does not require
rebuilding `buildTree`'s output.

**K-4. Green dots for active/live games.** **Half-built and worth knowing which half:**
`.br-match[data-done="1"]` already marks a FINISHED game with a 3px `--positive` left border
(`admin-brackets.html:48`) — so a "done" state exists and an "in progress" state does not.
**Two cautions.** (a) The 3px coloured side-tab is the exact cliché D-10 records and v0.95.1
removed elsewhere; if this is touched, replace it with the dot idiom rather than adding a second
tab. (b) The owner asks for a **glowing** dot on the live board — a glow that animates forever is
the one animation class the design standard rules out on a screen people stare at all day, so it
should pulse gently or not at all, and must respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**K-5. Double elimination and round robin, chosen in settings.** **This is the largest item in the
brief and it is a FORMAT-ENGINE change, not a settings checkbox.** `buildTree` produces a single
-elimination tree; round robin already exists for POOL play (that is what the scheduler does) but
not as a bracket format, and double elimination needs a losers' bracket the schema has no shape for.
**Spec this with the owner before any code** — in particular whether "round robin tournament" means
a bracket-less event (which mostly exists today) or a round-robin FINAL after pools.

**K-6. Email the schedule, not just print/export — this is T2-2, already queued.** Transport exists
and is Brevo-gated; **`BREVO_API_KEY` is not set**, so the sandbox-honesty rule is load-bearing: with
no key it must SAY nothing was sent and never report success. Owner's new wording extends it to
leagues as well as tournaments, which is the same route with a different recipient query.

**K-7. The live score page: ESPN-style tiles, live updates, readable at distance.** **ALREADY-EXISTS
in part, and the part that exists is the hard part:** `live.js` already polls every 25 seconds,
already says when it last refreshed, and already **diffs against the previous poll** so it can
animate only what changed (`:106-137`) — including a stable row identity across polls, which is the
whole requirement for a diff and the thing that is expensive to add later. So "this should live
update as scores are entered" is **built**; what is unbuilt is the TILE DESIGN and the numbers from
K-1/K-2 appearing on it. **Do not rebuild the polling.**

**K-8. Cast the live board to a TV.** Chromecast needs a sender SDK and an app id; AirPlay screen
-mirrors whatever the browser shows and needs nothing from us. **The honest cheap answer is the
third one he offered — "or see it online somehow":** the live board already has no login, so a
short per-event URL opened on the TV's own browser is a URL change, not a casting integration.
**Recommend that first**, and treat Chromecast as a separate decision with a real cost.

**K-9. Team logos, optional, with a member's "favorite team".** **CONFIRMED UNBUILT end to end** —
zero `logo` references in `tournaments.js` or `registrations.js`, and zero `favorite`/`favourite`
anywhere in `worker/src` or `web/assets`. **Prior art exists for the upload half**: orgs already
carry `logo_url` (`orgs.js:144`) and `uploads.js` exists. The favourite-team half is a new column on
the member profile plus a display rule, and it needs a decision the owner has not made: **does a
member's favourite team let them see anything they could not see before?** If yes it is a
permissions change; if no it is decoration. Ask before building.

**K-10. The named URL system, and per-org URL masking (`bt.<platformname>.com`).** **Two different
jobs that must not be confused, and the first one is a real latent hazard.**
(a) **Surviving the domain change.** The app is served from GitHub Pages under a `/btplatform/`
path prefix today. Anything that hardcodes that prefix or the `github.io` host breaks the day the
name changes. **This should be measured NOW rather than on the day** — one grep of `web/` for the
literal host and prefix, and `config.js` is the single place an origin belongs.
(b) **Per-org vanity hosts** (the `colorado-boom.gymdesk.com` shape). `orgs` already has `slug`
(`orgs.js:144`), which is the identifier such a host would resolve to, so the data half is largely
there; the rest is DNS, a Cloudflare custom-hostname configuration and an org-settings field. **(a)
is engineering we control and should be done early; (b) is configuration and money and is the
owner's call.**

**K-10(a) MOVE-DAY RUNBOOK — written at ship time (v0.149.0, iteration 77), and
`origin_portability.test.mjs` pins every line of it with a positive control so none can silently
go stale.** After B8, the app's own address lives in exactly these places; the day the GitHub
address becomes a real domain, this is the complete edit list:
1. **`wrangler.toml`** — `APP_URL` (the frontend base every worker-built member link uses) and
   `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (CORS). Two lines.
2. **Root `index.html`** — the canonical link. Static SEO metadata; regenerate the 15-line stub.
3. **Root `404.html`** — its root-absolute links (`/btplatform/web/…` today; on a custom domain
   the prefix disappears). Kept static DELIBERATELY: a 404 page renders at any depth so it cannot
   use relative paths, and static keeps it working without JavaScript — six find-and-replaces on
   move day beat a JS-dependent error page every day before it.
4. **GitHub Pages settings** — the custom-domain configuration itself (outside the repo).
5. `web/assets/config.js` `apiBase` and `signup-widget.js`'s `data-api` default change only if the
   WORKER's address moves too — a separate decision from the Pages domain.
**Consequences that are not edits, so nobody discovers them on the day:** the live passkey is
bound to rpId `10xequity.github.io` (`webauthn.js:347` derives it from APP_URL) and **will not
authenticate on the new domain** — re-enrol after the move; sandbox sign-in covers the gap. And
every external site that already pasted an embed snippet keeps pointing at the old origin —
their nature; keep the old origin redirecting through the transition or re-paste them.

**K-11. Let a member change their own display name — ✅ SHIPPED v0.130.0, and the v1.24 record
below was WRONG ONE LEVEL DEEPER.** Its half (i) — "his own name can be fixed today, by an admin,
no release required" — was **FALSE**: a positive-controlled grep for `UPDATE users` matches nothing
in the worker, and `addUser` sets `display_name` only when INSERTING a new user, **silently
discarding it for an existing one while returning ok** (→ **D-27**). The column had NO writer for
existing accounts since it was born; `admin-users.js:280` sends a name that the server throws away.
**v0.130.0 ships the first writer:** `PATCH /api/me`, session-is-the-target (no id read from the
body — structurally impossible to rename anyone else), whitespace→NULL, junk refused wholesale,
audited, with the Settings Account card's new row as the UI and the **D-18 boundary pinned** (never
touches `contacts.full_name` — which members already edit on the Profile page via
`profiles.js update()`, the OTHER thing the re-measurement found). Guard `display_name.test.mjs`
(9), including a deliberately-green **D-27 pin** its fixer must rewrite. The original v1.24 text,
kept because its refutation of "add a display name" still stands:
`users.display_name` **already exists** — the admin dashboard greets by it, and it is what
WebAuthn registers as the account's display name (`webauthn.js:63`). The owner sees `vvisuth`
because **his row's `display_name` is NULL and every surface falls back to the email's local part**
(`admin-dash.js:20` splits on `@`).

**K-12. Expired events drop out of the sub finder, the game finder and the announcements —
✅ SHIPPED v0.126.0 (iteration 52).** Owner 2026-08-10. **Confirmed and worse than reported:** five
member-facing queries selected `status='open'` with **no time predicate at all**, and `subs.js`
orders `COALESCE(needed_at, created_at) ASC` — oldest first — so an expired request was **the first
thing a member saw**. The owner's word "announcements" was literal: `announcements.js` runs its own
copies of both queries, so a fix to `subs.js` and `lfg.js` alone would have left the home feed stale.
`notPastSql()` is now the one definition, exported from `subs.js` (the leaf both other modules
already import), day-granular because `needed_at` is often date-only, and **failing open** because
`date()` yields NULL for anything unparseable. **The exits were enumerated before removing
anything and BOTH boards had one:** `web/assets/lfg.js:112` is the only trigger for "Report a
no-show" and renders from the filtered list, and both flood caps count `status='open'` with no date
filter while telling the member to "cancel one" — so **a stale row leaves the boards but never
leaves its own author's view.** Guard: `stale_listings.test.mjs` (14). **Noted in passing: the
feed's `events` category has always filtered `starts_at >= datetime('now')`, so this was already the
house style on that screen and subs + community were simply the two that never got it.**

### Recommended order for §-1k

**K-11(ii) self-service display name** (smallest, and it is the owner's own daily irritation) →
**K-2 + K-3 + K-4 as ONE bracket unit** (numbers, connectors and the live marker are all the same
screen and the same render, and shipping them separately means three releases over one file) →
**K-7 tile design on the live board**, which then has the numbers K-2 produced → **K-10(a) the
domain-portability grep**, which is cheap and gets more expensive the longer it waits → **K-6/T2-2
email** → **K-9 logos** → **K-5 formats, after a spec session**. **K-8 casting** rides on K-10.

## §-1l. THE ORGANIZATION MODEL — the owner's 2026-08-10 question, measured and answered (v1.23)

Owner: *"No, the subfinder should be universal. messages should be universal with 1 account. —
plan/discussion — perhaps instead of global organizations, the organization decision should be in
the league and tournament areas that work as a branding option rather than replicating the system 3
times. This allows MPS does not need the other functionalities outside of leagues and tournaments,
and even qc does not have tournaments. Would that make things easier to navigate? Provide feedback
and proposals analysis."*

**This closes the cross-org question that had been open for four sessions. It also contains an
architectural proposal, and the honest answer to that proposal is "you are right about the problem
and the proposed cure would be a rewrite of the security model". Both halves are below, measured.**

### What is actually true today — read from live D1 and the schema, 2026-08-10

| Fact | Measured |
|---|---|
| Orgs | **6, not 3** — active: Boomtown Athletics, Match Point Social, Queens Club, Colorado Boom; inactive: Oda Up, External/Rental |
| Data outside org 1 | **NONE.** Org 1 holds 49 contacts and 6 events; orgs 2, 3, 4, 5, 10 hold **zero of both** |
| Tables carrying `org_id` | **86 of 97** |
| `org_id` references in the worker | **1,007 across 45 of 51 modules** |
| `users` table | **Has NO `org_id`, and `email` is globally UNIQUE** |

### The finding that reframes the whole question: ONE ACCOUNT ALREADY EXISTS

**`users` is global.** A person has exactly one account, signs in once, and `user_org_roles` grants
them whichever orgs they belong to. So *"messages should be universal with 1 account"* is **half
already true and half misdiagnosed**: the account is not the fragmented thing. **`contacts` is.**
A person has one `users` row and **one `contacts` row per org**, and every member-facing feature —
profile, visibility, messages, sub availability, blocks — hangs off `contacts`, not `users`.

**That is exactly §-1c D-18**, which records that two resolvers already disagree about how a user
maps to a contact (`contactForSession` joins on email and consults `contacts.user_id` only for
ORDER BY; `messages.js ownContact` matches `user_id OR email`, case-sensitively). **D-18 is
therefore no longer a tidy-up — it is step one of anything cross-org**, and it must be settled
before, not during.

### On the proposal itself: "put the org decision inside leagues and tournaments"

**Recommendation: do NOT unscope `org_id`. Do the thing the owner actually wants by a different
route — per-org MODULE VISIBILITY — which delivers the whole navigation benefit at roughly one
percent of the cost and changes no security property.**

**Why the literal proposal is the expensive answer.** `org_id` is not a feature that was added to
the system; at 86 tables and 1,007 call sites it **is** the spine. Two guards exist solely to hold
that line (`cross_org_isolation.test.mjs`, `admin_route_gating.test.mjs`), and the reason they exist
is that org scoping is the only thing standing between four clubs' member lists. Turning
organizations into "a branding option inside leagues and tournaments" means every one of those 86
tables needs a new answer to "who may read this row", and the honest description of that work is a
rewrite of the authorization model, not a navigation change.

**Why the owner is nonetheless right about the problem.** His evidence is exact: *"MPS does not need
the other functionalities outside of leagues and tournaments, and even qc does not have
tournaments."* Today every org gets an identical admin rail — Point of Sale, Membership Plans,
Staff Pay, Marketing, Tryouts, Waivers, Passes — whether it uses them or not. **That is a real
defect of presentation**, and it is what makes the system feel "replicated three times". It is felt
most sharply right now precisely because five of six orgs are empty: the rail promises a club a
dozen modules and every one of them opens on nothing.

**The proposal that answers it — P-1, per-org module visibility.** One additive column
(`orgs.modules_json`, or a small `org_modules` table), an admin screen listing the modules with
checkboxes, and rail filtering in `admin-nav.js`. Boomtown enables everything; MPS enables Leagues +
Tournaments; Queens Club enables Leagues only. **Three properties it must have, and each is a lesson
this track has already paid for:**
1. **It is a VIEW filter, never a permission.** A hidden module's routes stay gated exactly as they
   are today. A rail that hides Point of Sale must not be the only thing preventing access to it —
   *"do not edit" is not a permission boundary*, and neither is a missing menu item.
2. **It must not become a lockout.** An org that switches everything off must still reach the
   settings screen that switches things back on. Enumerate the exits before shipping the filter, and
   pair the hiding rule with a presence test for the sanctioned exit (the v0.123.0 rule).
3. **Default ON for every existing org.** A default of "off" would silently empty Boomtown's rail on
   deploy.

**P-2 — the branding half is largely built already and should be finished rather than designed.**
Login branding is per-org and settled (2026-08-08); `orgs` already carries `name`, `slug` and
`logo_url`; `/api/public/org-brand` already serves them. Combined with **§-1k K-10(b)**'s per-org
vanity host, "the org is a brand on a league or tournament" is mostly a matter of surfacing what
exists — and it is worth noting that P-1 + P-2 together *are* the owner's proposal, delivered as
presentation rather than as a data-model change.

### The universal features, now that they are decided

**Owner has chosen: sub finder universal, messages universal.** Leagues and tournaments stay
per-org — which is what he said and also what the schema is shaped for. **Before either is built,
two consequences still need his explicit yes, and they are the same two recorded in the handoff §5;
they have not gone away just because the direction is settled:**
1. **Profile visibility was chosen inside one org.** Members set who can see their player card
   believing the audience was that club. Going universal republishes those cards to every other
   org's members. The fix is a visibility level that means "my club only" and defaults to it.
2. **Blocks are org-scoped and would silently stop working.** Block someone in Boomtown and the
   block is stored against Boomtown; go universal without re-scoping blocks and **the person you
   blocked reappears and can message you from another org.** This one is not a preference — a
   blocking feature that silently fails is worse than not having one.

**Recommended order if he confirms:** D-18 (one identity rule) → blocks re-scoped to the person, not
the org → messages universal → profile visibility gains a "my club only" level, defaulted → sub
finder universal. **The sub finder goes LAST on purpose: it emails matching members**, so making it
universal without the four steps above turns one club's request into a cross-club email fan-out.

## §-1m. PARTNER / PARTNER-HOSTED EVENTS — a scoped plan (owner request 2026-08-10, v1.23)

Owner: *"Add to roadmap a member view of partners that can host or feature information about their
organization on the system — like a market place where they have their own view. Then they can
register either through this platform or through their own but link from our system. It then can
list those events on this platform with links to the system OR their own individual link. For
example using volosports.com OR volleyballlife.com/boomtown where we are forced to use a 3rd party
online system for registrations. We would just link to this on our platform but be able to advertise
and etc. — Scope this out and plan it."*

**The core insight, and it is what makes this cheap:** this is **not** a new events system. It is
the existing events system with **two things made optional** — the registration step, and the
assumption that we own the event. An event that links out is an ordinary published event whose
"Register" button is an external URL.

### What already exists and must not be rebuilt

`events` already carries `org_id`, `type`, `name`, `starts_at`, `location`, `status` and price;
`orgs` already carries `name`, `slug`, `logo_url` and is already a first-class entity with **six rows
live, four of them empty** — which is the point: **`orgs` is already the partner table.** Org 10 is
literally named *"External / Rental"*. The public events feed, the calendar feed and the embeddable
widget all exist.

### The scope, in four units, smallest first

**PM-1 — an event can point at someone else's registration.** ✅ **SHIPPED v0.147.0, and all three rules below were built rather than noted.** Two things the plan did not predict: the fork's SIGNATURE had to change (a third parameter would have let a caller omit the new axis — D-29 a third time), and rule 3 had to be evaluated on the RESULTING state rather than the request, because a write carrying only the URL still contradicts an event that is already priced. Two additive columns:
`events.external_url` and `events.external_label` (e.g. "Register on Volleyball Life"). Where the
event page renders its Register button, an event with an `external_url` renders an outbound link
instead. **Three rules that must hold, and each is a trap this repo already knows:**
· the outbound link must be visibly outbound (an external-link affordance and `rel="noopener"`), or
we have made a third party look like us; · an external event **must not** offer check-in, waitlist
or payment surfaces that will never receive data — *empty and broken look identical to a user*; · it
must be impossible to set both a price and an external URL, or the product contradicts itself.

**PM-2 — a partner profile page.** `orgs` gains a public description, links and a hero image, served
by the existing `/api/public/org-brand` shape. **A read-only page at `/p/<slug>`** listing that
partner's published events. No new auth, no new roles.

**PM-3 — the marketplace view.** One member-facing page listing partners and their upcoming events,
which is a query over `orgs` + `events` that is **already org-scoped and must deliberately NOT be**
for this surface — this is the first legitimately cross-org READ in the product, and it must be
built as a *published-only, public-fields-only* projection rather than by relaxing any existing
query. **This is the unit where the §-1l discipline gets tested: a marketplace is a cross-org read,
and it must get its own narrow query rather than a loosened one.**

**PM-4 — partner self-service.** A partner admin editing their own profile and posting their own
linked events. This is the only unit that needs new authorization thinking, and it should not be
started until PM-1..PM-3 are live and the §-1l identity questions are settled.

### The open questions for the owner, before PM-1

1. **Is a partner an `orgs` row, or a new `partners` table?** Recommendation: **`orgs`**, with a flag
   — it already has the identity, the branding and the slug, and a second near-identical table is how
   two lists start disagreeing. The cost is that "org" then means both "a club that uses the
   platform" and "a club we merely advertise", so the flag must be explicit.
2. **Does a partner event appear in the main events list, or only in the marketplace?** This is a
   product decision with a real trade-off: mixed-in means better discovery and a confusing "why can't
   I register here"; separate means clean but ignored.
3. ~~**Money.**~~ **ANSWERED 2026-08-10:** *"No commission or fee for now since it is a passthrough
   and used for backlinks."* PM-2 stays a page, not a billing relationship.

### THE PLANNING SESSION (owner 2026-08-10: "we need to scope this out in more detail… suggest ways
### to do this or ask a planning and scoping session questions for this")

**How to run it:** one sitting, walk ONE concrete partner end-to-end — Cathy's Mile High Classic is
the perfect specimen, because it already lives on a SignUpGenius URL that gets advertised and needs
"a link back." Answer the questions below against that single real case, then generalise. **Nothing
in PM-2…PM-4 is built until this session has happened.**

**The agenda, in decision order:**
1. **Who is a partner?** Walk the six existing organization rows (org 10 is literally
   "External / Rental"). Is Cathy's masters program (a) an organization of its own, (b) a program
   inside Boomtown, or (c) the first true "partner"? The answer decides whether PM-2 is a flag on
   `orgs` or something new.
2. **Where do partner events appear?** Open the current events list and the future marketplace side
   by side (paper is fine): when a member searches "August tournament", do they see Cathy's event
   with an outbound Register link, or only inside a Partners tab? Mixed-in = discovery + "why can't
   I register here" confusion; separate = clean + ignored. Decide by walking the member's actual
   path, not in the abstract.
3. **Who edits the partner's page?** The owner on their behalf (cheap, PM-2 alone), or the partner
   themselves (PM-4 — the first non-staff authorization concept in the product; same class as
   §-1o SG-3's host role, and probably the SAME mechanism, so decide them together).
4. **What does the backlink actually point at?** `bt.<platformname>.com/p/cathy` needs K-10's
   domain work; `github.io/...` URLs in printed flyers go stale the day the domain changes. This
   ties the marketplace to K-10(a/b) and should set their relative order.
5. **What is success?** If it is "Cathy stops paying $550/yr and her sheets live here", then §-1o
   (the sign-up sheets) is the marketplace's real first unit and PM-2 is its decoration — worth
   saying out loud before building the decoration first.

### ✅ THE QUESTIONNAIRE IS ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-11 00:16 — recorded verbatim-first, v1.29)

**Q1 — who is a partner:** *"Perhaps. Allow them to select one of the configured options, if they
write Partner, they can write the name in, but it must be approved by Super Admin or Cathy can be
allowed to type any she wishes with approval toggle (but her programs are usually under
Boomtown)."* → **Partner identity = pick from the configured organizations, OR free-text a new
partner name that sits PENDING until approved** — with a per-account "trusted, no approval needed"
toggle for operators like Cathy. Two consequences recorded now: (a) this is the project's **first
APPROVAL WORKFLOW** (a pending state + an approve action + who may approve), and "Super Admin"
names a role tier the platform does not have — today `admin` is the top; treat "Super Admin" as
`admin` unless the owner says otherwise, and SAY that reading back at spec time. (b) The trusted
toggle is another PER-ACCOUNT grant — the same mechanism family as SG-3's module toggles; design
them once. **Cathy's programs usually run under Boomtown**, so partner rows are for genuinely
external organizations, not for her.

**Q2 — where do partner events show:** *"B, main list of events needs to be sortable. Have tabs at
the top to sort, similar to the tournament page in Boomtownvb.com."* → **Separate marketplace page
only** (PM-3 stays a distinct surface), **plus a NEW ask, K-14**: the member events list gets
sort/filter tabs like the boomtownvb.com tournament page. **Measured: `schedule.js:21-23` already
has a working `.tab` mechanism on that page** — K-14 is extending an existing tab row, not
building one.

**Q3 — who edits a partner's page:** *"A and may be B."* → **Staff-edited now (PM-2 is unblocked
on this axis); partner self-service (PM-4) stays deferred** until the SG-3/PM-4 shared
limited-account spec moment.

**Q4 — what the advertised link points at:** *"If it is outside registration, to an outside
registration."* → The backlink target is **the external registration system itself** when one
exists — which is exactly PM-1's `external_url`. The domain-priority half of the question went
unanswered; **K-10 keeps its queue position** and was not promoted.

**Q5 — success:** *"Sheet page, then page later."* → **SHEETS FIRST, marketplace page later.**
§-1o's SG order stands and PM-2/PM-3 sit behind it. This matches the §-0 queue as written — no
reorder needed.

**Rider 1 (with Q5): "Events should have an option to have fees charged and assessed."** Already
half-true and now pinned to SG-1's scope: events carry `price_cents` and the paid registration
path exists; **SG-1's sheet must therefore support BOTH free and priced sign-ups** (the owner said
the same at 22:03 — "free or to be charged"), with the priced path reusing the existing
registration/payment flow, never a second one.

**Rider 2 — NEW, K-15: "Ensure the square API has ability to write and create items in square
under the appropriate organization and then name the item correctly."** **Measured before
recording (D-26 discipline): the write machinery EXISTS** — `memberships.js:86-100` already
creates Square Catalog objects (`POST /v2/catalog/object`, a SUBSCRIPTION_PLAN and its variation)
through the `sq()` helper, and `orgs.square_location_id` exists for per-org scoping. **What does
NOT exist:** creating a regular catalog ITEM for an event, named from the event, under the right
organization's location — and the open questions are credential-shaped (one platform Square token
today; "under the appropriate organization" may mean per-location naming or per-org tokens, which
is config the owner holds). K-15 = extend the existing `sq()` pattern to event items; spec the
credential question at build time.

## §-1n. PROPOSED FEATURES — CORRECTED v1.25 after the owner had already approved (2026-08-10)

> **THE v1.24 VERSION OF THIS SECTION WAS WRONG, AND THE OWNER APPROVED IT BEFORE THE ERROR WAS
> FOUND.** Its "verified unbuilt" greps used `\|` inside `grep -E`, which matches a **literal
> backslash-pipe** — every multi-alternative pattern searched for a string that cannot occur,
> returned nothing, and "no matches" was read as "not built" (§-1c **D-26**). Measured properly,
> **four of the five proposals described features that already exist.** The original claims are
> preserved struck-through in the git history; the truth per item is below.

### P-A. Duplicate an event — ~~unbuilt~~ **WAS BUILT AND WIRED ALL ALONG; its real gap SHIPPED v0.127.0**

`POST /api/events/:id/duplicate` (`events_admin.js:130`) and the **Duplicate button on the event
screen** (`admin-event.js:107`) predate the proposal — plus a whole `event_templates` system
(save-as-template, create-from-template) beside them. **The genuine gap:** the copy took the events
ROW only, so a duplicated league arrived with **no divisions** — and for "set up next season", the
divisions ARE the configuration. **v0.127.0 ships that half:** live divisions ride along (new rows,
never shared), the count is reported and audited, and the people/results boundary is pinned by the
feature's first-ever route tests (`event_duplicate.test.mjs`, 7). **Residual:** `EVENT_FIELDS` omits
`ends_at`, so a copy keeps its start and loses its end — recorded, minor for the next-season case.

### P-B. Waitlist auto-promote — ~~unbuilt~~ **ALREADY BUILT, in exactly the shape the proposal "recommended"**

`offerNext` (`waitlists.js:140`) offers the spot to the first in line **with a TTL**, a sweep expires
unanswered offers, and `registrations.js:713` fires it on withdrawal. The registration flow's own
copy says *"we'll offer you the next open spot."* The proposal's open question ("auto-register or
offer that expires?") was answered years-of-code ago: offer that expires. **Nothing to build; worth
a tester pass to confirm it behaves as designed, nothing more.**

### P-C. Cancel an event and tell everyone — **HALF RIGHT, and the missing half is real and QUEUED**

The Cancel button exists (`admin-event.js:50`) and series-cancel exists (`events_admin.js:197`) —
both flip `status='cancelled'` and **tell nobody**. No notification insert or email send touches
either cancel site, verified by reading both. **The missing half is exactly Cathy's workflow**
(cancel a session when not enough people sign up — the people signed up need to hear). Queued as
**§-0 B-list work**: one helper called from BOTH cancel sites, notification rows for active
registrants always, email where addresses exist, **and with no mail key set it must say plainly that
nothing was emailed** (the T2-2 honesty rule).

### P-D. Seasons / recurring — ~~unbuilt~~ **THE ENGINE IS BUILT AND IN THE UI**

`createRecurring` (weekly / biweekly / monthly, `events_admin.js:162`), series edit-this-and-future,
series cancel-this-and-future, `events.series_id` + `recurrence_json`, and the event screen already
shows *"Part of a recurring series."* **What the engine does NOT do is the history half** — nothing
links a member's play across seasons, nothing carries standings forward, and `series_id` is a
scheduling identity, not a sporting one. That half remains real, is the substrate for K-1 tier 1's
"returning team", and still needs an owner direction before design.

### P-E. The day sheet — ~~stands as proposed; the only one of the five that was truly unbuilt~~ **SHIPPED v0.163.0**

The printed artifact a tournament desk holds: schedule, pools and bracket on one page. No new data.
**Approved 2026-08-10 with the rest; now the only P-item that is a build from scratch.**
*(Shipped as a print MODE of Tournament Ops — see §-0 row 19's strike for the shape. "One page"
was read as one PRINT JOB with a page break per section, the pool sheet's own standings-break
precedent; a 30-team day on one literal sheet was never legible.)*

### The lesson, so it is not paid twice (D-26)

A grep with a broken alternation returns the same "nothing" as a grep over code that lacks the
feature — **a search that cannot match anything is the search-tool version of a fixture that cannot
exhibit the defect.** The fix is the same negative-control discipline the tests use: before trusting
"no matches" from any verification grep, run the same pattern against a string it MUST match, and
prefer `grep -E "a|b"` (unquoted-pipe alternation) over `"a\|b"`, which under `-E` is literal.
**Also recorded:** the D-4 route-reachability ratchet scans `/api/admin/*` shapes only, so
built-but-uncalled routes outside that prefix — like `/api/events/:id/duplicate`'s siblings — are
invisible to it; widening that corpus is its own unit.

## §-1o. SIGN-UP SHEETS / DROP-IN SESSIONS — scoped from the real user (owner request 2026-08-10, v1.25)

Owner: *"Review signupgenius.com modules, and see if we can build something similar for
registration/events program like that similar to a drop-in session sign up for someone to evaluate.
Cathy Mitchell emails to me provide the user context and how she uses it to cancel events if not
enough sign ups and etc. Plan and scope this if this not definitive information."*

### The user context, read from the actual emails (owner's inbox, with his direction)

Cathy M (`javatime_30@msn.com`) runs senior/masters volleyball at the owner's facility, and the
correspondence gives the whole requirement set:

- **She uses SignUpGenius today and is dropping it over price** — *"it's the price — $550?/year"*
  (2026-02-28), to which the owner replied, verbatim: *"Can you look for an alternative to the
  system that we can use?"* **This feature request is six months old and came from the owner
  himself.**
- **The count is the decision.** *"What is your count for Sunday?"* → *"9 so far, could likely start
  earlier"* (2026-01-23). She watches individual sign-ups against a threshold and decides
  go / no-go / start-time from it. **Cancel-if-under-threshold is a COUNT feature plus P-C's
  notify-on-cancel — not a new system.**
- **A public link she can advertise is the product.** *"Would you please create a
  registration/info link for the August tournament? We want to advertise in various places… and
  need a link back"* (2026-03-02). Her SignUpGenius sheets are public URLs
  (`signupgenius.com/go/…`) pasted into emails and Facebook groups.
- **Division-structured sheets for tournaments** ("Suggested divisions for August Mile High
  Classic", with the signup as a SignUpGenius link) and **self-run round robins of 4–8 teams** and
  **drop-in skills/drills sessions on Tuesdays** are the recurring shapes.

### What already exists toward this (measured, with D-26's positive-control discipline)

The events system already has: types `training`/`event` beside league/tournament ·
`capacity` · a public registration widget (`signup-widget.js`) · waitlist with offer-TTL ·
per-event registration counts · the recurring-series engine (weekly Tuesdays is literally
`createRecurring`). **What a drop-in sheet needs that does NOT exist:** individual (not team)
slot sign-up against a visible count, a zero-price sign-up path that skips payment entirely, a
public sheet page readable without login showing WHO is coming (first name + initial), and the
under-threshold cancel flow (P-C + a threshold field).

### The scope, three units, smallest first — NOT started until the owner reads this section

**SG-1 — the drop-in sheet. ✅ SHIPPED v0.132.0** (free AND priced per the Q5 rider, not
price-0-only as first scoped). An event of type `training`/`event` with `price_cents` 0 gets a
public sheet: capacity, live count, the names of those coming ("First L."), one-tap sign-up for a
signed-in member and a name+email form for a guest. This is mostly the existing registration path
with payment skipped and the list made public.
~~**SG-2 — the threshold.** One additive column (`events.min_signups`), the count surfaced against it
on the admin event screen ("9 of 12 needed"), and P-C's cancel-and-notify wired so the decision
Cathy makes by email thread becomes one screen and one button.~~ **SHIPPED v0.154.0 (iteration 82),
exactly as scoped — this block HELD in every particular.** Migration 0049; the count is
`activeRegistrationCount` (already built — the capacity gate's own number, in rows; the cancel
notice counts people, both pinned); the line is quiet when no minimum is set; no sweep and no
auto-cancel — the operator decides, the button notifies (B16). `min_signups` joined patchEvent's
allow-list in the same unit so the new field never wore D-34's silent drop; the old two fields
(price, capacity) still do — D-34/D-35 remain their own queued unit.
**SG-3 — the organizer view for a non-staff host.** Cathy is not Boomtown staff. Today only staff
see registration lists. A per-event "host" grant (view the sheet's list, message its sign-ups,
trigger the cancel flow for THAT event only) is the piece that lets her self-serve — **and it is the
first new authorization concept since roles, so it gets its own spec moment with the owner.**

**Explicitly NOT in scope:** payments on sheets (her events are free or cash), custom fields per
sheet (member_fields exists if ever needed), and anything resembling SignUpGenius's ads/themes.

### REVISED BY THE OWNER, 2026-08-10 22:03 — the sheets grew into THE EVENTS PROGRAM (v1.26)

Owner, verbatim: *"We continued with Cathy's signup account for another year in the short term, but
I would like an easier system. My belief is have this module for sign up event (as an added event)
with public guest list feature basically that is sent to a specific demographic (her age group is
40+) and then distribute or announce to specific selection, this can be made in the events area for
example. This would be managed under an events management page (similar to facebook event invite).
Cathy can be given a specific admin access to access event manager as a role and have to that and
tournament system and maybe league system (perhaps toggles for that account role). The events can
be free or to be charged — this basically designs a more robust events module and is then displayed
on the calendar."*

**What this changes, read carefully:**
- **The urgency is GONE** — SignUpGenius is paid for another year. The sheets stop being a rescue
  and become a program to build properly. SG-1/SG-2 stand as scoped.
- ~~**NEW SG-4 — targeted distribution.**~~ **SHIPPED v0.153.0.** Segments gain an AGE BAND
  (`age_min`/`age_max` — the owner's "40+" is one end of a band, so both ends shipped from birth;
  a contradictory band drops BOTH ends rather than guessing). The band is validated at BOTH gates
  (cleanFilter at write, buildSegmentWhere again at read — stored filter_json can predate the
  clean), computed as bound year-offsets against `member_profiles.date_of_birth`, one EXISTS per
  band. **The coverage honesty is the ONE reach judgement:** the counter returns
  `{count, no_birthdate}` where `no_birthdate` is AXIS-SCOPED — contacts passing the filter's
  OTHER axes that the age filter cannot judge — and it is zero when no age axis is active (an
  honesty line on every segment would train the operator to ignore it). The sentence reaches the
  segment list, the preview AND the composer's send screen, which was the owner's requirement in
  as many words. **Live D1 at build (2026-08-14): 49 contacts, 1 profile, 0 birthdates — today
  the honesty line IS the feature**, or a 40+ send reading "0 people" looks broken. Original
  caution, kept: birthdate coverage is sparse, and a 40+ segment can only reach members who have
  one (*empty and broken look identical*).
- ~~**NEW SG-5 — the events management page** ("similar to facebook event invite"): create → target →
  announce → watch the guest list. Mostly composition of things that exist (event CRUD,
  announcements, the new sheet, segments); its value is being ONE screen.~~ **SHIPPED v0.156.0
  (iteration 84), by composition — as this line predicted.** The ONE screen was already the
  manager hub (WF-5, owner-approved); it grew **Overview** (admin-event.html framed — details,
  share link, SG-2's count line) and **Announce** (admin-marketing.html framed — W-F's `?event=`
  hand-off given a tab) for EVERY event type, so a drop-in shows face / sign-ups / megaphone and
  the hub is Cathy's whole screen. The 23:09 messaging requirement shipped as **B16's promised
  second caller**: `POST /api/events/:id/notify` — ONE recipient selection (`activeRegistrantsOf`,
  extracted, spelled once), the operator's words as the body, keyless-honest, honest zero,
  refusals in sentences, audited. "Create" stays on Events & Programs, which already links every
  event's manager.
- **SG-3 IS CONFIRMED AND SHAPED BY THE OWNER HIMSELF:** *"a specific admin access … as a role …
  perhaps toggles for that account role"* — **per-ACCOUNT module toggles.** Cathy: event manager +
  tournaments, *maybe* leagues. **v0.128.0 built the registry keys-first precisely so this can hang
  off a user grant** (`BT_MODULES` keys are the vocabulary; a `user_module_grants` shape reuses
  them). **The hard part is unchanged and must be said at spec time:** ~190 `requireStaff` gates
  admit ANY staff today (§-1e S-2's measurement), so "Cathy as staff with a trimmed menu" would be
  a VIEW of less and ACCESS to everything — the per-account grant must be enforced SERVER-SIDE at
  the gates, which is the first real authorization change since roles. It deserves its own unit
  with the authorization_matrix harness extended, not a rider.
- **Free or charged** — the sheet path merges with the paid registration path behind one create
  flow; already how the scope reads, now explicit.
- **NEW SG-6 — calendar display.** ICS feeds exist (`calendar.js`, admin-calendar.html); ~~what
  does NOT exist is a visual calendar page of upcoming events~~ **— MEASURED FALSE (iteration 83)
  and RESOLVED v0.155.0.** The visual calendar page had existed since before this brief was
  written: `schedule.html`'s Month view is a full month grid with navigation, in the member site
  nav, fed by the public view profile whose live `type_filter` is NULL — so every published type
  (drop-ins included) already lands on it, and its empty state is real. This line was queued
  unmeasured; K-14 (v0.145.0) had already recorded the List|Month switcher as "already here."
  **What was genuinely missing — "the natural place the announced events land" — was a URL:**
  the view toggle was client-side only, so nothing could open the calendar directly. v0.155.0
  ships the landing: `?mode=month` opens the Month view (a whitelist — the renderer's
  else-branch would treat a typo as the calendar), the toggle keeps the URL honest via
  replaceState, and a deep link lights the Month tab. **SG-5's spec should treat
  `schedule.html?mode=month` as THE calendar surface** — a second calendar page would be the
  styled-twice defect.

**Revised order: SG-1 sheet → SG-4 targeting (with the coverage honesty) → SG-2 threshold+cancel
(needs P-C/B16 anyway) → SG-6 calendar → SG-5 the one-screen manager → SG-3 the scoped role (spec
moment FIRST — it is the security-sensitive one and nothing above depends on it).**

### ANSWERED 2026-08-10 23:09 — the SG-3 boundary, and SG-5's messaging requirement (v1.27)

Owner: *"She can see the emails and phone number. That screen also needs to be able to contact and
email the participants with information or news."*

- **SG-3's data boundary is SETTLED: full contact details** (emails and phone numbers), not the
  names-and-counts fallback that had been recommended. The registrations CSV and list already carry
  email/phone for staff, so her scoped role changes WHICH modules answer her, not what those
  modules show. The spec moment now has one question left — the module set (events + tournaments ±
  leagues) — and the enforcement rule is unchanged: server-side at the gates, or a trimmed menu is
  a view of less and access to everything.
- **SG-5 gains its second confirmed requirement: message/email an event's participants from the
  event screen.** **The substrate SHIPPED in v0.129.0:** `notifyEventCancelled` selects an event's
  active registrants (one message per member, statuses from the schema's CHECK constraint, honest
  keyless email reporting) — the generic "send these people some news" caller reuses that recipient
  selection with its own title/body, plus the mail-key honesty rule verbatim. **B16 was built
  one-helper-first precisely so SG-5 is a second caller, not a second implementation.**

## §-1p. OWNER BRIEF 2026-08-11 12:52 — workflow, calendar, brackets, waivers (added v1.31, MEASURED)

Owner, verbatim (8 items): *"1. Events and pRograms, calendar collapses adn not even rows. 2.
Registration -> Divisions -> Pool -> Bracket do not flow into one another. 3. Bracket lists all
unused and empty bracket, it should not do that only display the ones that are active. 4. Onpools,
divisions should be able to also pivot to horizontal vs vertical options. 5. Instead of vertical
menus for these, please make the horizontal tab underneath each event as it is created. 6. Workflow
should be built as follows -> Events and Programs -> Create event as an program and on the calendar
-> Create the registration -> this appears on the schedule and then creates a tournament or league
management. Registrations should have all the events and registrationslisted for easy access and
finanacial review. Once each event is created, under a singular section - there is a tournament or
league management page - then within that page - there are tabs with the modules on top to smooth
out navigation. Each event will have its own instance of registration -> tournament or league
ops/mgr. 7. Tournament manager along the top tab/buttons that do not reload - will have
Registrations |Waitlist is a sub section fo registration | Divisions & Create Pools | Scoring Links
should be integrated once pools are created | Schedule editor if needed | Scoring Edit | Live
Scoring Board | Bracket. 8. Registration should check against signed waivers for each team. And
mark or flash waivers not completed and auto send waivers to them then also send email."*

**Measured before queueing (the habit is now 7 for 7 on owner briefs). Prefix `WF-` grepped free
before numbering (D-25's rule; first candidate `UX-` was TAKEN by the 2026-08-01 review backlog).**

- **WF-1 (item 1) — the Events & Programs page, two CONFIRMED defects.** (a) The calendar has no
  per-day cap and no uniform rows: `.cal-day` min-height 96px but a busy day stacks `.cal-ev`
  nodes unbounded, stretching its whole grid row — busy weeks are 3× empty weeks ("not even
  rows"). Fix = cap visible events per day + a "+N more" opener + `grid-auto-rows: 1fr`.
  (b) FOUND MEASURING (a): **the Views & Embed tab is DEAD** — `admin-events.js` `renderViews()`
  (line 331) and `viewModal()` (line 355) reference a global `orgs` that NO script on the page
  defines (admin-nav.js's `orgs` at :724 is closure-scoped; positive-controlled grep across all
  three loaded scripts). Every `loadAll()` ends in an unhandled `ReferenceError`; the calendar and
  list render first, so the page looks alive while the Views table stays empty. As old as the
  v0.52.0 org-switcher consolidation. Both halves are ONE unit (same page, same file).
- **WF-2 (item 3) — brackets: CONFIRMED, live evidence.** 11 live matchless `brackets` rows render
  as empty trees (event 90006 carries TEN — five stranded A/BB generation pairs; 90003 one).
  Mechanism, from the code: `generateBracketFor` INSERTs bracket rows BEFORE writing matches with
  no transaction, and the replace-cleanup fires only when live MATCHES exist (`existing.n > 0`) —
  a mid-flight failure strands the rows and the next attempt never cleans them. (The sandbox wipe
  is NOT the culprit — `DELETE FROM brackets` IS in WIPE_SQL, checked.) Fix = self-healing
  generation (soft-delete live-but-matchless bracket rows for the event before inserting) +
  `loadBrackets` returns only trees that have live matches. **The empty state + generate panel
  stay the way in — a filter that hides everything must not delete the last way out.**
- **WF-3 (item 4) — pool board divisions pivot: HALF-BUILT.** The board already has the
  segmented-control + `data-view` attribute mechanism (T2-8 shipped it for the WAITING AREA:
  bottom vs side). The ask extends the same mechanism to the pools/divisions axis (row vs column
  layout). Extend, do not invent.
- **WF-4 (item 8) — registrations × waivers: HALF-BUILT, the sweep already exists.**
  `waiverReminderSweep` (cron, daily) already selects rosters of events starting within 14 days
  with no live waiver — via `WAIVER_IDENTITY_MATCH` + `WAIVER_LIVE_PREDICATE` (the door gate's own
  pair), dedupes on a 2-day notification window, writes in-app rows, and is keyless-honest about
  email. What does NOT exist: (a) waiver status in the staff registrations LIST (`listRegistrations`
  returns no waiver fields — measured iteration 59); (b) an on-demand "send waiver requests now"
  button on that screen. Fix = surface the SAME predicate as status chips (one judgement, never a
  second copy) + an on-demand caller of the sweep's selection. "Auto send email" exists and is
  keyless-honest today — production has no mail key, so the response must say what was NOT emailed.
- **WF-5 (items 2+5+6+7) — THE PER-EVENT MANAGER HUB: CONFIRMED UNBUILT, and it is a PROGRAM, not
  a unit.** Today every module is its own rail page; tournament.js is one page with an event
  `<select>`; nothing is per-event-URL except admin-event.html. The ask: one manager page per
  event with horizontal top tabs that do NOT reload — Registrations (Waitlist as a subsection) ·
  Divisions & Create Pools · Scoring Links (integrated once pools exist) · Schedule editor ·
  Scoring Edit · Live Scoring Board · Bracket — reached from Events & Programs after creation;
  the top-level Registrations page becomes the all-events financial overview ("easy access and
  financial review" — the goal clause is the requirement). **Design moment FIRST**: this is the
  biggest IA change since the admin rail; the existing pages become the tab CONTENTS (reuse, not
  rewrite), and the tab shell must not fork any module's logic. Sequence after the design moment:
  shell + 2 tabs (Registrations, Divisions/Pools) → remaining tabs → the financial overview.

**Queue effect (v1.31):** WF-1 and WF-2 are defect-shaped and enter the queue FIRST (B23, B24);
WF-3/WF-4 follow (B25, B26); WF-5 is queued as a design moment + program (B27) — the owner's brief
outranks the pre-existing B4-first order, and B4 (live-board tiles) folds naturally into WF-5's
"Live Scoring Board" tab when the hub arrives.

### WF-5 DESIGN — the per-event manager hub. **A PROPOSAL AWAITING APPROVAL** (added v1.37, 2026-08-12)

**Nothing here is built. This section is the thing to approve or change; the build starts after.**

#### What was measured first (every claim below has a file:line)

| Tab, in the owner's order (item 7) | The page that becomes it | Already per-event? | Its own event picker | Page-local `<style>` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Registrations | `admin-registrations.html` | no | `#eventSelect` (`admin-registrations.js:46-48`) | 11 lines |
| └ Waitlist (subsection) | `admin-waitlists.html` | **yes — reads `?event=`** | `#wlEvent` | 25 lines |
| Divisions & Create Pools | `admin-divisions.html` + `admin-pool-board.html` | no | `#dvEvent` · `#pbEvent` | 54 · **208** lines |
| Scoring Links | `admin-score-links.html` | no | `#lEvent` | 28 lines |
| Schedule editor | `admin-schedule-editor.html` | **yes — `?event=`** | `#sEvent` | 52 lines |
| Scoring Edit | `tournament.html` (Ops: grid + `openScoreSheet`, `tournament.js:257,268`) | no | `#eventSelect` | **0** — uses shared `tournament.css` |
| Live Scoring Board | `live.html` | **yes — `?event=`** | — | member-side page (`app.css`+`tokens.css`, no rail) |
| Bracket | `admin-brackets.html` | **yes — `?event=`** | `#bEvent` | 122 lines |

Four facts fall out of that table, and they decide the design:

1. **Seven of nine surfaces carry their own event `<select>`.** The hub does not need to invent event
   selection; it needs to *remove* seven copies of it from the operator's path.
2. **Four already accept `?event=` in the URL.** The remaining five need the same three lines those
   four already have. That is the only edit to any existing page, and it is additive — every page
   still works standalone from the rail, which is what makes this reversible.
3. **Every tab's content owns page-local CSS, one of them 208 lines** (`admin-pool-board.html`), and
   one tab is a *member-side* page with a different stylesheet set (`live.html`). **Standards §11
   forbids a shared script leaning on one page's styles**, and mounting seven pages' markup into one
   document would put 500+ lines of page-local CSS into one cascade.
4. **`admin-event.html` (`?id=N`, `admin-event.js`) has NO page-local style at all** and is already
   the per-event URL. It is a summary card page, not a container.

#### The mechanism — recommended: **each tab is the existing page, in a same-origin iframe, chromeless**

**Both halves of this mechanism already ship in this repo.** `schedule.html?embed=1` sets a body
class and posts its own height (`schedule.js:10-11, 101-107`), and `web/widget.js:19-30` is the
parent: it creates the iframe, listens for `bt_widget_height`, filters by `slug` so several frames
on one page do not fight, and sizes the frame. The hub's shell is the second consumer of a parent
that exists. Same origin means the session token in `localStorage` is shared — no new auth path.

- **Reuse is literal.** The tab content is the page, byte for byte. No page's logic is forked,
  copied or wrapped, so a fix to the Pool Board is a fix in the Pool Board.
- **§11 is satisfied structurally, not by discipline.** 208 lines of pool-board CSS cannot reach the
  Bracket tab because they are in a different document. This is the "structurally impossible beats
  refused" rule applied to the cascade.
- **The member-side Live board embeds on the same terms as the admin pages** — the one tab that does
  not share the admin stylesheet is not a special case.
- **Chromeless is ONE rule set, not seven edits:** the rail is static markup inside every admin page
  (45 rail nodes in `admin-divisions.html` alone), so `body.embed .admin-rail, body.embed .header
  { display: none }` in `admin.css` hides it everywhere at once.

**What it costs, said plainly.** Seven iframes are seven document loads (cached CSS/JS after the
first); the browser Back button moves within a tab, so the shell owns history via the URL hash; and
deep keyboard focus crossing a frame boundary needs testing on the tab row. **The alternative
considered and rejected: fetch-and-mount.** It reads cheaper until you count what it forces —
injecting each page's `<style>`, re-running scripts that bind at boot (D-6's handler-stacking is
exactly this failure), and one id collision already exists today (`#eventSelect` is used by BOTH
`admin-registrations.html` and `tournament.html`). Rewriting nine pages into mountable modules is
the "rewrite" the owner's brief rules out.

#### The page

`admin-manager.html?event=N` — one manager per event, reached from Events & Programs after
creation. **It grows FROM `admin-event.html` rather than replacing it:** that page is already the
per-event URL and already carries the summary, the public sign-up link and the KOTC session, with
no page-local CSS to migrate. Proposal: `admin-event.html` becomes the hub's **first tab strip
header** (event name, date, status, the advertised link) and the tab row sits under it. The old URL
keeps working; the rail keeps every page it has today.

**The tab row is not new UI.** `.tabs`/`.tab` is already a shared `admin.css` component
(`:138-143`) with the 44px floor and an active state, used with `role="tablist"` on
`admin-events.html:105-108` and again on `schedule.html:54-56`. Tabs switch by hash
(`#registrations`), never reload the shell, and the frame for a tab is created on first visit and
kept — so returning to a tab is instant and no page reloads (item 7's requirement).

#### Tab visibility by event type — the default, said out loud

Reuse `BT_MODULES` (P-1, v0.128.0) vocabulary where it fits; per **event type** the default is:

| Type | Tabs shown |
|---|---|
| `tournament` | all eight |
| `league` | Registrations · Waitlist · Divisions & Pools · Schedule editor · Scoring Edit · Live board *(no Bracket until one is generated)* |
| `training` / `event` (drop-in, SG-1) | Registrations · Waitlist only — a sheet needs no pools, no bracket, no scoring |
| `tryout` | Registrations · Waitlist · Divisions & Pools |

**A tab is hidden, never disabled** — except Bracket and Scoring Links, which are *shown with their
own empty state* on tournaments, because the owner's item 7 says Scoring Links "should be integrated
once pools are created" and WF-2 already proved a filter that hides everything must not delete the
last way out.

#### The top-level Registrations page becomes the all-events financial overview

Item 6's goal clause — *"easy access and financial review"* — is the requirement, and **the query
already exists.** `reports.js:184-201` (`revenueCsv`) returns, per event, exactly: event name, type,
`starts_at`, program, `card_cents` (Square COMPLETED), `cash_cents` (cash/comped at event price),
and `registrations` (active statuses), ordered by date. Today it renders only as a CSV download.
**So the overview is a rendering job, not a data job:** add a JSON sibling of the same SELECT (one
query, two renderers — the repo's one-judgement rule) and the screen is columns —
**Event · Date · Type · Program · Registered · Paid (card) · Paid (cash) · Total · →** where the
arrow opens that event's hub. WF-4's waiver chips already live on the per-event list and stay there.

#### B4/K-7 folds in here, explicitly

`§-0 B4` (K-7 live-board tiles) becomes **the Live tab's** work and is struck as a standalone item
when the hub ships. K-7's measurement stands: `live.js` already polls every 25s and **diffs against
the previous payload with a stable row identity** (`live.js:141, 228, 288`) — *do not rebuild the
polling*; the tile design and K-1/K-2's numbers are the unbuilt part.

#### Build sequence — four shippable units, each a release on its own

| Unit | What ships | Size |
|---|---|---|
| **H-1** | The shell: `admin-manager.html?event=N`, hash tabs over the existing `.tabs` component, `?embed=1` chromeless rule in `admin.css`, the height-posting contract lifted from `schedule.js`/`widget.js` into one shared helper, **plus the first two tabs — Registrations (with Waitlist as its subsection) and Divisions & Pools** | M |
| **H-2** | The remaining five tabs + the `?event=` preselect added to the five pages that lack it + type-based visibility | M |
| **H-3** | The all-events financial overview (JSON sibling of the revenue SELECT + the columns above) | S |
| **H-4** | The Live tab's tiles (B4/K-7) | M |

**Approval buys this plan, not a promise: H-1 is the next session's unit if the answer is yes.**

#### APPROVED 2026-08-12 · H-1 SHIPPED v0.139.0 — and one amendment made at build time

**The owner approved the design as written.** H-1 shipped: `admin-manager.html?event=N`, the tab
row over the shared `.tabs` component in his order (all seven declared, the two built ones
rendered — an unbuilt tab is ABSENT, never a dead button), hash routing with frames created once
and kept, Registrations (+Waitlist sub-tab) and Divisions & Pools (+Create Pools), chromeless as
ONE `body.embed` rule set in `admin.css`, and an ADDITIVE `?event=` preselect so every tab page
still works standalone from the rail.

**THE ONE AMENDMENT, and why it was made rather than asked about.** The design said *"put the
shared half of the embed contract in ONE helper and make both the widget and the hub call it."*
Measuring the loaders killed that: **`web/widget.js` is a drop-in `<script>` served to EXTERNAL
customer sites** (boomtownvb.com, coloradoboom.com) and cannot import from this repo without adding
a network request to someone else's page. So the CONTRACT is shared and the implementations are
two — the member/external pair (`schedule.js` posts, `widget.js` listens) and the admin pair
(`admin-nav.js` posts, the hub listens). **What prevents drift is a test, not a file:**
`manager_hub.test.mjs` asserts the message key `bt_widget_height` is identical across all four,
which is the only place that judgement can live. One judgement, one home — the home is just a guard
instead of a module.

**H-2 SHIPPED v0.140.0.** The five remaining tabs, and two findings worth keeping:

**(a) THE APPROVED DESIGN NAMED AN EVENT TYPE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** Its visibility table carried a
`tryout` row. `events.type` is CHECK-constrained to `tournament, league, training, event,
court_rental` — tryouts are their own module, not a kind of event. The guard now DERIVES the type
list from the schema and asserts the visibility map matches it exactly, which is what caught it; a
hardcoded list would have shipped a rule nobody could ever reach. The shipped map: tournament and
league see every tab · training, event (SG-1's drop-in sheet types) and court_rental see
Registrations only · **Bracket stays visible on a league** rather than appearing "once one is
generated", because WF-2 proved a filter that hides everything can delete the only way back in and
`admin-brackets`' empty state plus its Generate panel IS that way in.

**(b) THE SCORING TAB FORKS BY TYPE AT THE PANE, NOT THE TAB.** His item 6 says "a tournament or
league management page"; the scoring surface is `tournament.html` for one and `admin-league.html`
for the other. A pane carries `types: [...]` and the tab stays single — one tab, the right screen
behind it.

**(c) TWO THINGS MOVED, BECAUSE THE LIVE TAB IS A MEMBER PAGE.** `live.html` loads `app.css` and
`site-nav.js` and has never loaded the admin shell, so H-1's arrangement could not reach it. The
embed CHILD moved from `admin-nav.js` to `config.js` (the only script both shells load — which is
why `BT_SIGNUP_LINK` is already there), and the `body.embed` rule set moved from `admin.css` to
`app.css` (linked by every page in `web/`) with the member rail's selector added. A second copy
for the member shell would have been a third implementation of one message and a second rule set
for one concept — D-23 and D-24's class exactly.

**H-3 SHIPPED v0.141.0 — and the premise was already true, which is the finding.**

The unit was queued as *"add a JSON sibling of the revenue SELECT — one query, two renderers."*
**Both already existed.** The SELECT lives in `sales()` behind `GET /api/admin/reports/sales`, and
`revenueCsv` calls that function and renders its JSON as the CSV — its own comment says *"Same
source of truth as sales()."* The earlier measurement cited `:184-201` as being inside
`revenueCsv`; those lines are inside `sales`. **So H-3 added no route and no query, and instead
PINNED the property** (`revenueCsv` must call `sales()` and must not grow its own `FROM events`,
with a re-inlining NC): an unpinned true thing is one refactor away from being a false one.

**What shipped is the screen.** The top-level Registrations page's no-event state was *"Pick an
event above."*; it is now **Event · Date · Type · Program · Registered · Paid (card) · Paid (cash) ·
Total · Manage →**, with a totals row, the arrow opening `admin-manager.html?event=N`.

**A MODE, not a new page** — the rail's Registrations entry should land on it (item 6), and a new
page would need a rail slot beside the one that already means "registrations". The mode is keyed on
the `?event=` parameter the hub already passes, so the hub's Registrations tab is untouched and
clearing the picker returns to the overview. The status filters hide while the overview shows: with
no event chosen they act on nothing.

**An existing guard caught a live defect in the change before it shipped** — `hidden_overlay`
failed on `.filters`, which carries an author `display: flex` rule, so `hidden` would have painted
them anyway (the v0.119.0 lesson). Fixed with the `.br-pick[hidden]` precedent that guard's own
negative control names.

**H-4 SHIPPED v0.142.0 — AND THE HUB PROGRAM IS COMPLETE.**

K-7's measurement held on re-measure (the first recorded claim in six iterations that did): the
page already polled every 25 seconds and diffed against the previous payload with a stable row
identity, and none of that was touched. **What was unbuilt was the tile** — the score rendered at
the same size as the team name, and an unstarted game rendered an EMPTY STRING where its number
goes, which at thirty feet is a wall of uniform grey with holes in it.

Shipped: the score as the headline (`clamp(22px, 3.4vw, 40px)` against the name's
`clamp(14px, 1.7vw, 18px)`, with the guard asserting the score's floor exceeds the name's ceiling)
· an en-dash placeholder from one named constant so the tile keeps its shape · the leading side
emphasised by WEIGHT and full-strength text while the trailing side dims, never by colour alone
and never gold as text · **conditional seed chips** from `teams.seed`.

**The seed was measured against live D1 before a chip was drawn: 62 of 70 teams carry one and one
event of six carries none**, so the chip is conditional and the test fixture was given both cases.
**The team NUMBER was deliberately left off** — K-1 tier 3's `board_no` is a correlated subquery
