inside `divisions.js:697`, not a stored column, so rendering it here would copy that judgement
into a second module, and K-1 tier 2 (§-0 B5) will change what the number means.

**Every motion assertion written in v0.084.0 still passes against the redesigned tile**, which is
what extending `live_motion.test.mjs` rather than opening a second file bought.

**The program is done: H-1 → H-2 → H-3 → H-4, four releases, no rewrites of any existing page.**

---

### WF-6 — print ⇒ email + CSV parity (owner rider, 2026-08-11 13:48; added v1.33, MEASURED)

Owner, verbatim: *"Esnure anywhere there is a print, we also have email and download to CSV."*

**Measured — THREE print surfaces exist** (grep positive-controlled against tournament.js's known
button): `tournament.html` "Print pool sheet" · `admin-league.html` "Print schedule" (W-B: "print
is export") · `admin-score-links.html` "Print cards". Current state per surface:

| Surface | CSV | Email |
|---|---|---|
| tournament.html pool sheet | ✅ `csvBtn` ships one already | ✗ |
| admin-league.html weeks | ✗ — has "Copy as text" (paste-ready lines FOR a text/email, W-B) | ✗ direct send |
| admin-score-links.html cards | ✗ — has per-team PNG save (owner 2026-08-03: "for sending by text or email") | ✗ direct send |

So the rule is **one-third built for CSV, zero-thirds for direct email — but two surfaces already
carry a send-it-yourself affordance** the owner asked for by other means earlier. **The email half
IS §-0 B10 (T2-2/K-6 "email the schedule") generalized** — WF-6 absorbs B10 rather than sitting
beside it, and B10's constraint travels: **no mail key is set in production, so every send button
must say honestly what was NOT emailed** (the `remind()` sandbox pattern: return the content, let
the operator copy it). Recipient question (email to WHOM — captains? the operator's own inbox?) is
K-6's open half — take the sensible default at build (active registrants' captains for schedules;
the operator's own address for score cards) and SAY so. **P-E the day sheet (B19), when built,
inherits this rule at birth.** Queued as **B28**; size M (two CSVs are S each; the three send
buttons share one keyless-honest helper).

**SHIPPED v0.138.0 (iteration 65) — and the build measurement changed the shape of the email half.**
The recording above held on re-measure (three surfaces; only the pool sheet had CSV; none had send)
— *though note a `window.print()` grep finds only two of the three, because `tournament.js:333`
calls bare `print()`; the guard matches the call, not the spelling.* What changed is that **the
"ONE keyless-honest send helper" was already written.** `marketing.js` ships event-scoped segments
(W-F, v0.99.0 — `filter.event`), campaigns, and a `sendCampaign` that is already honest about a
missing mail key; `admin-marketing.js` already accepts `?event=` and opens the segment form with
that event chosen, arriving today from the registrations screen. So WF-6 built **one hand-off**
(`BT_ADMIN.emailDocument`: the document into `sessionStorage`, the operator into the composer with
the event pre-selected) rather than a second sender — which is why no assertion in
`print_parity.test.mjs` re-checks the keyless sentence: that judgement has ONE home and a second
sender would have needed its own copy of it. **Recipient default, said out loud: the event's own
registrants**, which is what `?event=` already selects; nothing is sent by pressing the button.
**B10 closed with it, as recorded.** Also added: `BT_ADMIN.csvRow` (one cell-quoting judgement;
`tournament.js`'s hand-rolled escaper retired to it).

OR email=?)` copy, and calendar.js:297 defined its OWN `contactForSession` — the shared helper's exact name, a different body, and the weakest rule of all (email-only, `LIMIT 1`, no `ORDER BY`). index.js's header had claimed six modules were consolidated into it; consent and registrations genuinely were, **the other four never had been, so the comment documented a finished job for nine releases.** **And "case-SENSITIVE" was false at the database:** `contacts.email` and `users.email` are both `COLLATE NOCASE` in live D1 and in the fixture, so `email = ?` never was case-sensitive — no guard asserts case divergence, because it would test a column collation rather than the code. **The real defect was the authority half alone**, and it ran the opposite way from this row's assumption: the private copies matched on `user_id` and were therefore MORE correct than the shared helper, which joined on email alone and could not see a linked contact whose address had been edited. Found while placing B22's print-day Escape handler, which is deliberately independent: it is registered ONCE at boot and gated on the mode, so the two never interact — **CLOSED v0.167.0**, riding D-42's release exactly as this row's trigger prescribed: the inline self-removing handler became a named `onEsc`, and `closeSheet` detaches it, so every close path (Cancel, score, Escape) leaves nothing attached. Pinned by a code-shape assertion in `day_sheet.test.mjs` with an NC — **and the pin says out loud that it is a shape pin**, because the harness cannot reach `openScoreSheet` (wired per rendered cell; `querySelectorAll` is a stub), so a true listener-count test is not available | ~~the next release touching `openScoreSheet`~~ **done** |

Measured: `[^)]*` cannot cross a `)`, so it matches `all.some(o => o.default_org_id)` and **NOT** `all.some((o) => o.id === me.default_org_id)`, nor a `function (o) {…}` callback. Invisible because all 34 consumers assert the PRESENCE of a needle that happened to sit outside an eaten span — the quiet direction is an ABSENCE assertion silently policing 155 fewer lines. B22 guarded both grid files whole (they now hold zero unguarded lines). **Measured with one instrument** (a line touches storage; it is guarded if that line also carries `try {`): **JS modules — 125 lines across 28 files, 26 guarded, 99 UNGUARDED. ## §0. Headline count

**Recounted at v0.86.0 (2026-08-04) — 17 items of real build work remain.** Counted bucket by bucket
against the tree, not carried forward.

> **The number is still 17, and that is a coincidence, not a copy.** The stale v0.57.0 figure was also
> 17. Nine items closed across v0.58.0–v0.86.0 and nine opened — five of them KOTC work that did not
> exist as a category when the old count was taken. If you are checking whether anyone actually
> recounted: the closures and the arithmetic are both below, and this table's rows now sum to its
> total, which the v0.57.0 table's did not (its buckets summed to 19 against a stated 17).

| Bucket | Open at v0.86.0 | Closed since v0.57.0 |
|---|---|---|
| §1 Unbuilt feature modules | **7** | M22 API **+** its admin screen (`web/admin-member-fields.html`, verified in tree) |
| §2 Small feature gaps | **3** | — |
| §2c KOTC / tournament gaps *(new category)* | **3** | league-night board for single players · divisions setup screen (v0.71.0) · KOTC engine (v0.76.0) + all three screens (v0.85.0–v0.86.0) |
| §3 Engineering / quality tracks | **3** | E2E harness (v0.57.0) · admin screens for unreachable APIs (§3.5, closed) |
| §6 Documentation debt | **1** | build-status registry (v0.55.0) · README §Roadmap (v0.86.0) · the phantom "requirements §15" citation (gone from the handoff, verified) |
| **Total build work** | **17** | **9 closed** |
| §4 Owner-gated config | 5 | *not build work — switches only the owner can flip* |
| §4b Owner-gated **build** work — blocked on a migration | 2 | KOTC per-session points cap · net labels. Both **already decided**; a human applies the migration via Cloudflare MCP before the push, and the CI schema-gate fails closed until then. |
| §5 Carried non-code items | 5 | *not build work — excluded from the total. The v0.57.0 table folded these in, which is part of why it did not add up.* |

**The 7 open in §1:** M-TF remainder (third-place and consolation games, double elimination, Swiss,
ladder, multi-day split) · M17 achievements & public standings (the v0.73.0 public scoreboard is the
scoreboard, **not** season achievements — there is no achievements module in `worker/src/`) · M20
lessons/clinics/camps · M21 auto-scheduler v1 · M12C public rental booking · R-04 installment payments
· M18′ roster RSVP.

**The 3 open in §2c**, in handoff §6 order: withdraw a player for the night (`kotc_players.withdrawn_at`
exists and is read, never written) · redistribute when a net empties · both teams see and can dispute
a bracket result (**carries a design question — do not build it unasked**).

**Handoff §6 remains the live next-build order.** This section is the inventory; §6 is the queue.

**Nothing in the core operating loop is missing.** Register → pay → schedule → check in → play →
score → notify is complete, and since v0.57.0 it is covered end to end by a harness that drives the
real router. Everything below is an extension, a revenue add, a polish pass, or a switch to flip.

Two of the six remaining modules are gated on someone other than a developer: **M-TF** is
Fable-gated, and **M12C** needs Square in production plus the org-10 confirm. The genuinely
unblocked module queue is therefore **four**.

## §-1r. OWNER REFINEMENT LIST 2026-08-18 — 12 items, MEASURED (added v1.73, iteration 109)

Owner, verbatim (12 items): *"1. Pool sheet - needs to be visible from 1 screen during print. print
shoud only print pool sheet not entire site. The pool sheet neesd to be its own frame, and allow to
view or shrink to get on 1 page. The print button should be next to the pool sheet, same with the
email. Email should only email registrants, not anyone else, so it does not need an elaborate email
system. The emails should be ready as a bcc. THe tournament should also be able to push the teams
into bracket on the same page, right now when the button is to bracket teams, nothing happens. 2.
League manager needs to add option for 2 rounds per week night, so 2 sets of matches or 3. Please add
that to the court page. Add button to jump to week to get to that week, and add a button to go back
to top. Make the right side boxes, team level and etc collapsable as they may not always be necessary
or needed. 3. The Court editor on the league page needs to be made more clear and easier. Select the
week first, then present the teams. allow for teams to be dropped or forfeit. add a note that this
team has played together prior (last week recency bias) and denote not to do that but can be ignored
if necessary. Then review the future weeks and denote the upcoming week schedule in the same regards.
Sometimes team drop or get added, so we need to rearrange. So add the option to add teams and remove
etams from the schedule, then it will replace the current league's week scores and add / remove
etams. Teams that are removed or forfeit receive losses. Review the print schedule in the league, and
make it more visibly appealing when the scheudle is printed for each week, there should be a print
button on each week to print that specific week. 4. The left side tiles still exist like shedule
editor/brackets/etc and tehy shoud be housed in the modules not standalone on the side. 5. Pool
board- add option to remove pools after it is created. This needs to be exported then into the
tournament system to record points and then match ups then move into bracket. 6. Court board is not
working. 7. Events and registration, the calendar boxe are still not correct. 8. Admin login still
bear and wrong. 9. Color toggle still not working fully. 10. Subfinder is its button, not under
league. League should present league ifnormation that night, if they are playing and who they play
under hte member view. 11. Scheudle is not correct name, this hsould be called Event Schedule, where
they sign up. They may need their own schedule but that can stay under league and tournaments
buttons. The need an achievements and history (events participated) button. 12. Home button on member
view seems reduandant vs dashboard. Dashboard should be the primary screen for members. Review the
order of buttons for utiliation optimization."*

**MEASURED BEFORE QUEUEING — nine readers, and the adversarial pass overturned four of the twelve
first verdicts.** Prefix `RF-` grepped free before numbering (D-25's rule). Every code claim below
carries `file:line`; every roadmap citation was resolved by CONTENT, not by line number (iteration
106's lesson). **Three verdicts came back saying the report is partly wrong, and two of those say a
capability he asked for is ALREADY BUILT AND INVISIBLE — which makes the screen the defect, not the
feature.**

- **RF-1 (item 1) — the pool sheet. PARTIALLY-BUILT, and the print half is BROADER than he said.**
  The pool sheet is not on the pool board: it is `#gridPanel` + `#standingsPanel` on
  `web/tournament.html:159-173`. **`admin.css` and `app.css` contain ZERO `@media print` rules** —
  the only print CSS in the whole tree is `tournament.css:82-98`, which hides `.no-print, .header`
  and nothing else, while `admin.css:12` makes `.admin-layout` a `216px 1fr` grid. **So the admin
  rail genuinely prints, and it also steals 216px of sheet width.** The precedent is two pages over
  and does the right thing — `admin-league.html:40-46` and `admin-score-links.html:37-44` both open
  their print block with `.sidebar { display:none !important }` + `.admin-main { padding:0 }`.
  **The day-sheet print MODE (`tournament.js:539-562` + `tournament.css:66-97`) is on this same page
  and works, but it does not suppress the rail either** — so the shipped precedent is a partial one.
  No `@page`, no scale/fit control, no pool-sheet frame; `tournament.css:89` deliberately breaks the
  page before standings. Print/CSV/email all sit in `.toolbar` at `tournament.html:99`, ~55 lines
  and one whole grid above the sheet. Email already reaches registrants only (the `?event=` segment
  preselect, `admin-nav.js:711-713`) but does it by NAVIGATING to the campaign composer
  (`admin-nav.js:717-724`); **`bcc` appears nowhere in the repo — zero hits across .js/.html/.mjs/.sql.**
  **"Nothing happens" on the bracket button is REFUTED AS WIRING AND REAL AS EXPERIENCE, and this is
  the sharpest finding in the brief:** every hop exists (`tournament.html:170` → `tournament.js:402-411`
  → `POST /api/admin/events/:id/brackets` → `brackets.js:983-1012`, mounted `index.js:597`) and the
  bracket really is written — but the handler's only visible effect is
  `$("warningsBox").innerHTML` at `tournament.js:408-410`, and `#warningsBox` is at
  `tournament.html:157`, **ABOVE the tall Court×Round grid and above the button that was pressed**.
  There is no `scrollIntoView` and no `.focus()` anywhere in `tournament.js`. **The second press is
  strictly silent:** `brackets.js:826-833` answers 409 unless `replace:true`, and Tournament Ops
  never sends it or confirms — while `admin-brackets.js:349-358`, on the same route, confirms,
  re-POSTs with `replace` and redraws. Same route, two behaviours; the one he uses is the mute one.
  **→ (f) SHIPPED v0.175.0 (iteration 116), both halves:** on 409 the press confirms with the
  engine's own sentence and re-POSTs with `replace:true` (the `generate()`/`plCommit` behaviour,
  through ONE `gen()` writer site so bracket_rewire's uniqueness pin kept its licence), and the
  outcome speaks at `#bracketNote` BESIDE the button (`aria-live="polite"`), never in
  `#warningsBox` a grid above — which stays the schedule generator's voice. Guards:
  bracket_rewire.test.mjs v1.1 (+4, watched red first).
  **Roadmap:** print/email surface §-1p WF-6 (SHIPPED v0.138.0); print-MODE precedent §-1n P-E +
  §-0 row 19 (SHIPPED v0.163.0); bracket rewire §-1j T2-5 ("FULLY CLOSED"). **NOT ON THE ROADMAP:**
  print scoping / rail suppression, the sheet frame, shrink-to-fit, button placement, BCC, and
  rendering the bracket on `tournament.html`. **Build hazard:** `day_sheet.test.mjs:121-122` pins the
  literal `$("printBtn").onclick = () => print();`, so giving the pool sheet a print mode reddens
  that assertion — rewrite it to its PURPOSE, do not delete it.

- **RF-2 (item 2) — league manager. CONFIRMED, NOT ON THE ROADMAP, and it is two units wearing one
  bullet.** **Rounds-per-night does not exist as a concept:** `leagues_admin.js:216` sets
  `round = (maxRound.r || 0) + 1` — one round per press — and `pairWeek` (`:150-183`) adds every team
  to a `used` Set (`:157`, `:172`), so a team gets AT MOST one game per week; there is no slot loop
  and no config key. **`round` is OVERLOADED as the week number**, which is what makes this
  expensive: the board groups matches into weeks by `mt.round`, so a second round on the same night
  needs a new axis, not a bigger number. `admin-league.js:273` posts `{}` to
  `POST /api/leagues/:id/week`, and courts come from `ev.court_count || 4`
  (`leagues_admin.js:220`). Jump-to-week: **both ends missing** — no control in the toolbar
  (`admin-league.html:157-163`) and no anchor to jump to (`admin-league.js:119` emits week cards with
  no `id`). Back-to-top: no such control on any page in `web/` (0 grep hits). Collapse: no toggle and
  no `aria-expanded` on `admin-league.html:139-154`, so there is no handler to wire — but the pool
  board already ships the idiom to copy (`#pbCollapse`, `admin-pool-board.html:388`).
  **Unit A is small and client-only** (week ids + jump control + back-to-top + collapse). **Unit B is
  large** (teach the league a second round per night, which touches the pairing engine, the week
  axis, the board and the printed schedule).

- **RF-3 (item 3) — the court/schedule editor. PARTIALLY-BUILT, needs a migration, and the register
  already owns its hardest half.** What exists: `admin-schedule-editor.html` renders ONE grid of
  every round × court for the whole event (`admin-schedule-editor.js:82-116`); moves are local and
  held, fairness re-scores through `…/schedule/preview` which writes nothing (`formats.js:895-910`),
  and Save is the one writer (`…/schedule/apply`, `formats.js:917-966`) — T2-1a, shipped. What is
  missing: **no week `<select>` exists at all** (only `#sEvent`), so the week-first flow has no
  control to wire; **no league forfeit path** — the only forfeit route in the worker is
  `POST …/brackets/forfeit` (`brackets.js:1229`) and no league surface calls it; **no route removes
  or withdraws a team.** **§-1j T2-1b IS THE RECORDED VERSION OF THAT SERVER GAP and it is UNSTRUCK**
  (roadmap:1530 — "No route moves a team across events… nothing today touches `teams.event_id`, and
  that team's matches, standings, registrations and `score_token` all assume the original event"), so
  the first verdict's "all closed" was wrong. Recency ("played together last week") has no signal
  anywhere: the board payload returns no meet map. Per-week print buttons and a per-week print
  layout do not exist. **Losses for a forfeit or a removal are a SCORING decision, not just a UI
  one** — say what a forfeit writes before building the control.

- **RF-4 — OWNER DECISION 2026-08-18: NEITHER OPTION. HE WROTE OPTION C.** Asked whether all nine
  event-scoped rail tools should move into the event screen or only the five that cannot work
  standalone, his answer, verbatim: *"A - but create an option C - whre tournaments and Leagues have
  their own buttons for management that sorts which they can pick of a filtered list. This also adds
  the caveate that as the list grows, the events that past must be removed from the active management
  list. They should still be available in the events page as an option to duplicate and historical
  data, but should not force a user to scroll through every event over time."*

  **What that means for the build, in his terms.** The rail gets **TWO management entry points —
  Tournament Management and League Management** — and each opens a **filtered picker** of that type's
  events, from which the operator chooses one and lands in that event's tab frame. The nine
  event-scoped rail entries collapse behind those two. This is a better reading of his original item 4
  than either option offered: the tools stop being rail destinations without making the operator hunt
  through an undifferentiated event list.

  **THE PICKERS SHIPPED v0.173.0 (iteration 114); THE NINE-ENTRY COLLAPSE IS THE REMAINING HALF.**
  Two rail entries — `admin-manage.html#tournaments` / `#leagues`, one page, hash-scoped (the
  `admin-users.html#roles` precedent; the highlighter's hashchange listener flips between them
  without a reload) — each listing that type's ACTIVE events; every row opens the event's hub
  (`admin-manager.html?event=N`). A hash-less visit defaults to `#tournaments`. Past events sit
  behind a "Show past (N)" toggle and stay on Events & Programs (his words). `GET /api/events`'
  staff projection gained `ends_at` — the column existed, the route never returned it. Guards:
  `admin_manage.test.mjs` (7 tests, watched red first; the active/past rule is EXECUTED there,
  extracted from the shipped source, against the D1-measured trap). **COLLAPSE SHIPPED v0.184.0 (iteration 125)**
  (owner 2026-08-23: "Yes collapse it and it's admin only", and the scheduler decision resolved by
  "add the rail for scheduler to the tournament page/league page ... Do not have it on the menu"). The pickers were
  confirmed working; the EIGHT event-scoped rail entries (the earlier "nine" double-counted the shared
  Schedule Editor) were removed from `rail.partial.html` AND `admin-nav.js`'s NAV, synced into all 40
  admin pages. Eight PARENT mappings were added (hash-carrying values); `nav_highlight.test.mjs` v1.1
  extended its extractor for those and now verifies each parent against the WHOLE rail href (the
  positive control that stops the highlighter going dark on the collapsed pages). "admin only" was
  already true — these are admin-shell rail entries.

  **MEASURED iteration 124 (before building — the set and the one open decision).** The event-scoped
  entries in the "Run events" NAV group are EIGHT distinct pages: `tournament.html` (Tournament Ops),
  `admin-league.html` (League Manager), `admin-schedule-editor.html` (Schedule Editor),
  `admin-brackets.html` (Brackets), `admin-divisions.html` (Divisions), `admin-pool-board.html`
  (Pool Board), `admin-kotc.html` (Court Board), `admin-score-links.html` (Scoring Links). The
  "nine" in the earlier count double-counts `admin-schedule-editor.html`, which appears under BOTH
  the `tournaments` and `leagues` MANAGE_MODULES lists (`admin-nav.js:145-146`). **THE OPEN DECISION — RESOLVED (owner 2026-08-23):**
  "add the rail for scheduler to the tournament page/league page for custom builds when needed to edit.
  Do not have it on the menu." So Schedule Editor is COLLAPSED (not left railed), reachable from BOTH
  the #tournaments and #leagues pickers through the hub's Schedule editor tab; its PARENT-highlight
  defaults to `#tournaments`. Shipped v0.184.0 exactly as recommended: the six tournament-only pages
  and Court Board → `admin-manage.html#tournaments`, `admin-league.html` → `#leagues`,
  Schedule Editor → `#tournaments`.

  **RF-4b — THE ACTIVE/PAST SPLIT IS PART OF THE SAME ASK AND IT IS THE HALF WITH A DATA QUESTION.**
  The picker must show only events still being managed; past ones must fall off it, while staying on
  the Events page for duplication and history. **MEASURE BEFORE CHOOSING THE RULE — both candidate
  columns exist and they can disagree.** `events` carries `starts_at`, `ends_at` and
  `status TEXT CHECK (status IN ('draft','published','in_progress','completed','cancelled'))`
  (foundation migration / `journey-schema.sql`). A status-based filter is the obvious choice and is
  the likely trap: **if nothing in the product ever sets `status='completed'`, every old event stays
  "active" forever — which is exactly the complaint he is reporting.** So read live D1 first: how many
  events have `ends_at` in the past while `status` is still `published` or `in_progress`, and does
  anything anywhere write `completed`? If D1 cannot be read, say the rule is unmeasured rather than
  picking one. The safe shape is almost certainly date-derived with an explicit override, never a
  status the operator has to remember to set.

  **MEASURED 2026-08-21 (iteration 114) AND SHIPPED v0.173.0 — the trap was real and in the
  majority:** 4 of 7 live events carried `ends_at` in the past while `status` was still
  `published`/`in_progress`; the only `completed` row was sandbox seed (event 90001); every
  `completed` reference in `worker/src` is a reader or the manual dropdown's validation list —
  nothing writes it. The shipped rule: **past = ended by date OR `completed`/`cancelled`; NULL
  `ends_at` = not ended** (a draft under construction stays manageable). The "Show past" toggle
  is the explicit override — no new column, nothing for the operator to remember.

  **AND THE WRITERS EXIST NOW (v0.174.0, D-53):** iteration 115 measured one level up — every
  non-NULL `ends_at` was sandbox seed because nothing on the owner's path could write one (the
  modal never asked, `patchEvent` dropped it, `createEvent` never named it, `EVENT_FIELDS`
  stripped it). The modal and the recurring form collect ends now; recurring instances derive
  each night's end from its OWN date. The rule and its data pipeline are both real.

  **Still true from the measurement below:** the rail carries **45 `.nav-item` entries**, a removed
  page needs a PARENT entry so `nav_highlight` keeps working, `sync-rail.mjs --write` re-syncs the
  partial but does NOT check NAV parity, and §-1d N-4/N-5 are the unstruck rows this closes.

- **RF-4 (item 4) — the left-side tiles. PARTIALLY-BUILT, AND ALREADY ON THE ROADMAP, UNSTRUCK:
  §-1d N-4 ("Tournaments Manager — one rail item, functions as in-screen tabs") and §-1d N-5 (the
  menu brevity pass).** The rail lives at `web/assets/rail.partial.html` (there is no `web/partials/`)
  and carries **45 `.nav-item` entries**. The ~9 the event hub genuinely duplicates: Waitlists,
  Check-in, Tournament Ops, League Manager, Schedule Editor, Brackets, Divisions, Pool Board,
  Scoring Links. **Side finding, failure class 1, and NEW:** `web/admin-consent.html` (Media consent)
  has **zero links from anywhere in `web/`** — the only hits are a parent-highlight mapping
  (`admin-nav.js:459`) and the build registry marking it "live" (`build-status.js:91`). It is in
  neither `rail.partial.html` nor the NAV array. Recorded as D-47.

- **RF-5 (item 5) — remove a pool. THE CAPABILITY ALREADY EXISTS AND IT IS HIS OWN 2026-08-03 SPEC;
  what is missing is the AFFORDANCE AND THE WORDING.** `admin-pool-board.js:7` quotes him verbatim:
  *"if i drag to a square or block with + it will add a pool. and if it is empty, itll auto delete"*,
  restated as design law at `:15-17`. That is why `newPool` (`:316-324`) has no counterpart — **the
  counterpart is Save**: `save()` drops empty zones at `:535-536`, and server-side
  `divisions.js:602-626` hard-DELETEs a pool that never held a game (clearing `teams.pool_id` first
  to respect migration 0039's FK) and soft-deletes one that matches still reference. **So the unit is
  a delete affordance in `.pb-pool-head` that empties the pool for you, plus a line on the board
  saying an empty pool disappears on Save — not a new capability.** §-0 row 5 (K-1 tier 2) and
  handoff:28 both already record "`teams.pool_id` is cleared **on pool delete**". His second
  sentence — pools → points → matchups → bracket — is the §-1p WF-2 flow question and is NOT closed;
  keep it with RF-1's bracket work rather than here.
  **→ SHIPPED v0.178.0 (iteration 119), exactly the affordance-plus-wording unit.** Every pool head
  carries a real ✕ button (36px, keyboard-reachable, in the head — not a drop zone); pressing it
  sends the teams back to the WORKSPACE (rule 1, never nowhere), removes the zone locally and marks
  dirty — Save then omits the pool and the server does what his spec always said. The board says
  the law under the keyboard tip ("an empty pool disappears when you save"); the button's
  aria-label says the same. Guards: pool_board.test.mjs v1.1 (+4, red first — the orphan pin is the
  center: a removed pool must never silently orphan its teams). The WF-2 flow sentence stays open
  with RF-1's bracket work, as recorded above.

- ~~**RF-6 (item 6) — "court board is not working."**~~ **FIXED AND LIVE, v0.170.0 (iteration 110).** Both 200 paths of `/move` now spread the roster; the contract guard derives the client's render-from-response routes and checks each handler region, so a new one cannot ship the same hole. *(Original measurement, kept — TRUE-BUT-NARROWER: the chain resolved end to end and the defect was in a RESPONSE CONTRACT.)* The screen is `admin-kotc.html` (title "Court board",
  `:22`, `:255`) + `admin-kotc.js`; every id it touches exists, listeners wire at `:370-378`, and all
  five routes exist and are dispatched (`kotcplay.js:494`, `:788`, `:405`, `:539`, `:692`). **The
  break: `kotcplay.js:662` (the `/move` return) omits a `roster` key, and `admin-kotc.js:177` renders
  the withdrawn list from `data.roster` — so `:185` hides `#kbDoneWrap` after every drag.** A director
  drags once and the withdrawn panel vanishes, which reads exactly as "not working". **Fix is one
  line in each of the two `/move` returns** (`:571`, `:662`) — `...(await roster(env, ctx.orgId,
  sessionId))`, the shape `/withdraw` already proves. Separately, the seats have no pointer-events
  drag path, so the board is mouse-only on the tablet it is used on. **Not on the roadmap as a broken
  screen** (D-24 was a different KOTC defect, CLOSED v0.137.0).

- **RF-7 (item 7) — "the calendar boxes are STILL not correct." TRUE-BUT-NARROWER, and "still" is
  accurate for the MEMBER calendar while REFUTED for the admin one.** There are exactly two
  month-grid renderers and they share one stylesheet. `admin-events.html`'s Calendar tab **has
  WF-1's fix and is guarded**: `CAL_DAY_CAP = 3` (`admin-events.js:72`), a "+N more" opener
  (`:75-101`), the 42-cell loop (`:112-118`), `grid-template-rows: auto repeat(6, 1fr)`
  (`admin.css:81-83`), pinned by `events_calendar.test.mjs`. **The member calendar
  (`web/assets/schedule.js`) never got it** — no cap, no "+N more", and its month pager (`:281-282`)
  calls `render()` but never `load()`, while the fetch window is pinned at `:203-205` (today-7/+180)
  and enforced server-side at `worker/src/schedule.js:141-147`. **So a member paging past the window
  gets empty boxes because of the REQUEST, not the schedule.** Roadmap: §-1p WF-1(a), whose fix
  landed on one of the two readers. The unit is to promote the cap into the member calendar as ONE
  judgement with two readers, and to make the pager refetch from `calCursor`.
  **→ SHIPPED v0.176.0 (iteration 117), exactly as written.** `BT_CAL` (DAY_CAP + split) lives in
  config.js — the one script both shells load (BT_SIGNUP_LINK's/BT_THEME's precedent) — and BOTH
  calendars render through it; the admin's local `CAL_DAY_CAP` literal is deleted deliberately.
  Member "+N more" expands the day IN PLACE (no modal on the member side; `aria-expanded`
  maintained); stale config degrades to the uncapped cell for its ~10-minute window, never a dead
  calendar. The pager and the List/Month switch REFETCH; the month window derives from
  `calCursor`'s 42 visible cells (the server honours any from/to — re-measured; no server change).
  Guards: events_calendar.test.mjs v2.0 — extracts BT_CAL from config.js's shipped bytes and
  EXECUTES it through the admin cell builder; the one-judgement property itself is enforced (a
  cap literal reappearing in either reader fails); member pins + NC-C1/NC-C2. 7 reds watched.

- **RF-8 (item 8) — "admin login still bare and wrong." TRUE-BUT-NARROWER, and it corrects a claim
  THIS LOOP has been repeating.** **A sign-in page DOES exist** — `web/index.html` +
  `renderLogin()` (`app.js:148-224`) renders an org lockup, `<h1>Sign in</h1>`, a Member/Manager
  tablist, an email field and "Send sign-in link". The ledger's flat *"there is NO login page"* is
  REFUTED; the roadmap's actual words are "no SECOND login page" (§-1f F-1), which is accurate.
  What is genuinely wrong: **(a) bare CONDITIONALLY** — the brand lockup is built only when
  `loginOrgHint()` returns a value (`app.js:157-159`), so the default arrival is unbranded;
  **(b) FAILURE CLASS 1 — `admin-nav.js:499-500` bounces an expired staff session to
  `index.html?expired=1&from=<page>`, and NOTHING in `web/` reads either param** (`app.js` reads only
  `token` and `org`), so a timed-out director lands on a bare sign-in card with no reason and no way
  back. Recorded as D-48. **(c)** the Member/Manager tabs are cosmetic. **The Face-ID sentence is NOT
  a defect — that sub-claim was REFUTED:** `passkey.js:96-120` injects a real "Sign in with Face ID /
  fingerprint" button after `#sendLink` with a MutationObserver, so the hint at `app.js:189` points
  at a control that exists whenever the browser supports passkeys.
  **→ (b) SHIPPED v0.177.0 (iteration 118): D-48 struck** — the expired sentence, the from-carry
  through the emailed link, the return redirect (passkey path covered at boot), one validator per
  side with hostile shapes dropped. **(c) was closed earlier by v0.171.0's tablist removal.**
  **REMAINING in RF-8: (a) only** — the conditionally-bare brand lockup (`loginOrgHint()` gates it). **Also found: §-1f F-3
  (login-page branding) is a STALE-OPEN ROW** — it reads "REAL AND UNBUILT" at roadmap:884 while
  handoff:74 records it shipped in v0.106.0. Struck in this edit.

- ~~**RF-9 (item 9) — "color toggle still not working fully."**~~ **FIXED AND LIVE, v0.170.0 (iteration 110)** — and the guard that should have caught it was decorative: it could not match the const-bound spelling every shell in this repo uses, so its exemption list exempted files it could never catch. Theme is now a property with no exemption list, measured across all 62 asset scripts. *(Original measurement, kept — CONFIRMED, small, and the axis is ONE PAGE.)* Every admin page (38 + `tournament.html`) delegates to `BT_THEME.toggleMode()`
  (`admin-nav.js:845-850`) and so do all 17 member pages (`site-nav.js:165-172`). **`web/index.html`
  does not:** `app.js:39-44` sets only `dataset.theme` and never touches `data-template`, which is
  applied at `index.html:16` and supplies every colour token because `tokens.css:278-368` follows
  `tokens.css:12-132` at equal specificity. **So on the sign-in/dashboard page the toggle is a
  visible no-op that also desynchronises the stored theme keys.** Fix: delete `app.js`'s private
  `setTheme` and delegate to `BT_THEME.toggleMode()`, then drop `app.js` from
  `header_shell.test.mjs:338`'s `allowed` set so the guard finally covers the last page. Roadmap:
  §-1j T2-15 claims completion (SHIPPED v0.160.0) — TRUE for 55 of 56 pages, and this is the 56th.

- **RF-10 (item 10) — subfinder and the member league view. CONFIRMED, NOT ON THE ROADMAP.** The Sub

  and the public court+opponent payload that would answer it exists (`live.js:64`, `on_now` built at
  `:137-147`) with exactly ONE caller — the standalone board (`live.js:258-260`). **Two units:** a
  Subs rail item owning a Sub-finder page, and a member-scoped "your league tonight" read. Note the
  member already meets subs in three places (leagues page, `home.html`'s availability toggle,
  `lfg.html`), so the first unit needs a decision about which fold in.
  **→ HALF 2 SHIPPED v0.179.0 (iteration 120): "your league tonight."** A signed-in member with a
  team in a live league sees a banner above the Leagues list — tonight's court and opponent

  the decoration rule (un-awaited, fail-soft to nothing) is pinned in `league_tonight.test.mjs`.
  **HALF 1 SHIPPED v0.180.0 (iteration 121) — his word arrived 2026-08-22:** "make it 1 button on
  the menu called Sub-Finder … additional links to that menu on the league page and tournament page
  and community page as well, but it should lead to that module, so buttons along the top." The Sub
  finder MOVED off leagues.html to its own module — `web/subs.html` + `web/assets/subs.js` — reached
  by ONE rail button ("Sub-Finder", signed-in Play group) and by a top button on leagues.html and
  lfg.html (Community Play). ONE home now: leagues.js is the league list again, no `/api/subs/*`.
  `home.html`'s availability toggle was left as-is (his word named league/tournament/community, not

- **RF-11 (item 11) — rename Schedule → Event Schedule, plus achievements/history.
  PARTIALLY-BUILT.** The label is four literals: `site-nav.js:212`, `schedule.html:8` + `:73`,
  `schedule.js:212` (which overwrites the `<h1>` at runtime), and the index card at `app.js:255`.
  **Its live hook is §-1c D-19**, whose trigger column already reads "the next release that renames
  rail items" — this ask IS that release. **Achievements/history: a cross-event attendance route
  EXISTS and has ZERO callers** — `/api/profile/attendance` (`checkin.js:148` → `myAttendance:330-343`,
  LIMIT 50), and `grep -rn "profile/attendance" web/` returns 0, so per the call-site rule it is a
  definition, not a feature. What DOES render is a different dataset: the results résumé
  (`/api/profile/resume`, `profiles.js:91`) at `home.js:318` and `profile.js:410`. **Third broken
  link, NEW:** `index.html`'s "Tournaments" card points at `schedule.html?type=tournament`
  (`app.js:256`) and `schedule.js` never reads a `type` param (`typeFilter = ""` at `:40`, set only
  by a tab click at `:187`) — the card silently lands on the unfiltered list. Recorded as D-49.

- **RF-13 (owner req 2026-08-23) — a member can see the tournament view from an event. VIEW-HALF
  SHIPPED v0.181.0 (iteration 122).** His words: "is there a view for members to see the tournament
  display or tournament info from the member screen, they will not edit but receive info" and
  "members from the event page can click an event and then see a view for members that have the
  views of pools, and bracket and are able to enter scores from their screen if needed."
  **MEASURED: the view already existed.** `live.html?event=N` (`/api/live/events/:id`, team names
  only, public — loads site-nav.js) renders divisions→pools (`live.js:263-272`), brackets (`:278`)
  and standings, and degrades gracefully for a not-started event. The gap was the PATH — the
  RF-6/RF-9 class: `schedule.js` rendered a started event with NO action and `leagues.js` showed a
  DEAD "In progress"/"Closed" label. **Wired the way in (no new page/route):** a started
  tournament/league card links to `live.html?event=N` ("Pools & bracket" / "Standings & scores"),
  gated to those types + started status; upcoming still shows Register. `member_event_view.test.mjs`
  (7, real-source NCs). **SCORE-ENTRY HALF NOT BUILT — a real decision:** members have no self-serve
  scoring-link path today (`score.html` links are token-based and staff-distributed; no member page
  exposes one). How a member "enters scores from their screen" — surface their team's scoring link
  on the view, or a new authed in-view entry — HAD needed the owner's word.
  **OWNER'S WORD ARRIVED 2026-08-23, verbatim (the next unit).** His words: *"When teams visit the
  event page, they should see their own team and performance - please have the code listed there as
  well - or if they click their team name - there needs to be a view where they see THEIR opponents or
  individual play schedule and have the score code or links there to press. Does not need to be a QR
  code. Alternatively, if they login to see the schedule - their team is highlighted or filtred for
  them to see and then the game will have a link to the score entry."* So the score-entry half becomes
  a TEAM-SCOPED view: a team sees THEIR team + performance with the score code listed, or clicks their
  team name to a view of THEIR opponents / individual play schedule carrying the score code or a link
  to press (no QR needed); the signed-in alternative is the schedule highlighting/filtering to their
  team with each game linking to score entry. **MEASURE FIRST (§C):** what a "score code" is in the
  live schema/routes (per-team or per-game code, or is the `score.html` token the only key?), whether
  `/api/live/events/:id` already returns a team's own games, and how a signed-in member's team is
  identified (`/api/profile/teams`, matched by NAME per RF-10) — before choosing between "surface the
  existing code/link on the view" and "a new authed in-view entry." Its own iteration: routes + screen
  ship together (no self-serve scoring path exists yet).
  **a11y refinements SHIPPED v0.182.0–v0.183.0 (iterations 123–124, two Gemini rounds):** the links
  carry an event-name `aria-label` (WCAG 2.4.4), the prefix now CONDITIONAL so a nameless event has
  no dangling " — " (v0.183.0), and `leagues.js` checks `completed` EXPLICITLY so a completed league
  with no parseable date still links its standings. Gemini findings measured and NOT changed: the
  cancelled-leak REFUTED (public feed excludes cancelled/draft server-side), double-quote escaping
  refuted (both `esc()` map `"`→`&quot;`), an isNaN guard declined (invalid date already yields no link).

- **RF-12 — OWNER DECISION 2026-08-18, AND HE RAISED A SECURITY REQUIREMENT THAT OUTRANKS THE REST.**
  Asked whether a signed-in member keeps any route back to the public card grid, his answer, verbatim:
  *"B - but the issue is there are options for the admin panel on that page or lead to the admin page.
  This is not allowable for security reason. Agree with the explore but the member page should be
  limited to the member features. There should be no admin access from this screen. Yes fix the
  notifications thing to make sense with the current build. Realign that menu from most useful to least
  useful. Inbox should be 2 or 3, while Home at #1, then notifications (similar to logical for other
  apps). Group things together that make sense."*

  **Four things, and they are separable units.**

  **(1) NO ADMIN AFFORDANCE ON MEMBER SURFACES — SHIPPED v0.171.0 (iteration 112).** Enumerated
  first, removed second. The full list, measured across all 22 member-facing pages (everything not

  member keeps one route to the public card grid — with (1) applying to that screen too. The rail's

  the current build" was read as: point the anchor at what exists. home.html's feed box — the

- ~~**RF-12 (item 12) — Home vs Dashboard.**~~ **CLOSED v0.172.0 with (4): D-19 struck** — home.html
  is Home, index.html's one signed-in route is Explore, and no two rail items may share a name.
  Original measurement, kept: CONFIRMED, small, AND ALREADY RECORDED AS §-1c D-19
  (~~STILL OPEN~~): "Two landing pages, both in the member rail, with names that do not distinguish
  them… Not chased because the fix is a decision, not an edit." The member menu is 13 items in two

  **no element with that id exists anywhere in `web/`**, with no `hashchange` handler in `home.js` —
  so it silently lands on the top of My Dashboard. Recorded as D-50.

**THE SHAPE OF THIS BRIEF, and it is worth reading before picking an order.** Four of the twelve are
small and certain — RF-6 (one line in each of two returns), RF-9 (delegate one toggle), RF-12(a)
(role-gate one rail item) and RF-1(f)'s confirm-and-redraw — and each of them fixes something that
currently reads to him as broken. Two are large engine work (RF-2's second round per night, RF-3's
week editor). Two are decisions he owes rather than code (RF-4's rail scope, RF-12's landing page).
And **two asks were already built and invisible** (RF-5's pool removal, RF-11's attendance route),
which is the pattern this project keeps paying for: a capability with no affordance is indistinguishable
from a missing feature, and the screen is the defect.

## §-1q. THE GRANT & APPROVAL MECHANISM — SG-3 + PM-4 + Q1 designed ONCE (added v1.59, iteration 85 — **AWAITING THE OWNER'S READ**)

**What this is, in plain words.** Three queued asks are the same machine seen from three sides:
Cathy needs *some* admin screens but not all of them (SG-3, your words: *"a specific admin access …
as a role … perhaps toggles for that account role"*); a partner should eventually manage their own
page (PM-4); and a free-typed partner name must wait for an approval before it counts (Q1, your
words: *"it must be approved by Super Admin or Cathy can be allowed to type any she wishes with
approval toggle"*). One mechanism serves all three: **a limited account that holds explicit grants,
and a pending-approval row that an admin decides.** Nothing in this section is built; it is written
for you to approve, amend, or defer. **No authorization code ships until you have read it.**

### The measurements this stands on (all taken 2026-08-14, iteration 85)

- **180 `requireStaff` call sites across 39 of 51 worker modules** (corpus asserted: all 51 files
  scanned; §-1e S-2's "~190" was the same fact at v0.103.0). `requireAdmin` has exactly 4 call
  sites, all in `admin.js` (listUsers / addUser / setRole / revokeRole). **Every other admin
  surface admits any staff of the org.** So "Cathy as staff with a trimmed menu" would be a view
  of less and access to everything — including S-2a's rescue-link, which in keyless sandbox mode
  returns a working sign-in link for ANY account to the caller. **Creating her account is
  therefore GATED ON this mechanism: the grant system must ship before or with user #2.**
- **Live D1: ONE user account** (the owner), holding admin on 3 orgs; **zero staff rows have ever
  existed** (`user_org_roles`: 3 rows, 1 holder). Day one of this mechanism changes nothing for
  anyone — S-2's "loaded gun, not a fire" stays unloaded until the first limited account exists.
- **`isStaff` (index.js:343) recognises only `admin` and `staff`.** A NEW role value is invisible
  to every existing gate — it fails all 180 of them by construction. **Deny-by-default is free.**
- **Every module receives `requireStaff` by INJECTION** (`wireXxx(helpers)` — e.g.
  tournaments.js:392, registrations.js:128), wired once in index.js. The module axis can therefore
  be bound at the ONE mounting site without touching a single call site — the shape changes, not
  the arity, and no caller can forget the axis (the D-29/BT_SIGNUP lesson, applied to gates).
- **`BT_MODULES` (admin-nav.js:116) is the vocabulary: 14 keys** — and it has **no `events` key**;
  the events/hub surfaces (admin-events, admin-event, admin-manager) sit outside the registry as
  always-on core. Cathy's "event manager" grant needs an `events` key ADDED to the registry (one
  entry; P-1's org-level toggles keep working — unknown keys fail open to a fuller menu, measured
  at `pagesToHide`). Note honestly: P-1's org toggles are a VIEW filter; **this mechanism is the
  enforcement half P-1 deliberately did not build.**

### The design

**1. A third role: `host`** (in `user_org_roles`, beside admin/staff — the owner's "as a role",
taken literally). A host passes NO existing gate (measured above). Grants open doors one module at
a time:

**2. `user_module_grants`** — additive migration: `(id, org_id, user_id, module_key TEXT,
granted_by, created_at, deleted_at)`. Keys are BT_MODULES' keys plus `events`. The worker gets a
`MODULE_KEYS` constant pinned byte-equal to admin-nav.js's list by a guard — the deliberate-copy
pattern ACTIVE_REG already uses across events_admin/waitlists, because web and worker cannot
import each other.

**3. Gates bound at the wire.** index.js mounts each routes-module with a module-bound gate:
`wireTournaments({...h, requireStaff: staffGateFor("tournaments")})`. The bound gate: admin →
pass; staff → pass (unchanged — nobody holds it, and it stays the unscoped tier); **host → pass
only with a live grant row for THIS org and THIS key, else 403 with its own sentence** ("Your
account doesn't include <module> for <org>."). Modules outside any key (orgs, security, sandbox,
users, admin) keep the UNBOUND gate, which refuses hosts always — **S-2a's rescue-link is closed
to hosts by construction**, while staying queued for the staff tier. Revoking a grant only ever
narrows: a host with zero grants can reach nothing gated, and never silently widens into staff
(the widen-on-revoke failure is structurally impossible because host ≠ staff).

**4. The menu follows the grants.** `/api/me` carries the host's granted keys; admin-nav reuses
P-1's exact `pagesToHide` machinery per-account. The menu is the VIEW half; the gates above are
the half that cannot be bypassed.

**5. The approval workflow (Q1) — the same family.** Additive table `approvals`: `(id, org_id,
kind TEXT, payload_json, submitted_by, status 'pending'|'approved'|'rejected', decided_by,
decided_at, note, created_at, deleted_at)`. First kind: `partner_name` (the free-typed partner
from your Q1 answer — the row an admin approves before the name counts). **The per-account
"trusted, no approval needed" toggle is a grant row in the SAME table as module grants** (reserved
key `trust.partner_name`), per your instruction that these are one family. Decisions are audited;
pending items surface in-app to admins (keyless-honest — no email promises). **PM-4 (partner
self-service) then = a host account in the partner's org + an `events`-class grant + approvals on
what they submit** — no new mechanism, exactly as §-1m predicted ("probably the SAME mechanism").

**Stated readings (per your own words, said back as instructed):** "Super Admin" is read as
`admin` — the platform's top tier today; say so if you meant a fourth tier. Grants are per-ORG
(Cathy's grants in Boomtown grant nothing elsewhere — cross_org_isolation extends to pin it).
Contact visibility inside granted modules is FULL (emails and phones — your 23:09 answer, already
settled); the members/users directory is core, so hosts never see it — participants reach her
through the granted screens (the hub's Registrations tab, the CSV, the message card).

### Build units (each its own iteration, guards first)

- **SG-3a — the mechanism:** migration 0050 (`user_module_grants`) — **plus the program's ONE
  non-additive step, stated here rather than discovered later: `user_org_roles.role` carries a
  day-one CHECK (`'admin','staff','member'` — foundation migration line 12, measured), so
  admitting `host` means a table REBUILD (SQLite cannot alter a CHECK). Three live rows are
  affected; per the standing rules the exact SQL and row count are shown to you before it runs.**
  (The alternative that lost: modelling hosts as `member` + grant rows is fully additive but
  erases the visible "limited admin" marker and makes a grant row the only discriminator —
  semantics worth three rebuilt rows.) Then MODULE_KEYS + the byte-equal guard, `staffGateFor` +
  wire bindings, `authorization_matrix` gains the host rows (host+grant passes its module; host
  without 403 with the sentence; host on core 403; admin unchanged; the admin-only-may-grow
  ratchet keeps), `cross_org_isolation` gains the per-org pin.
- **SG-3b — the screens:** grant toggles on the users screen (per-account, BT_MODULES labels),
  `/api/me` keys, per-account menu filter, Cathy's onboarding walk (addUser with role host +
  grants; sandbox sign-in until go-live).
- **SG-3c — approvals:** the table, `partner_name` kind, the pending queue surface, the trusted
  bypass, audit rows. PM-4 unblocks after this.

### ✅ BOTH QUESTIONS ANSWERED BY THE OWNER, 2026-08-15 (recorded verbatim, iteration 86)

1. ~~Cathy's module set: events + tournaments now, and leagues too, or add leagues later?~~
   **Owner:** *"Cathy module is fine. Leagues later can be built in the bt league module."* →
   **SETTLED: events + tournaments at creation; leagues added later** (one grant row when he
   decides — the mechanism makes it a click, not a build).
2. ~~When a free-typed partner name is pending, is the EVENT visible with a placeholder, or held
   back entirely until the name is approved?~~ **Owner:** *"Plese put Pending invitiation note
   and add the partner and mark it as partner needs to register."* → **SETTLED, and richer than
   either offered option: the event stays VISIBLE carrying a "Pending invitation" note; the
   partner row is CREATED IMMEDIATELY and marked "partner needs to register."** Two consequences
   for SG-3c, read at build time: (a) the approval's pending state is a visible marker on the
   surface, not a gate on the event; (b) "needs to register" names the RESOLUTION — the partner
   claiming their account (PM-4's registration) or an admin approving completes the row. The
   free-typed name still enters the approvals queue per his Q1 answer; what approval gates is
   the partner's standing, never the event's visibility.

**With both answered and no amendment to the design above, SG-3a is authorised — EXCEPT its one
non-additive step, which has its own standing gate: the `user_org_roles` role-CHECK rebuild
(3 live rows) runs only after the exact SQL has been shown and the owner has said go. The SQL
is in the session reply's closing block and reproduced here for the record:**

```sql
-- widen the day-one CHECK to admit 'host' (SQLite cannot ALTER a CHECK; rebuild + copy):
CREATE TABLE user_org_roles_new (
  user_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
  org_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES orgs(id),
  role TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('admin','staff','member','host')),
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
  updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
  deleted_at TEXT,
  PRIMARY KEY (user_id, org_id)
);
INSERT INTO user_org_roles_new SELECT * FROM user_org_roles;  -- 3 rows, the owner's own admin roles
DROP TABLE user_org_roles;
ALTER TABLE user_org_roles_new RENAME TO user_org_roles;
```

## §1b. OWNER-SPECIFIED MODULE PROGRAM (his words, 2026-08-16 — recorded verbatim, iteration 100)

He was shown five *candidate* modules and answered each. **These are now scope, not proposals.**
None is built; each needs its own measurement pass before a line is written — the candidates were
offered as UNVERIFIED and his "yes" authorises the work, not the premise.

**FIRST, THE FRAMING HE CORRECTED, because it changes what belongs here:** *"Please stop calling
this a club platform. This is our total business operations, club is one aspect of our business but
we do much more."* Modules are scoped to the BUSINESS, not to a club. A proposal narrowed to club
use is narrower than he asked for.

| # | Module | His words | Shape to measure first |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OM-1** | **Staff scheduling** → then **coach assignment** | *"staff scheduling and then add coaches assigment as an option to members"* | Two steps, ordered. Staff Pay exists; whether anything ASSIGNS a person to a session is unmeasured. Step 2 makes coach a thing a MEMBER can be assigned as — so it touches the member record, not just a staff table. |
| **OM-2** | **Universal attendance history** | *"make attendance history universal over all events and programs and keep a log for admin that is searchable and able to create tables and for the members to see their own history"* | **Universal** is the load-bearing word: every event AND program type, not only what check-in covers today. Three surfaces: an admin log that is SEARCHABLE and can BUILD TABLES (a report builder, not a list), and a member-facing own-history view. Check-in today is per-event; there is no cross-event record. |
| **OM-3** | **Achievements driven by participation** | *"This also should help with the achievement module where goals can be set based on their participation"* | **OM-2 is its data source — build order is OM-2 then OM-3.** M17 (achievements & public standings) already sits in §1 with a spec approved; check M17 against this before treating it as new. |
| **OM-4** | **Team rosters take coaches** | *"Team rosters can add coaches - input via text"* | **Free-typed text, not a picker** — the same input shape as the §-1q partner name. Whether it should also enter the approvals queue is NOT settled; ask when OM-4 is picked up. |
| **OM-5** | **Membership plans, Gymdesk-class** | *"membership plans need to be similar to gymdesk and able to modify and edit payments and charges"* | The operative half is **modify and edit payments and charges on an EXISTING membership** — today's plans are create-and-price. Gymdesk is the named reference; measure what the current plan/charge model can already express before proposing a rebuild. Overlaps R-04 (installments) in §1 — check for duplication rather than building both. |
| ~~M12C~~ | ~~Member-side court booking~~ | *"We will hold off on this for now."* | **DEFERRED by owner, 2026-08-16.** Do not raise it again unless he does. |

**Standing caution for all of the above:** three of the five event types the schema allows —
`training`, `event`, `court_rental` — have NEVER been published (handoff §1, live D1 2026-08-13).
OM-2 says "universal over all events and programs". **Understand why those three types are empty
before building anything that iterates over all of them** — an attendance log spanning types nobody
uses is a report with three permanently blank sections.

   into LFG v0.45.0 (any member posts · casual is free-form · 12h bail window · poster reports the
   no-show · in-app messaging only · 18+ · team shell forms immediately). [INTERPRETATION] the doc
   was never re-versioned after the build. Handoff §5's "LFG deep behavior gated on requirements §4"
   therefore looks like a stale blocker — **owner to confirm** it can be struck.
4. **Handoff §6 cites "requirements §15"**, which does not exist — that document ends at §4. The
   intended reference is almost certainly requirements **§3** ("requirement specs for existing
   modules, benchmarked against LeagueApps / TeamSnap / SportsEngine, then owner interview").

## §7. Suggested sequence
1. **build-status.js v1.1** (§6.1) — small, tester-facing, currently wrong. Pair it with the README
   roadmap pointer (§6.2).
2. **SPA shell** (§3.1) — closes uiux-review, the last structural UI debt.
3. **E2E harness** (§3.2) — before more feature surface, not after.
4. **M22 membership field registry** (§1) — owner-requested, self-contained, unblocks richer LFG
   matching criteria.

6. **M-TF** when the Fable gate lifts, then **M17** behind it.
